# Ms-137

<details>
<summary>775 published remarks, VB, LW I</summary>

</details>

### <span class="fac">[1r\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1r.webp)</span>

## Volume ‘R’

### <span class="fac">[1a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1a.webp)</span>

02.02.1948

One doesn’t say “It probably is the case; but it is actually different.” Or: “I assume he will come tomorrow; but he will in fact not come.”

### <span class="fac">[1a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1a.webp)</span>

In the language-game of reporting, the reporter enters into it, whether he is mentioned in it or not. Now, one does speak of a _sense_ of the sentence (the report), & does the reporter enter into _this_, even if he is not thought of in the sentence? – If the report is “The enemy is approaching,” then it refers to something that can be depicted in a picture, in which the reporter certainly does not appear. – Which picture would then correspond to the report “I believe that the enemy …”? Must the reporter be visible in it? – I mean: it doesn’t depend on the picture, but on its use.

### <span class="fac">[1a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1a.webp)</span>

But can one not imagine a use in which the reporter does not play a role? If, for example, a voice sounded from heaven. – And what if this voice now said: “I believe that …”?

### <span class="fac">[1a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1a.webp),[1b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1b.webp)</span>

03.02.1948

Let us imagine that the announcements of train arrivals at our stations are made by a machine that receives and processes the reports from outside. – Would it make sense if it announced “I believe that the train …”? One could interpret it as a sign of uncertainty about the situation. But if it announced “The train … will now arrive; I don’t believe it” – we could not make anything of it. If a human announcer said this, one could interpret it _like_ this: “According to the reports, the train will arrive at … , but I believe (perhaps without reason) that it will not happen.” The announcer must therefore announce the one thing as a matter of course, but unofficially says that he has a feeling that it will not happen.

### <span class="fac">[1b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1b.webp)</span>

That is to say, one _could_ use the assertive sentence “p, & I believe that ~p” in a language-game, but one does not. – Because even if the announcer said this, we would not understand him without further ado; we could only find an interpretation for his words.

### <span class="fac">[1b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1b.webp)</span>

One does not say “This will probably happen, but not in reality,” although the sense of it would be easy to guess.

### <span class="fac">[1b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/1b.webp),[2a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2a.webp)</span>

What I want to say is very difficult for me to express: _This_ is our concept of ‘believing’ – _thus_ we continue the line from the assumption to the assertion. Now we try a different continuation, we change the line of the concept & now say “That is no longer the same!” That is to say: the word “believe” no longer fits for _that_ part of the line that we left unchanged. If I say to myself: “Let’s assume, I believe that … , but it is not the case” – then I ask myself: “What am I actually assuming if I assume that _I believe_ this and that?” There _seems_ to be a possible answer, by _imagining_ what it is like to believe. (Of course, it’s all just a misunderstanding.) So I direct my gaze in my imagination inward to discover the belief in the state of affairs. – Now I ask: Why can I not assert this state of mind of belief, which I _assume_ in this case, as being present? even if this assertion does not quite correspond to our “I believe …”. – No sooner do I want – in this new sense – to assert that I believe … although it is not the case, than this assertion loses all sense. And I feel that I cannot assert what I previously assumed as being present. When I spoke the assumption, it was certainly quite understandable to me; I could casually think of a use for it, knew that I would not be embarrassed when using it, but that was all. I did not have a clear picture of the difference in the application of “Let’s assume it’s raining” & “Let’s assume I believe that …”.

### <span class="fac">[2a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2a.webp),[2b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2b.webp)</span>

The meaninglessness of the assertion, where the assumption had meaning, surprised me, because I did not see its grammar in the assumption, but only heard the familiar wording and knew that it would be used.

### <span class="fac">[2b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2b.webp)</span>

The line is already different in the _assumption_ than you think.

### <span class="fac">[2b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2b.webp)</span>

I would like to say: In the words “Let’s assume I believe this,” you already presuppose the whole grammar of the word “believe.” You are not assuming something that is, as it were, clearly given to you through an image, so that you can then attach something other than the usual assertion to this assumption. You _wouldn’t even_ know what you are assuming here if you were not already familiar with the use of “believe.” –

### <span class="fac">[2b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2b.webp)</span>

It is the invisible application that shows its face here. We are not conscious of the particular _technique_; it flows, as it were, underground, without us noticing it; & we only become suddenly aware of it where it openly contradicts our false idea. Where we realize that a sentence may have no sense. We do not know what to do with it, which was not obvious.

### <span class="fac">[2b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/2b.webp),[3a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3a.webp)</span>

Yes, if I look out the window, I see that it is raining; if I look inward, I see that I don’t believe it. – Do I really see in myself whether I believe something? Well, if it is so, then I can also make the paradoxical statement. Then one part can be understood as a report about the weather, the other about a strange state of my mind. But this is not obvious according to the normal technique of the use of those words.

### <span class="fac">[3a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3a.webp)</span>

Compare: “I advise you that you … , but the advice is bad.” – as opposed to “Let’s assume I advised you … , but the advice would be bad.”

### <span class="fac">[3a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3a.webp)</span>

He reports that he has pain & believes that the enemy is approaching.

### <span class="fac">[3a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3a.webp)</span>

Can one communicate to the doctor as a symptom of a mental illness “I believe …”? – But perhaps something like: “I always hear voices.” “I always assume that he is unfaithful, but he is not.” The line of the concept seems to be abruptly broken! –

### <span class="fac">[3a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3a.webp),[3b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

If I represent the statement “The enemy is approaching” by a picture of the approaching enemy, how should I represent the statement “I believe the enemy…”? Again by a picture of the approaching enemy, but perhaps not so clearly depicted – or by a picture of myself and in my head, perhaps a picture of the approaching enemy? – But how if I were also to bring the statement “The enemy is approaching” to representation in this way, by a picture in my head? Would that be wrong? As wrong as if I had learned to put the words “I believe” before _every_ statement of mine. It is the eternal conflict between the picture of meaning, of sense, & the technique of language.

### <span class="fac">[3b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

“The sentence ‘I believe it, & it is not true’ _can_ indeed be true. Namely, if I really believe it, & this belief turns out to be false.”

### <span class="fac">[3b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

“I believe it, but God lets me say that it is not so.”

### <span class="fac">[3b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

Imagine a custom according to which one only has the right to make statements about one’s own state. Everything else is considered presumptuous. But, in practical terms, people live just as we do.

### <span class="fac">[3b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

“It is certainly raining today.” “Let’s assume it is certainly raining today.” Likewise: ‘perhaps’.

### <span class="fac">[3b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

I say of the other, “He seems to believe…”, & others say it of me. Now, why do I never say it of myself, even if others say it of me _justifiably_? Likewise: “It is clear that he believes…”. Do I not see myself? – One can say it.

### <span class="fac">[3b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/3b.webp)</span>

A: “I believe it is raining.” – B: “I do not believe it.” Well, they are not contradicting each other; each is only saying something about himself.

### <span class="fac">[4a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4a.webp)</span>

I assume I look out the window & say to myself, “Ah, it’s raining.” – This is, after all, _something like_ the assumption: I believe that it is raining. – What corresponds to the assertive sentence “I believe that it is raining”? Should I say _that_ one: “I say to myself ‘Ah, it’s raining’”, or _that_ one: “Ah, it’s raining”? – In the assertion, I can omit the “I say to myself”, but not in the assumption. But it seems as if these assertion & this assumption don’t really belong together! Or if I do say that they fit, then one would now have to find the assertion that fits that assumption in the _ordinary_ sense. _That_ would be ‘like trying to get out of one’s own skin’ (Frege).

### <span class="fac">[4a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4a.webp)</span>

If I assume that someone believes…, then I can also assume that he expresses his belief & says “I believe that it is so” or even “It is so”; I assume that he uses these as approximate equivalents.

### <span class="fac">[4a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4a.webp)</span>

What is it that suggests that the person who says “I believe…” is saying something about the state of his soul? This is supposed to be a state of our soul, & disgust is also, & sensations are also.

### <span class="fac">[4a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4a.webp),[4b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4b.webp)</span>

Someone might suggest that the sense of “I believe it will…” is: “My reaction is quite clearly ‘It will rain’”. As if the utterance were simply a reading of an inner instrument. – But now he would have to add that this reading must be used in the same way as the assertion: it is the case that it is so – i.e., that this belongs to the analogy with the utterance “I believe”. For this would not follow from the picture of reading an instrument.

### <span class="fac">[4b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4b.webp)</span>

“The concept is not just a technique, but also a physiognomy.” Does “physiognomy” here mean “something memorable”? Something that can become a good acquaintance? The _center_ of a collection of associations? –

### <span class="fac">[4b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4b.webp)</span>

I feel unwell. Not physically, but mentally. I fear the outbreak of madness. God alone knows if I am in danger.

### <span class="fac">[4b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4b.webp)</span>

04.02.1948

Sensory impressions. They seem to be similar in their nature. What do we mean by this? “Can there really be anything more dissimilar than a tone & a color?” one might ask. But here too, one makes a strange mistake. For what is the function of this statement? To whom are we making this communication? Perhaps to someone whose language & whose language is foreign to us, & to whom we want to explain a great misunderstanding. It is therefore an explanation of a meaning.

### <span class="fac">[4b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/4b.webp),[5a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5a.webp)</span>

Now, to whom are we making the communication that there is indeed some kinship, & for what purpose? We want to explain what kind of kinship can be in question.

### <span class="fac">[5a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5a.webp)</span>

“There is no bluish yellow,” similar to the sentence “There is no regular two-triangle,” one might call it a statement of color geometry, i.e., a concept-defining sentence.

### <span class="fac">[5a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5a.webp)</span>

If I had taught someone to use the 6 primary color names & the syllable “-ish,” then I could give him commands such as “Paint a greenish white here!” – But then I say to him “Paint a reddish green!” I observe his reaction. Perhaps he will mix green & red & not be satisfied with the result; perhaps he will finally say: “There is no reddish green.” – Analogously, I could have brought him to say “There is no regular two-triangle!” or “There is no square root of -25.”

### <span class="fac">[5a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5a.webp),[5b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp)</span>

Should I now say: “It is in the nature of the colors green & red that they do not have intermediate colors”? – ‘In their nature’ – that would mean: if you know what is understood by “green” & “red”, i.e., if you are familiar with the nature of these objects, then you know that they do not have intermediate colors. – But the expressions used here are borrowed from cases in the environment, & their application is difficult to carry out. The sentence says something like: “Just look at green & red, & you will know that it is so”; & that is true, insofar as the person understands the meaning of those words when shown something green & something red. But what do I look at in order to understand the meaning of “greenish”? Perhaps at a greenish yellow? – And now the application to greenish red!? I could also say: “I do _not_ know what is meant by ‘greenish red’”.

### <span class="fac">[5b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp)</span>

“In the nature of the colors,” i.e., actually: in their inner structure, in the structure itself. But do colors have a structure? The application of the color word has one. And in that sense, the concept has one.

### <span class="fac">[5b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp)</span>

To say “It is due to the concept” sounds like: “Nothing is easier than for us to devise another concept.”

### <span class="fac">[5b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp)</span>

Between green & red, I want to say, there is a _geometrical_ void, not a physical one.

### <span class="fac">[5b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp)</span>

But does this correspond to something physical? I do not deny that. (And even if it were merely our habit with _these_ concepts, with these language-games. But I am not saying that this is the case.)

### <span class="fac">[5b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp)</span>

Is the fact that we compare things in _this_ way (with each other), that we take them together in _this_ way in use, is this arbitrary? No more than the fact that we nourish ourselves from this and not from that.

### <span class="fac">[5b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/5b.webp),[6a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6a.webp)</span>

If we teach a person a certain technique through examples – that he then, in a certain new case, proceeds _so_ and not _so_, or that he then falters, that for him, therefore, this and not that is the ‘natural’ continuation, this alone is a very important fact of nature.

### <span class="fac">[6a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6a.webp)</span>

Perhaps someone explains: “I cannot imagine anything under bluish-yellow, so this is a fact of psychology.” And there is some truth in that. One could call _green_ “bluish-yellow”; and if this seems unnatural to you, whether through your particular education or for some other reason, then this is an important fact.

### <span class="fac">[6a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6a.webp)</span>

“But if I mean green by ‘bluish-yellow,’ then I simply understand this expression differently than in the original way. The original understanding refers to another and indeed _unattainable_ way.” But what is the correct simile here? That of a physically impassable path, or that of the non-existence of the path? So the simile of physical or mathematical impossibility?

### <span class="fac">[6a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6a.webp)</span>

05.02.1948

We have a system of colors, just like a system of numbers. Do the systems lie in _our_ nature, or in the nature of things? How should one put it? – _Not_ in the nature of numbers or colors.

### <span class="fac">[6a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6a.webp)</span>

Can one, or does one want to, imagine ‘nothing’ under bluish yellow? Strange question. “Bluish yellow” falls into a hole.

### <span class="fac">[6a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6a.webp),[6b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6b.webp)</span>

Does this system contain something arbitrary? Yes and no. It is related to something arbitrary and to something non-arbitrary.

### <span class="fac">[6b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6b.webp)</span>

Is the color octahedron a picture of the _nature_ of colors?

### <span class="fac">[6b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6b.webp)</span>

Imagine I show someone a series of colorful colors and say, “Look, here I show you some of the furnishings of the world; look at them carefully, how beautiful they are” – could one really say that God created these colors; not rather _these_ colored objects that I point to? If I show someone colors (i.e., color samples) and say “Look, these are the colorful colors; there are no others,” am I showing him the _nature_ of colorful color? Does he see a fact? He only sees a picture! (Mathematical proof.)

### <span class="fac">[6b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6b.webp)</span>

I could imagine that someone looks at the color octahedron and says: “It is wonderful how everything here corresponds to the nature of color!” So a differently arranged scheme would not be so good. – Or would it be just as good, but represent something else? (That is, correspond to a different concept?) Or should I say that some scheme does not correspond to any or to a not _important_ concept? Should I say: “The color octahedron represents a very important concept”?

### <span class="fac">[6b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/6b.webp),[7a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp)</span>

It is immediately apparent that one wants to recognize nothing but intermediate colors between red & green. (And whether this is always immediately apparent to humans, or only after experience & education, is irrelevant here.)

### <span class="fac">[7a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp)</span>

“_This_ is how I want to take things together!” one could say.

### <span class="fac">[7a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp)</span>

One could also say: “_This_ combination makes sense to me!” – But what makes it make sense to me? Is it not as if the _abacus_ made sense to one?!

### <span class="fac">[7a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp)</span>

Is the concept of “ …-like” not precisely _determined_ by the color octahedron? (That is, do not the words “ …-like …” derive their _meaning_ from it, in contrast to the idea that it says something about colors that are “ …-like … ”?)

### <span class="fac">[7a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp)</span>

What would we think of people who knew a ‘reddish green’ (e.g., call olive green that)? And what does _this_ mean: “They then have a different concept of colors”? As if we wanted to say: “It would then not be _this_, but another” – by pointing to ours. As if there were therefore an _object_ to which the concept unambiguously belonged.

### <span class="fac">[7a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp)</span>

People know a reddish green. But there _is_ no such thing! – What a strange sentence. – (How do you know?)

### <span class="fac">[7a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7a.webp),[7b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7b.webp)</span>

(The picture that characterizes the concept would be something like an algebraic formula.)

### <span class="fac">[7b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7b.webp)</span>

Let’s say it (once) like this: Must these people notice the discrepancy? Perhaps they are too dull for it. And then again: perhaps not. –

### <span class="fac">[7b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7b.webp)</span>

Yes, but does nature have nothing to say here?! Yes – Only it makes itself heard in a different way. “Somewhere you will run into existence and non-existence!” – But that means _facts_, not concepts.

### <span class="fac">[7b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7b.webp)</span>

Is there a natural history of colors? – It would be timeless. – But why is one tempted to speak of such a thing, and, for example, to regard the color octahedron as a schema for it? (A mineralogy of colors.) But because nothing arbitrary attaches to it. But that does not make it a representation of _one_ natural phenomenon. Rather, it only points out that much depends on this representation.

### <span class="fac">[7b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7b.webp)</span>

“If he does _that_, then he simply has a completely different character.” The concept of ‘character’ from which everything else springs; the sources of this concept.

### <span class="fac">[7b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/7b.webp),[8a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8a.webp)</span>

It is a fact of the highest importance that a color, which we are inclined to call, for example, “reddish yellow,” can actually be produced by mixing red & yellow (in various ways). And that we are unable to recognize a color that has been created by mixing red & green as one that can be produced in this way. (But what does “easily” mean here?) There might be people who, without counting, recognize a regular 97-sided figure at a glance.

### <span class="fac">[8a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8a.webp)</span>

If the door opens inward, & I don’t _think_ about the possibility that it could open that way, then I am locked in.

### <span class="fac">[8a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8a.webp)</span>

Concepts compared to a style of painting: Is even our style of painting arbitrary? Can we simply decide to adopt the style of the Egyptians? Or is it just a matter of beautiful & ugly?

### <span class="fac">[8a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8a.webp)</span>

06.02.1948

A country in which every form has its own color & every color has only that form. If a stone were to appear that was green (as only grass usually is), people would call it “stone-grass.” In nature, color & form seem to be more _one_ than in a room.

### <span class="fac">[8a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8a.webp)</span>

But are ‘looking at the form’ & ‘looking at (or enjoying) the color’ not always different?

### <span class="fac">[8a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8a.webp),[8b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8b.webp)</span>

So do you want to assume that these people would be unable to arrange different natural objects in a series according to their color? That they do not have this language-game? I mean: they may not even need to have color names, but they can still say something that amounts to: this stone is closer in color to this leaf than to that one. (Russell.) Yes, of course, they don't necessarily have to say that either, but just enjoy having objects – as we would say – arranged in rows according to their color gradations. But would I have to say that these people have abstracted the color concepts from the phenomena?

### <span class="fac">[8b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8b.webp)</span>

Because it sometimes seems to us as if we had, as it were, _extracted_ the object green (e.g.) from the things around us, like an essence.

### <span class="fac">[8b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8b.webp)</span>

It would be questionable to say that our concepts show how we _see_ the world. But there is something true in it; only it is not so much about _seeing_ as about _use_. Are the pieces of furniture in a room ‘unities’ for us because we ‘see’ them as such? Isn’t it rather that they are firmly put together, that I generally do not use their parts individually & in new combinations for different purposes (and everything that is connected with it)?

### <span class="fac">[8b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/8b.webp),[9a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9a.webp)</span>

But what do I want to say? That we would have different concepts if our environment & our life were different? And would that be a scientific hypothesis? Or do I want to say: different concepts – that is: different language-games, thus a different life. But a different life is one that is similar to ours; otherwise we would no longer see it as ‘life’, i.e., it would lose that specific interest for us. – How close must it be to our life? This question cannot be correctly posed; because in it I forget the _purpose_ that this imagining has in the first place. Why do I even want to construct variations of our concepts? Doesn't it happen in order to emphasize differences, & sometimes similarities, that have been blurred so far? We build new boundaries, tear down old ones, in order to escape (?) the hypnosis of a familiar way of representation (?)

### <span class="fac">[9a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9a.webp)</span>

Did we _invent_ human language? No more than walking on two legs.

### <span class="fac">[9a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9a.webp)</span>

The concepts of constellations. Here Köhler would say: You see that sensation brings them together & is therefore responsible for our concepts.

### <span class="fac">[9a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9a.webp),[9b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp)</span>

It is an important fact if it is the case that people, when asked to reproduce the Big Dipper in lines, always do so in _one_ particular way, or mostly, & never in a certain other way, if left to themselves. But does _that_ mean: seeing the constellation in this way? Does it already contain the possibility of a change of aspect? Because it is the change that we perceive as analogous to the change of a picture.

### <span class="fac">[9b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp)</span>

If the change of aspect did not exist, there would be only _one_ perception, not a so or so _seeing_.

### <span class="fac">[9b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp)</span>

That seems absurd. As if one wanted to say, “If I only ever heat with coal, & not also sometimes with something else, then I am not heating with coal.” But can one not say: “If there were only _one_ substance, then one would have no use for the word ‘substance’”? But that means: the concept of ‘substance’ presupposes the concept of ‘difference of substance’. (As does the concept of the chess king the concept of the chess move; or as does the concept of _color_ the concept of _colors_.)

### <span class="fac">[9b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp)</span>

The characteristic of the aspect _is_ its comparability with seeing _this_ object & not _that_ one, even though both are contained in our perception.

### <span class="fac">[9b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp)</span>

We must always remember that we do not want to explain one psychological phenomenon from another; but only to group them as we find them.

### <span class="fac">[9b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp)</span>

We do not want to say that _this_ is actually _that_, but only, as far as we can, to point out similarities & dissimilarities.

### <span class="fac">[9b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/9b.webp),[10a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10a.webp)</span>

07.02.1948

I communicate something different to someone if I say:

a) that in the drawing, which he does not see, this & that form is contained –

b) that in the drawing, which he sees, the form is contained, which he has not yet noticed –

c) that I have just discovered that the drawing, which was familiar to me, contained this form –

d) that I am now just seeing the drawing in this aspect. Each of these communications has a different interest.

### <span class="fac">[10a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10a.webp)</span>

The first is a partial description of a perceived object, somewhat analogous to “I see something red there.” The second is what one could call a “geometric communication.” It is timeless in contrast to the first. The discovery that this is the case is of the nature of mathematical discoveries.

### <span class="fac">[10a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10a.webp)</span>

But could the communication not also be made in a temporal form? Perhaps like this: “If you turn this drawing back & forth, you will _see_ this form in it, without its lines seeming to have changed.” The fact that we use this as a concept-defining element is not yet established.

### <span class="fac">[10a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10a.webp),[10b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10b.webp)</span>

How does one make the _discovery_? Perhaps like this: one traces certain lines of the drawing on translucent paper – perhaps quite randomly. Then one sees: that’s a face! Or one makes this exclamation upon seeing the drawing & then traces those lines. – & where is the _discovery_ here? – This must first be interpreted as a discovery, & in particular, as a _geometric_ discovery.

### <span class="fac">[10b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10b.webp)</span>

The discovery, the recognition of this form in that one, didn’t have to proceed as if the well-known image had suddenly changed its nature – as it is when we suddenly see the cube schema in a different spatial arrangement. (It could just as well happen that the discoverer picks out a figure _randomly_ from the drawing.) A play with the shifting of the aspect is, in any case, not necessary.

### <span class="fac">[10b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10b.webp)</span>

How is it with that? One sees a surface covered with lines that are without rules; suddenly one notices a script in them, which one now follows & reads. What should one say: Does one _see_ the script in those lines? Of course. But is it the same case as seeing the shape in the ambiguous figure? It is a related one. The script _fell_ to him, _upon_. First something script-like in the lines, then that it really is a script. “The script began to light up & now it is shining brightly.”

### <span class="fac">[10b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10b.webp)</span>

An aspect can appear to me in that someone draws my attention to it, thereby _making me aware_ of it. How greatly does this differ from the _seeing_ of colors & shapes!

### <span class="fac">[10b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/10b.webp)</span>

Philosophizing is systematic remembering.

### <span class="fac">[11a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

Think of the process of memorizing a specific constellation among a larger number of dots so that you can immediately find it again when you look at it next time. While you do this—doesn’t what you see change constantly? There is, for example, a bringing together of the constellation with the gaze, before it is seen as a whole.

### <span class="fac">[11a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

The question is not “How does one do that?”, “what is happening there?”, but: What does one communicate to someone, when one says…?

### <span class="fac">[11a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

Noticing & seeing. One does not say “I noticed it for 5 minutes.”

### <span class="fac">[11a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

Does it follow from this that one has not seen it in _this_ way, that one has seen it in some other way? Does it follow from this that one has never compared it _with that_, that one has compared it with something _else_? (Or even with oneself?)

### <span class="fac">[11a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

It would be possible that someone, to whom I show a form in a picture by tracing it, would always lose sight of it and have to be shown it again.

### <span class="fac">[11a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

Black and white cross. “The one aspect now disappears completely, as if it had never existed. I don’t see a bit of it.”

### <span class="fac">[11a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11a.webp)</span>

It is for me now _this_, & now _that_ – _whatever that may mean_.

### <span class="fac">[11b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11b.webp)</span>

Black & white cross. “Sometimes I envision _this_, sometimes _that_.”

### <span class="fac">[11b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11b.webp)</span>

You must consider that the description “I now see a black cross…” should not be understood. Who said “Now I see a black cross… now a white one,” could be answered: “You describe the same thing each time!”

### <span class="fac">[11b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11b.webp)</span>

“That’s not really _seeing_!” – “That _is_ seeing!” – Both statements must be justifiable in terms of their meaning.

### <span class="fac">[11b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11b.webp)</span>

“When I look at a painting, the human figures in it are human figures for me; I _see_ them as such; I do not see color patches & merely _interpret_ them in that way.” And how do I know that the other person experiences it the same way? What are the signs of that? How would he behave if it were different? Well, he talks, for example, about the picture, as about this & that human situation.

### <span class="fac">[11b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/11b.webp),[12a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12a.webp)</span>

It is incorrect to say: “In this respect, it is exactly as common sense says it is.” (Köhler, Moore.) Common sense does not make any decision here at all. It is not _so_; rather, we simply see what common sense always takes to be the case with what is seen. For it does not express any opinion in this regard. Rather, it is the case that conceptual misunderstandings concerning ‘seeing’ arise from the fact that we do not properly grasp our everyday concept of seeing. We must adhere to this.

### <span class="fac">[12a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12a.webp)</span>

“But do we _really_ see the human figures in the picture?” What exactly is one asking? It seems that a disturbance of a concept is occurring through something slightly different. One should perhaps ask: “Do I then really see the figures in the same sense as…?” Or also: “What reason do I have to speak of ‘seeing’ here & what is it that resists this within me?”

### <span class="fac">[12a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12a.webp)</span>

For that is the constant philosophical question: “What resists it within me?”

### <span class="fac">[12a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12a.webp)</span>

I would like to pose the question, for example: “Am I _always conscious_ of the spatiality (depth) of this book, e.g., while I am looking at it? Do I, as it were, _feel_ it all the time? – But pose the question in the third person. When would you say that he is _always conscious_ of it, and when is the opposite the case? – Suppose you asked him – but how did he learn to answer this question? – Well, he knows, for example, what it means to feel pain continuously. But that will only confuse him, as it confuses me.

### <span class="fac">[12a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12a.webp),[12b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12b.webp)</span>

If he now tells me that he is continuously conscious of the depth, – do I believe him? And if he says that he is only conscious of it from time to time, when he happens to be talking about it, – do I believe _that_? It seems to me that these answers are based on a false premise. – But it is different if he tells me that the object sometimes appears to him spatially, and sometimes flat.

### <span class="fac">[12b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12b.webp)</span>

Black and white cross. One could imagine that the drawing of the cube diagram automatically assumes alternating aspects. The rotating drum sometimes suddenly changes its apparent type of movement, without one knowing why.

### <span class="fac">[12b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12b.webp)</span>

“But surely you won’t deny that it is a different visual impression; there is a different subjective visual object present!” – How, for example, in what other case? How were these (your) words first explained? – And now we must see how that explanation applies to the present case.

### <span class="fac">[12b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12b.webp)</span>

I could convey an important message to someone by sending them a picture of a landscape. Does he look at it as a technical drawing; do I mean: does he _decipher_ it? He looks at it and acts accordingly. He sees rocks, trees, a house, etc. in it.

### <span class="fac">[12b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12b.webp)</span>

08.02.1948

(Here, the situation is one of practical necessity, but the means of communication is one that is not subject to agreements, definitions, and the like, and which otherwise only serves poetic purposes. But it also serves the ordinary language for poetic purposes.) [This remark is extremely unclear.]

### <span class="fac">[12b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/12b.webp),[13a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp)</span>

When, as happens, in a landscape I first take something to be _that_, then _that_; I first see it one way, then another: – It is as if the arrangement of my visual impression is now different. “I _have_ something different” (or also “It _is_ something different”) I would like to say. The theater of my visual space.

### <span class="fac">[13a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp)</span>

The aspects of

![](../graphics/233a,43.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">:</span>

It is as if a _mental image_ came into contact with the visual impression and remained in contact for a time.

### <span class="fac">[13a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp)</span>

But the case of the black and white cross is different and similar to that of the _spatial_ aspects (e.g., the prism drawing).

### <span class="fac">[13a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp)</span>

We now have various descriptions that fit the different cases of ‘seeing aspects’ better or less well.

### <span class="fac">[13a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp)</span>

The _lighting up_ of the aspect is what captures our attention.

### <span class="fac">[13a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp)</span>

The temptation to say “I see it _this_ way,” by pointing at the same thing with “it” and “this way.”

### <span class="fac">[13a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13a.webp),[13b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13b.webp)</span>

When we have promised something, or in conversation we cannot find a word, we often say “You know –”. Does this mean that the meaning of the unmentioned object comes first? It is the word “thereby” that causes the problem.

### <span class="fac">[13b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13b.webp)</span>

In the foreground of the picture is a cross. “Has it always been an uninterrupted cross for you?” – I cannot say; I am at a loss as to what _I_ should answer. Now, the aspect changes (somehow), & I no longer see it as a cross, & then again as before. Now I say: “So I have after all always seen it as an uninterrupted cross, because now, for the first time, my aspect has been interrupted.” – But what if one answered: “You have always _regarded_ it as a cross (disposition), then, for the first time, you _saw_ it as something else, & then saw it again according to your first conception.” But what speaks for this _representation_? To justify it, I must ask him a question, the answer to which will be the _justification_.

### <span class="fac">[13b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/13b.webp)</span>

The problems are, as it were, all very serious. Why? Because they concern important institutions of people? Because they concern important things in human life, and it is important whether one says true or false things about them, most of all, whether one says sincere or insincere things about them.

### <span class="fac">[14a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14a.webp)</span>

“I see the picture all the time as a bird.” How do you see it? as _what_? What must you point to in order to explain this? And if you now look at a real bird, what then?

### <span class="fac">[14a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14a.webp)</span>

If he sees the _ordinary_ picture of a hare as a hare, the picture of a duck as a duck, then it is not so surprising that this transition, given a certain kind of picture, can take place. Because then it is simply the transition from one normal state to another normal state. But what is incomprehensible is that it is as if the picture is replaced – and _yet_ not changed.

### <span class="fac">[14a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14a.webp)</span>

I am inclined to say: In the aspect-switch, I see the picture more _vividly_ than that, or this. Corresponding, for example, to the exclamation “A duck!”

### <span class="fac">[14a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14a.webp)</span>

I look at a picture on which I first recognize nothing. Suddenly I say: “Ah, that is a …”. I now saw it as that and that. Or did I only recognize that it can be understood as a projection of these and those things? What is the difference? I refer to the picture as that and that animal. I behave quite differently towards the picture than towards a technical drawing that I understand.

### <span class="fac">[14a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14a.webp),[14b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14b.webp)</span>

Now I have long recognized the picture as that of a bird, for example, and I always see it as such. One could ask: Is this seeing now simply a continuation of what began in that sudden recognition? Or was the acute seeing something different from the chronic? – Again, I am at a loss. – This question cannot be answered, so it should not be asked.

### <span class="fac">[14b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14b.webp)</span>

One could also ask: Is what distinguishes acute from chronic seeing here simply the surprise in the former? or also something in the seeing experience itself? – Yes, also: Is the acute seeing experience during the transition from meaningless strokes to the meaningful picture the same as that during the shifting of the duck’s head into the hare’s head and vice versa? And the first thing I want to say is that the latter is _more_ astonishing than the recognition of the picture in the jumble of strokes.

### <span class="fac">[14b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14b.webp)</span>

We marvel at the picture. Say: How is it so very different now! –

### <span class="fac">[14b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14b.webp)</span>

Now: We say “A duck!”, when we previously saw it as a hare (in some sense). We marvel at it in the new aspect. We ask ourselves: How can it be so very different now? How _can_ the eye now look there, and now here?

### <span class="fac">[14b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/14b.webp),[15a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp)</span>

I ask: “Is seeing the same experience in both cases?” Yes, how do I compare it? I bring both before me – now they are different; but there is no isolating and considering the ‘experience of seeing’.

### <span class="fac">[15a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp)</span>

It says very different things “the seen,” or “the description of the seen.”

### <span class="fac">[15a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp)</span>

There are indeed manifold similarities here.

### <span class="fac">[15a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp)</span>

09.02.1948

‘Organization’ of the visual image. With the words “_This_ belongs together, _this_ does not,” aspects can be described; but not all.

### <span class="fac">[15a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp)</span>

The processes involved in _searching_ for the solution to the ambiguous figure. In one sense, one could say that what is seen changes continuously. However, what one would not say is that one suddenly sees something completely different, with the old image having disappeared.

### <span class="fac">[15a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp)</span>

One must simply take the concept of ‘seeing’ as one finds it; one should not try to refine it. – But why not, actually? – Because it is not our task to change it, to introduce a concept that is more suitable for some purposes (as science does), but to understand it; that is, not to form a false picture of it.

### <span class="fac">[15a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15a.webp),[15b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15b.webp)</span>

The concept of ‘seeing’ makes a confused impression. Well, that’s how it is. – I look at the landscape; my gaze wanders, I see all sorts of clear and unclear movements; _this_ registers clearly in my mind, _that_ only very vaguely. How completely fragmented what we see can appear to us! And now look at what we call a description of what is seen! But that is what we call it. We do not have a real, respectable example of such a description and say: Well, the rest is still unclear, still needs to be clarified, or simply has to be relegated to the corner.

### <span class="fac">[15b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/15b.webp)</span>

It is a great danger for us here to want to make fine distinctions. It is similar when one wants to explain the concept of a physical body from what is ‘actually seen’. Rather, one must _add_ the familiar language-game, and incorrect, but perhaps very obvious, explanations and constructions should be pointed out as such. The primitive language-game, which we originally learned, does not need justification; false attempts at justification and analysis that insistently impose themselves on us, however, need to be rejected.

### <span class="fac">[16a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16a.webp)</span>

The conceptual relationships are very complicated.

### <span class="fac">[16a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16a.webp)</span>

But when I see grass, do I not have a feeling of grass; when I see the branch, a feeling of wood; yes, different feelings when I see different types of wood (at least _sometimes_)? – Yes, I would sometimes say so. (One sometimes says that the image of a tree is ‘_felt_’.)

### <span class="fac">[16a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16a.webp)</span>

It is always necessary to separate the expression from the technique. And the case where we can specify the technique from the case where we cannot.

### <span class="fac">[16a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16a.webp),[16b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16b.webp)</span>

The image of a human face, of a human or animal, even if only schematic, is for me – as I would like to express it – an image-face, an image-human, an image-animal. A face, a human, an animal ‘in its own right’. “We do not think of a _real_ face, etc., when we look at it,” one might explain. But what does that actually mean? What is it like when one thinks of a real one? Yes, it is clear: the transition to the real animal can always be made; I _know_ that these lines are a mouse and that a mouse is an animal that looks and lives like this. But I participate in the images in the cinema without comparing what I see there with a real mouse in my memory. I don’t even think of such a thing. And yet the connection exists: if I knew nothing of mice, I would not understand the image. – And yet what I am saying here is still false, because it is far too general.

### <span class="fac">[16b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16b.webp)</span>

What is the _criterion_ for the fact that, for one person, the face in the image is an image-face, and has lost its close connection with a real face? (I am thinking of Busch’s drawings with a few dots and lines.)

### <span class="fac">[16b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16b.webp)</span>

I would like to say that in some images I see a real head, and in others only an image-face, an image-human. (How great is the _importance_ of this phenomenon?)

### <span class="fac">[16b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16b.webp)</span>

Just remember your relationship to a portrait: “It is very much like him/her!” You certainly don’t say that when you look at every face in the picture. But this is only _one_ example of a special relationship to an image. “You _see_ him/her sitting and working!”, even if we don’t recognize anyone in the picture, is something else. One would not say this about a caricature, for example, and yet one can find it excellent.

### <span class="fac">[16b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16b.webp)</span>

A self-satisfied, smiling pig in Busch (“Afterwards”). I would not exclaim “That is exactly how a pig does it!” But with other pictures, exactly that.

### <span class="fac">[16b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/16b.webp)</span>

One could sometimes explain that the image-human is a two-dimensional _variant_ of a human. (But one can also leave out the word “two-dimensional”.)

### <span class="fac">[17a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17a.webp)</span>

But what do I share through this? Does it merely mean that I participate in the figures in a picture story or in a cartoon film, which are very unlike real people, and that real people do not come to mind? Or is it just a witty, quasi-mathematical observation?

### <span class="fac">[17a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17a.webp)</span>

When I look at the photograph in front of me, I am certainly not inclined to talk about the ‘image-human’ in it. Much more so when I see a purely schematic, but expressive, drawing of a face.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-17a.3) <span class="fac">[17a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17a.webp)</span> {#17a3}

10.02.1948

Take money from every mistake.

### <span class="fac">[17a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17a.webp)</span>

The suspicious expression that I used was “I do not think of a real mouse etc. in these images.” For how is it when I do think of one? How do I think of it? – It happens that I compare an image with a real mouse, for example, in my memory. I say “That is exactly what it looks like,” or “That is not what it looks like.”

### <span class="fac">[17a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17a.webp),[17b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

If I say “I now see it as a duck” – do I mean: as a real duck, or as an image-duck? Can I say that? No. – If I had an ordinary picture of a duck and a duck at hand, – can I say what I would point to in order to explain it and what not? – But if I had ducks and hares drawn in the same way in front of me, in a picture story, then I would perhaps point to these image-creatures in the explanation and say that I see the ambiguous image particularly as _that_.

### <span class="fac">[17b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

I say “The picture is a squirrel for me.” Now I can ask: Do you mean: a real one, or: an image-squirrel? I would perhaps like to say: It depends on the picture.

### <span class="fac">[17b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

And if I say that this picture (e.g., Dürer’s) is a _real_ squirrel for me, then I certainly don’t mean that I took the depicted one to be a real animal. (There are indeed manifold affinities here.)

### <span class="fac">[17b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

Again: What do I share with another person?

### <span class="fac">[17b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

I could probably say: “My thoughts naturally go from _this_ picture to real grass, to real animals; from that picture, never.”

### <span class="fac">[17b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

(For I am indeed inclined to say this and that; although the _use_ of it is unclear to me. It is as if I were portraying something, more or less accurately, through my words. But rather it is just the picture for hanging in my room, than for any other use.)

### <span class="fac">[17b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp)</span>

When looking at the picture, one says: “Do you not _see_ a squirrel!” – “Do you not _feel_ the softness of this fur!” – And one says this about certain pictures, but not about others.

### <span class="fac">[17b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/17b.webp),[18a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18a.webp)</span>

“Yes, _that’s_ how a duck does it!” people say. But not with the schematic image of a duck.

### <span class="fac">[18a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18a.webp)</span>

Am I equally inclined to say that there are picture flowers, trees, grasses, as there are picture faces? No. Except perhaps in a cartoon film. And that should show me what the (actual) function of the expression picture-human, etc. is.

### <span class="fac">[18a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18a.webp)</span>

I come to the idea of the picture-concept, which is not unlike a mathematical idea, through _certain_ ways of representation, under _certain_ circumstances.

### <span class="fac">[18a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18a.webp)</span>

If in a letter, a line is written at a right angle to the other, and I read each (of them) without difficulty, because it is clearly separated from the other, – should I say: I see the picture _like this_? – How? – Well, as writing that I read. I could also have to report about this sheet, and either report that it is a messy scribble on it, or report that this and that is written on it. I report my perception.

### <span class="fac">[18a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18a.webp)</span>

But I may have to laboriously pick out the one writing from the other, or I read it, completely undisturbed by the other.

### <span class="fac">[18b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18b.webp)</span>

I also say: “The rest disappears,” or “I don’t see it at all.”

### <span class="fac">[18b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18b.webp)</span>

I now tell another person: “The writing is completely separated from all the other lines; I actually see only it, the other is not there.” He says, “That’s how it is for me too.” – It is not difficult to describe processes that are characteristic of this state of affairs. (Recognizing the _character_ of the handwriting, accurately copying it.)

### <span class="fac">[18b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18b.webp)</span>

Helmholtz on reading the sounds of a speaker from various noises that accompany the speaking: In this depiction, it seemed to me, there was a mistake. But what was it? – It seems to me that we are amazed, by misinterpreting the matter, at what we should not be amazed at.

### <span class="fac">[18b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/18b.webp),[19a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19a.webp)</span>

February 11, 1948

If someone sees a sheet written by me, then, if he can read and write the Latin alphabet, he can easily copy it quite accurately. He only needs to read it and write it again. Despite the deviations in the handwriting, he will easily produce a more or less accurate image of the lines on my sheet. If he had not learned to read and write the Latin alphabet, it would have been more difficult for him to copy those intricate lines. – Should I now say that whoever has learned this _sees_ the described sheet quite differently than another? – What do we know about it? It could be that we gave that sheet to someone to copy before he learned to write and read, and then again after he learned to write and read. And he will then perhaps say: “Yes, now I see these lines quite differently.” He might also explain: “Now I actually only see the writing that I am currently reading; everything else is around it, which does not concern me and which I hardly notice.” Well, that means that he sees the lines differently – if he really reacts differently to them. Likewise, someone who has learned to read can give a different account of the sheet that is written lengthwise and crosswise than someone who cannot read. And the same applies to speaking and the accompanying noises.

### <span class="fac">[19a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19a.webp)</span>

Now, something in me rebelled against the fact that, while the eardrum vibrates in a so-to-speak irregular manner, our ear, or hearing, performs a separation. I wanted to say “That is just how one _hears_ it! This is to be regarded as given.” Or also: We do not perform a separation. It only seems that we do. I could also say: There is no reason to be surprised by the artificiality of the ear. One cannot, for example, call this a skill of the ear; conversely, the opposite would not be a lack of skill.

### <span class="fac">[19a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19a.webp),[19b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19b.webp)</span>

February 12, 1948

[→] The natural answer would be: When we see an F or J, we usually don’t think that the letter ‘is looking in a direction’. – But if I had asked: “Do you _always_ see this sheet _green_, as long as you look at it, and you answer the question about its color with ‘Green’?” – then I would not be inclined to answer, I haven’t always thought about the color. That is, I want to say: One sees the F and the J looking in a _direction_, as long as one _thinks_ about it, as long as one is so _engaged_ with the letters. – But is what I say about this _reliable_? For it is not clear whether I am speaking about my own experience, or how another person could rely on my answer.

### <span class="fac">[19b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19b.webp)</span>

There is the answer: “I have never looked at a J in that way.”

### <span class="fac">[19b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19b.webp)</span>

Suppose someone answered: “For me, it _always_ looks in this direction” – would we accept his answer? It would seem to suggest that he thinks, whenever he sees this letter, about such connections. (Just as one says: “Whenever I see this person, I have to think about how he ‥‥․”)

### <span class="fac">[19b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/19b.webp)</span>

But if we now look at the picture of a face, or a real face, – can one also say here, I only see it looking in that direction as long as I am _so_ engaged with it? – What is the difference? The communication “This face is looking to the right” is, usually, about the location of the face. I make it to someone who does not see the face themselves. It is the communication of a perception.

### <span class="fac">[20a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20a.webp)</span>

[→] Does this then show that in these cases it cannot be a matter of ‘seeing’ – but rather of thinking? Something that speaks against this is that one even wants to talk about ‘seeing’. – Should I then say, here is a phenomenon between seeing & thinking? No; but a concept that lies between that of ‘seeing’ & that of ‘thinking’, i.e., has similarity with both; & phenomena that are related to those of seeing & thinking (e.g. the phenomenon of the utterance “I see the F looking to the right”).

### <span class="fac">[20a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20a.webp)</span>

“I always thought it was a bowl; I thought it went in, but it comes out.” Did I have to experience a change of aspect?

### <span class="fac">[20a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20a.webp)</span>

14.02.1948

“My impression of it used to be different: perhaps the lighting has changed.” I do not have to be able to specify the change in the visual image that now lets me recognize the shape differently. But I do not say that the visual impression is simply unstable, that the same visual image can take on both different spatial forms. So that, for example, I do not fall into saying “Now I can no longer see it as I used to.”

### <span class="fac">[20a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20a.webp)</span>

“Something happens & yet nothing happens to the visual image!”

### <span class="fac">[20a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20a.webp),[20b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20b.webp)</span>

How does one recognize that people spatially _see_? I ask someone how the terrain looks that he is surveying: “Is it _like this_?” (spatial gesture) – “Yes.” – “How do you know that?” – “It’s not foggy, I see very clearly.” – No reasons are given for the _assumption_. It is only natural for us to represent what we see spatially; while the flat representation, whether through drawing or words, requires special practice & instruction. The peculiarity of children’s drawings.

### <span class="fac">[20b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20b.webp)</span>

What is lacking in the one who does not understand the question of which side the letter F is facing, where perhaps a nose would be drawn on it? Or in the one who finds that something is lost with the repeated repetition of the word “bank”; its meaning; & it now becomes a mere sound? _We_ say “At first, there was something like an image.”

### <span class="fac">[20b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20b.webp)</span>

What is lost to the unmusical person? Is it not something similar?

### <span class="fac">[20b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20b.webp)</span>

Is it that he cannot enjoy, judge a sentence as those who understand it can; that the sentence does not live for him (with all that this implies; that the word does not have the aroma of its meaning. That he thus behaves differently towards a word in many cases than we do. – It _could_ be so.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-20b.5+21a.1) <span class="fac">[20b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/20b.webp),[21a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/21a.webp)</span> {#20b521a1}

15.02.1948

The understanding & the explanation of a musical phrase. – The simplest explanation is sometimes a gesture; another would be, for example, a dance step, or words that describe a dance. – But is not the understanding of the phrase an experience while we hear it? & what does the explanation do? Should we think of _it_ while we listen to the music? Should we imagine the dance, or whatever it is? And if we do, – why should one call _that_ a thoughtful listening to the music?? If it is about seeing the dance, then it would be better if _it_ were performed instead of the music. All this, however, is a misunderstanding. I give someone an explanation, tell him “It is as if ‥‥”; now he says “Yes, now I understand it” or “Yes, now I know how to play it.” Above all, he did not have to _accept_ the explanation; it is not as if I had given him, as it were, convincing reasons for the fact that this passage is comparable to that & that. I did not explain to him, for example, from statements of the composer, that this passage is meant to depict this & that.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-21a.2+21b.1) <span class="fac">[21a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/21a.webp),[21b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/21b.webp)</span> {#21a221b1}

If I now ask “What exactly do I experience when I hear this theme & hear it with understanding?” – then nothing but absurdities come to my mind in answer. Something like images, sensations of movement, thoughts, etc.. I say, of course, “I go along with it” – but what does that mean? It _could_ mean something like I accompany the music with gestures. And if one points out that this is usually only the case to a very rudimentary extent, one receives, perhaps, the answer that the rudimentary movements are supplemented by images. But let us assume that someone accompanies the music fully with movements, – in what way is _that_ his understanding? And do I want to say that the movements are the understanding; or his sensations of movement? (What do I know about them?) – It is true that I will, under certain circumstances, regard his movements as a sign of his understanding.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-21b.2) <span class="fac">[21b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/21b.webp)</span> {#21b2}

But should I (if I reject images, sensations of movement, etc. as explanations) say that understanding is simply a specific, non-further-analyzable experience? Well, that would be acceptable if it did not mean: it is a specific _experiential content_. Because with _these_ words one actually thinks of differences such as those between seeing, hearing & smelling.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-21b.3+22a.1) <span class="fac">[21b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/21b.webp),[22a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22a.webp)</span> {#21b322a1}

How does one explain to someone what it means to “understand music”? By naming the images, sensations of movement, etc., that the person who understands has? _Rather_, by showing him the expressive movements of the person who understands. – Yes, the question is also what function does explaining have here? & what does it mean to understand what it means to understand music? Some would say that understanding this means understanding music oneself. And the question would therefore be “Can one teach someone to understand music”, because only such instruction could be called an explanation of music. The understanding of music has a certain _expression_, both during listening & playing, as well as at other times. This expression sometimes includes movements, but sometimes only the way the person who understands plays or hums the piece, and also here & there comparisons that he makes & images that, as it were, illustrate the music. Someone who understands music will listen differently (with a different facial expression, for example), play differently, hum differently, talk about the piece differently than someone who does not understand it. His understanding of a piece will not only be shown in phenomena that accompany the listening or playing of that piece, but also in an understanding of music in general.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-22a.2) <span class="fac">[22a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22a.webp)</span> {#22a2}

Understanding music is a life manifestation of people. How would one describe it to someone? Well, first one would have to describe the _music_. Then one could describe how people behave towards it. But is that all that is necessary, or does it also include teaching him understanding himself? Well, teaching him understanding will, in _another_ sense, teach him what understanding is, than a teaching that does not do this. Yes, even teaching him understanding of poetry or painting can be part of the explanation of what understanding music is.

### <span class="fac">[22a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22a.webp),[22b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22b.webp)</span>

But if I now listen to a melody with understanding, is there not something special happening in me that does not happen when I listen to it without understanding? And _what_? – No answer comes; or what occurs to me is absurd. I can say, “Now I have understood it,” and now perhaps talk about it, play it, compare it with others, etc. _Signs_ of understanding may accompany the listening.

### <span class="fac">[22b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22b.webp)</span>

It is misleading to call understanding a process that accompanies listening. (One could also not call the manifestation of it, the expressive playing, an accompaniment of listening.)

### <span class="fac">[22b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22b.webp)</span>

So I can say, “Now I have understood it for the first time” – but not ‘what it consisted of’; I can say in explanation, “I went along with it,” or that this or that passage ‘made an impression’ on me for the first time.

### <span class="fac">[22b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22b.webp)</span>

Because how can one explain what ‘expressive playing’ is? Certainly not by something that accompanies the playing. What, then, does it include? A culture, one might say. – Whoever has been raised in a certain culture, and thus reacts to music in a certain way, can be taught the use of the word “expressive playing.”

### <span class="fac">[22b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/22b.webp),[23a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23a.webp)</span>

Understanding a theme is neither a sensation nor a sum of sensations. It is correct to call it an experience, in that _this_ concept of understanding has affinities with other concepts of experience. One says, “I experienced this passage quite differently this time.” But this expression only ‘describes’ ‘what happened’ for someone who is familiar with a particular system of concepts. (Analogy: “I won the match.”)

### <span class="fac">[23a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23a.webp)</span>

Can a small child, who is just learning to speak, find that a word loses its meaning if it is repeated again and again?

### <span class="fac">[23a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23a.webp)</span>

February 16, 1948

The child says a word several times in quick succession, and then lets us understand that the word no longer means anything. Do we have reason to assert here that it had the experience of meaning and the loss of meaning? What is missing in the environment of this expression, that could justify this interpretation? – We would only say of him that he has these experiences, who shows a ‘feeling’ for words, for example, who can choose between approximately equivalent words, and much of the like.

### <span class="fac">[23a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23a.webp)</span>

I see the word “bank” written in a certain script (something like what I am used to seeing on checks) and say: “I cannot imagine that it is meant to be a bench.”

### <span class="fac">[23a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23a.webp),[23b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp)</span>

I can, of course, understand under certain circumstances that it has that meaning, but then smile when reading it because it is, so to speak, written contrary to its meaning. _Written_ like that, I say, it always means financial institution to me.

### <span class="fac">[23b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp)</span>

When reading, _that_ floats before me. So something happens when reading? This question does not lead anywhere.

### <span class="fac">[23b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp)</span>

How can that float before me? Not in the dimensions you are thinking of.

### <span class="fac">[23b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp)</span>

“When I read it, it meant to me ‥‥” – The question is: what does the expression of simultaneity mean? Compare: “_This_ word was on the tip of my tongue.”

### <span class="fac">[23b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp)</span>

Certain things about seeing seem puzzling to us because seeing as a whole does not seem puzzling enough to us.

### <span class="fac">[23b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp)</span>

That someone sees a clearly drawn cube spatially, we all know. He may not even be able to describe what he sees other than spatially. And that someone could _just_ see the picture _that_ way is clear. If he now looks at the picture alternately, sometimes this way, sometimes that way, he has the experience of a change of aspect. What, then, is _in_ that incomprehensible? – Is it this: that the report “I am now seeing ‥‥” is no longer a report about the perceived object. Because earlier, “I see a cube in this picture” was the report about the object that I am looking at.

### <span class="fac">[23b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/23b.webp),[24a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24a.webp)</span>

The incomprehensible thing is, after all, that _nothing_ has changed, & yet _everything_ has changed. For that is the only way one can express it; not _like this_: it has not changed in _one_ sense, but it has in another. There would be nothing strange about that. But “nothing has changed” means: I have no right to change my report about what I have seen, I still see the same thing – but I am, in an inexplicable way, compelled to alternate between reporting completely different things.

### <span class="fac">[24a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24a.webp)</span>

And it is not _so_: I see the picture simply as one of the infinitely many objects, of which it is the projection; – but _only_ as _this_ – or as _this_. The picture _is_ therefore alternately the one & the other.

### <span class="fac">[24a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24a.webp)</span>

Now we have a language-game that is in a curious way _the same_, & in a curious way _different_ from the earlier one. The consequences of the expression “I am now seeing …” are now quite different; although there is still a close kinship between the language-games.

### <span class="fac">[24a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24a.webp)</span>

That the eye (the point in our picture) looks in one direction would not have surprised us at all, – until it had _changed_ its direction of gaze.

### <span class="fac">[24a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24a.webp)</span>

19.02.1948
