The question arises: Could we imagine people who never see anything _as_ anything? Would something essential be lacking in them, similar to how they might be colorblind or without perfect pitch? Let us call these people “shape-blind” or “aspect-blind”.

### <span class="fac">[24b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24b.webp)</span>

It will depend on what _kind_ of aspect he is blind to. Should I, for example, assume that he cannot even _see_ the cube scheme in space in this way, in that way? If it is _so_, then I must consistently assume that he cannot see the image of a cube as a cube, i.e., he cannot see the image of a spatial object as such. He would therefore have a different attitude towards images than we do. It could be the one we have towards a blueprint. He would, for example, be able to work from a pictorial _representation_. – But here is the difficulty, that he should never consider an image to be a spatial object, as we sometimes do with painted architecture. And _this_ could not be called ‘blindness’ in a good sense, rather the opposite. (This investigation is not psychological.)

### <span class="fac">[24b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24b.webp)</span>

One can, of course, imagine that someone never sees an _aspect-change_; in that the spatial aspect of every picture remains stable for him. But this assumption does not _interest_ us. [→ ]

### <span class="fac">[24b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24b.webp)</span>

But it is conceivable, and also interesting, that people might have a completely different relationship to pictures than we do.

### <span class="fac">[24b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/24b.webp),[25a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25a.webp)</span>

So we could imagine someone who sees only a painted face as a face, but not one consisting of a circle and four dots. Who therefore does not see the rabbit-duck picture as a picture of an animal’s head and thus does not see the aspect-change that we know.

### <span class="fac">[25a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25a.webp)</span>

20.02.1948

Someone is supposed to be unable to see the picture of a runner as a picture of movement: How would that show itself? I assume that he has _learned_ that such a picture represents a runner. So he can say that it is a runner; how will he then differ from normal people? He will not show any understanding for the representation of movement in a picture – I will assume. And what would we call the signs of this lack of understanding? – That can easily be imagined. (If such a person could now copy every picture very accurately, we would certainly not say that his visual sense is deficient.)

### <span class="fac">[25a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25a.webp)</span>

03.03.1948

I can regard every corner of a triangle as its apex: what is wrong with someone who cannot _see_ this?

### <span class="fac">[25a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25a.webp)</span>

Seeing the triangle _like this_ and _like that_ is – I would like to say – a remnant of the school instruction that has drilled certain ways of looking at things into us. Perhaps it is so; but that is just a historical remark. We are interested in the experiences, whatever is responsible for them.

### <span class="fac">[25a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25a.webp),[25b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25b.webp)</span>

It is clear that the pupil, who has only just become acquainted with the concept of ‘apex,’ ‘base,’ etc., that words like “I am now seeing _this_ as the apex – now _this_” will mean nothing to him. But that is not what I meant as an empirical proposition.

### <span class="fac">[25b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25b.webp)</span>

Only of someone who is _able_ to make all sorts of applications of the figure with ease would one say that he sees it _like this_, _like that_.

### <span class="fac">[25b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25b.webp)</span>

How strange, however, that this should be the condition for him to have _experienced_ this and that! You do not say that only someone who is able to do this and that has toothache. Hence (it follows) that we are dealing with very different concepts of experience. The concept of experience is different each time, even if it is related.

### <span class="fac">[25b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25b.webp)</span>

We speak, make utterances, and only _later_ do we obtain a picture of their life.

### <span class="fac">[25b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/25b.webp),[26a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26a.webp)</span>

But one could imagine this way of teaching the pupil that kind of seeing: One draws a second triangle next to the one, which is not yet turned over. Later

![](../graphics/137,25b.svg)

this is left out and he can now see the triangle as turned over. – But must he then understand this illustration, or rather see it correctly? – It could be that it only confuses him further. To someone to whom this illustration says nothing, other pictures will not speak as they do to us, he will not react to them as we do. (Not in an experiential way.) Analogy with the picture of the running horse.

### <span class="fac">[26a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26a.webp)</span>

It is far from self-evident that we see ‘spatially’ with two eyes. If the two visual images merge into one, one could expect as a result a blurred image, analogous to a blurred photograph.

### <span class="fac">[26a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26a.webp)</span>

A secret language that I agree on with someone, in which “bank” means apple: Immediately after the agreement, I say to him “Get rid of these banks!” – He understands me and does it; but the word “bank” still seems strange to him in this use, and he may have the idea of a bank in his mind.

### <span class="fac">[26a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26a.webp)</span>

What would one say about someone who cannot even see the cube diagram as a box, sometimes standing, sometimes lying down? If this is a defect, is it not more a defect of the imagination than of visual perception?

### <span class="fac">[26a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26a.webp),[26b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26b.webp)</span>

It is just as puzzling that someone can see a drawing spatially as it is that he can see it, for example, as something lying down or something standing. That is, it is just as puzzling that the body in the seen picture corresponds to it as a description, as that it corresponds to the standing one and not to the lying one. – And if viewed differently, one is no more puzzling than the other.

### <span class="fac">[26b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26b.webp)</span>

But what a remarkable method! – I form a concept and ask myself how it could be consistently carried out. What does its consistent implementation mean to us?

### <span class="fac">[26b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26b.webp)</span>

We see a painting spatially; it would not be easy for us to describe it as an aggregate of flat patches of color, but what we see in the stereoscope looks even more spatial. Someone looking at a photograph of people, houses, trees, etc., does not seem to lack spatiality!

### <span class="fac">[26b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26b.webp)</span>

I can see the cube diagram as a box, but _not_: sometimes as a paper box, sometimes as a metal box. – What would I say if someone assured me that he could see the drawing as a **metal** box? Should I say that this is not seeing? But, if not seeing, could he then _perceive_ it? It would naturally be plausible to answer: only what could actually be seen in reality can be imagined in this _visual_ way. (Knowledge in dreams)

### <span class="fac">[26b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/26b.webp),[27a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27a.webp)</span>

And yet, it could be that someone who, for example, has dealt intensively with certain materials, let's say types of glass, could ‘see’ a cube drawing once as a cube made of one material, and once as one made of the other, i.e., he might say that now he sees it this way, now that way – although he could not give a visual criterion for it & simply would not know why it alternately appears _this_ way & _that_ way to him. He might also believe that he feels the changed interpretation in his chest.

### <span class="fac">[27a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27a.webp)</span>

The experience one has when stepping out of the cinema onto the street, seeing the street and people as if they were on a screen and part of a film action. What is the reason for this? _How_ does one see the street and the people? I could only say: I have, for example, the fleeting thought, “Perhaps _this_ man will be a main character in the play.” But that alone is not it. My attitude is somehow like the one towards the events in the film; something like a mild curiosity, a pleasure. – But I cannot really say all of this at first.

### <span class="fac">[27a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27a.webp)</span>

Is it not also fantasy to hear something as a variation of a certain theme? And yet, one perceives something through it.

### <span class="fac">[27a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27a.webp)</span>

“Imagine it changed in this way, and you will have the other.” In general, one might say that the powers of imagination can replace an image, a demonstration.

### <span class="fac">[27a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27a.webp),[27b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27b.webp)</span>

March 4, 1948

One can simply express the aspects of the double cross by pointing once to a white cross & once to a black cross. Thus, the same applies to the question of whether “is _this_ contained in the figure on this paper?” – One could ask the same question with regard to the rabbit-duck image. However, it is also clear that in each case, it differs somewhat from the other. For example, in order to express the aspects of this image, one points to something that is not contained in the image, such as the black cross in the double cross.

### <span class="fac">[27b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27b.webp)</span>

March 7, 1948

You are talking about _understanding_ music. You _understand_ it while you are listening to it! Should we say that it is an experience that accompanies the listening?

### <span class="fac">[27b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27b.webp)</span>

I give signs of delight and understanding. Is it mere quibbling to say that joy, pleasure, delight are not sensations? – Let us ask ourselves: how much analogy exists between delight and what we call “sense impressions”?

### <span class="fac">[27b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/27b.webp)</span>

The link between them would be pain. Because its concept is similar to that of tactile sensation, e.g. (through the characteristics of localization, duration, intensity, quality) and at the same time to that of emotional states through the characteristic of expression (facial expressions, gestures, sounds).

### <span class="fac">[28a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/28a.webp)</span>

How do I know that someone is delighted? How does one learn the verbal expression of delight? What does it attach to? To the expression of bodily sensations? If we ask someone what he feels in his chest, in the facial muscles, to find out whether he experiences pleasure?

### <span class="fac">[28a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/28a.webp),[28b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/28b.webp)</span>

Does that mean there aren't sensations that often recur when enjoying music? Not at all. (In some passages, it might make him cry, & he feels it in his throat.)

A poem makes an impression on us when we read it. “Do you feel the same thing while reading it as you would if you were reading something indifferent?” – How have I learned to answer this question? I might say: “_Naturally_ not!” – which is to say: _this_ affects me, & the other does not. “I experience something different.” – And what is the nature of this? – I cannot give a satisfactory answer. Because what I state is not something important. – “But didn't you enjoy it _while_ reading?” Of course – – because the opposite answer would mean that I enjoyed it earlier or later; & that is not what I want to say. But now you do remember certain sensations & images & thoughts while reading, namely those that were not irrelevant to the enjoyment, to the impression. – But I would say that they only gained their importance through their context: through reading the poem, through my _knowledge_ of the language, the meter, & countless other things. (These eyes only smile in _this_ face & in _this_ temporal context.) You must ask yourself: How did we learn the expression “Isn't that wonderful!” (for example)? – No one explained it to us by referring to sensations, images, or thoughts that accompany listening. Yes, we would not doubt that he enjoyed it if he could not state such experiences; rather, if it became clear that he did not understand certain connections.

### <span class="fac">[28b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/28b.webp)</span>

But doesn't understanding also show, for example, in the expression with which one reads the poem, sings the melody? Certainly. But what is the experience during the reading? One would have to say: the one who enjoys & understands it, hears it well or feels it in the vocal organs.

### <span class="fac">[28b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/28b.webp)</span>

One can also say that understanding a musical phrase is the same as understanding a _language_.

### <span class="fac">[29a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29a.webp)</span>

I am thinking of a very short piece, only two measures long. You say, “There’s so much contained within it!” But it is only, as it were, an optical illusion if you think that what is contained within it comes to the fore when one listens. (Just remember that we sometimes say, and quite rightly: “It depends on _who_ is saying it.”) (Only in the flow of thoughts & life do words have meaning.)

### <span class="fac">[29a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29a.webp)</span>

“Optical illusion.” – So, what is it that deceives me? Is it an illusion when I exclaim, “Now I have understood this passage for the first time”? Or when, in answer to the question “When did you understand it?”, I reply, “While it was being played”? And I will often have had a new thought at that moment, a comparison occurred to me, I made a particular gesture. (The question arises here: Do I experience such a gesture through a feeling of movement or the sight of the movement – or do I simply _know_ about it?) The illusion can only lie in what I say in a conceptual discussion about it.

### <span class="fac">[29a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29a.webp)</span>

The deception does not lie in _that_: “_Now_ I understand it.” – & now perhaps follows a long explanation of what I have understood.

### <span class="fac">[29a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29a.webp),[29b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29b.webp)</span>

March 8, 1948

The optical illusion occurs when philosophizing. Then, when one wants to explain afterwards what must have happened when one understood it. When one tries to find an experiential content that was the understanding.

### <span class="fac">[29b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29b.webp)</span>

Optical illusion only occurs when one _looks_ for something. But what is one looking for? (If everything is clear, if our concepts do not deceive us, then one does not need to look.)

### <span class="fac">[29b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29b.webp)</span>

When we analyze a phenomenon, we never do so in order to replace what has not yet been analyzed with what has been analyzed. At most, in order to compare it with it.

### <span class="fac">[29b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/29b.webp),[30a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30a.webp)</span>

How is the seeing of an aspect related to the ability to operate (e.g., in mathematics)? Think of spatial seeing in descriptive geometry & operating in drawing. One moves the pen on the surface as if one were moving in the actual body. But how can that be a proof of _seeing_? Surely, isn't it also a proof of seeing when one moves around the room with certainty? There are simply different criteria for seeing. Ask yourself: Must one be able to draw animals, people, & all sorts of objects well, based on the image or memory, or see them before the inner eye? The answer could be: “In such a case, we simply _say_ – – – – – –” – but also: “One must ask the drawer whether he does it, or not.”

### <span class="fac">[30a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30a.webp)</span>

There is a connection between aspect & fantasy.

### <span class="fac">[30a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30a.webp)</span>

The aspects of the jacket & the floor. What is missing for the one who is blind to them? – It is not nonsensical to answer: imagination.

### <span class="fac">[30a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30a.webp)</span>

I have a concept of cloaks & base areas that I couldn’t explain in a simple way. And if one expresses oneself _in this way_: one considers the such & such elements as ‘belonging together’ – then one wonders what all this word entails! In what situations, & how, is it used.

### <span class="fac">[30a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30a.webp),[30b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30b.webp)</span>

Bear in mind that for an aspect, there is often a ‘fitting word’. If, for example, one is shown the double cross & asked to report which of the _two_ aspects (black cross or white cross) one sees, it may not matter to us whether one says one time one sees a white windmill with four blades, the other time a standing black cross, whether one sees the white cross as four points of a piece of paper folded towards the center. The cross which is ‘now seen’ can also be seen as a cross-shaped _opening_. But _these_ differences should not matter to us, & one could therefore make a distinction between ‘purely optical’ & ‘conceptual’ aspects. [Similarly, in the telling of a dream, it may or may not matter what particular words are used to describe the dream situations.]

### <span class="fac">[30b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30b.webp)</span>

One could not understand “See <math display="inline"><mi>F</mi></math> as

![](../graphics/230c,17.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">”,</span>

unless something else is said first. For would I understand “See

![](../graphics/137,30b-1.svg)

as

![](../graphics/137,30b-2.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">”?</span>

There must first be a mental connection.

### <span class="fac">[30b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30b.webp)</span>

For example, one suddenly pays attention to the viewing direction of the

![](../graphics/137,30b-4.svg).

This is connected with all sorts of comparisons. (Of course, not the

![](../graphics/137,30b-4.svg)

with itself.)

### <span class="fac">[30b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30b.webp)</span>

“It looks to me now as if it is pointing to the left – and now again to the right.” So, just as before? No; earlier, it did not have any _direction_ for me. I did not surround it with this world of images earlier.

### <span class="fac">[30b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30b.webp)</span>

Attention is dynamic, not static – one might say. I compare paying attention first with staring, gazing: but that is not what I mean by attention; & I now want to say that I think one _cannot_ pay attention in a static way.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-30b.6+31a.1) <span class="fac">[30b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/30b.webp),[31a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31a.webp)</span> {#30b631a1}

Our children learn in school that water _consists_ of the gases hydrogen and oxygen, or sugar of carbon, hydrogen, and oxygen. Whoever does not understand this is stupid. The most important questions are covered up.

### <span class="fac">[31a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31a.webp)</span>

One might exclaim, upon seeing a rock, “A man!” and then perhaps show the other person how he sees the man in the rock – where the face is, where the feet are, etc. (Another person might see a man in it in a different way.) One would say that imagination is required for this. But not to recognize a realistic picture of a dog as such.

### <span class="fac">[31a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31a.webp)</span>

“He compares the rock with a human figure,” “He sees a human figure in it” – but not in the same sense: he compares this image with a dog, or this passport photo with a face.

### <span class="fac">[31a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31a.webp)</span>

When I look at the photograph, I do not say to myself, “One could see this as a human being.” Nor, when I look at the F: “One could see this as an F.”

### <span class="fac">[31a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31a.webp)</span>

Whoever showed me the _figure_ & asked me “What is that?” could only receive an answer from me in this way. Nor in this way: “I consider this to be a…”, or “It is probably a…”. No more than when reading a book do I _consider_ the letters to be this or that.

### <span class="fac">[31a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31a.webp),[31b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp)</span>

“I see it as a ‥‥” goes together with “I am trying to see it as ‥‥,” or “I cannot yet see it as a ‥‥.” But you cannot try to see the ordinary F as this.

### <span class="fac">[31b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp)</span>

One can only see an aspect as the result of a change (I want to say). But this must not, of course, be an empirical proposition.

### <span class="fac">[31b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp)</span>

Asking someone in one’s mind for advice. [relating to: estimating time by imagining a clock.]

### <span class="fac">[31b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp)</span>

In the aspect, there is a physiognomy present that later disappears. It is almost as if there is a face that I first _imitate_ and then take in, without imitating it. – And is this not actually enough (of an) explanation? – But is it not too much?

### <span class="fac">[31b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp)</span>

If I say in a certain case that attention consists in the willingness to follow every small movement that may show itself, then you can already see that attention is not the rigid gazing, but a concept of a different kind.

### <span class="fac">[31b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp)</span>

“To see something in accordance with a conception” does not mean the same as “to see something in accordance with the _transition_ to a conception.”

### <span class="fac">[31b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/31b.webp),[32a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32a.webp)</span>

If I see the figure

![](../graphics/180a,25r-1.svg)

as a mirror-image F, then this could be represented by a certain characteristic way of producing the figure; likewise, if I see it as an F and its reflection side by side, and now there would also be a certain way of producing the letter F. As for the copying of what I see, the command “Copy this as a <math display="inline"><mi>F</mi></math>” originally does not make sense, because there is no particular way of copying an F. But if there is that, as it were, mimetic expression of the aspect, then the command makes sense. – But, of course, the concept of copying is now transformed; for the copy in the earlier sense, the result, is always the same; and the copy in the new sense is an action, that is, as it were, the imitation of an _action_.

### <span class="fac">[32a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32a.webp)</span>

I feel I could represent the aspect _mimetically_.

### <span class="fac">[32a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32a.webp)</span>

It is not the aspect-change that one sees, but the change of interpretation.

### <span class="fac">[32a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32a.webp)</span>

You do not see it in accordance with an interpretation, but in accordance with interpreting.

### <span class="fac">[32a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32a.webp)</span>

If one asked, “Can you see

![](../graphics/137,32a.svg)

as an ef?”, then that person would not understand us. But the question “Can you see it as a mirror-image

![](../graphics/137,32a.svg)?” he would understand. And also the question: “And can you now see it again as an ordinary ef? – Why?” “Can you see it as ‥‥?”, or “See it now as a ‥‥!”, goes together with: “Take it now as a ‥‥.” Only where this command makes sense does that question make sense.

### <span class="fac">[32a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32a.webp),[32b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

10.03.1948

It is as if something were seeing, but at the same time _thinking_, so that it could only last as long as a certain thought; and if one lets that go, the seeing also stopped. (Not meant causally.)

### <span class="fac">[32b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

Think, someone said, pointing to an ordinary printed F, “Now it is an ef.” – What does that mean? Does it make sense? It does not yet make sense. In what sense is it _now_ this? Perhaps in so far as it always is this? And in contrast to what? – I look at a lamp and say “Now it is a lamp” – what can I mean?

### <span class="fac">[32b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

You need a new conceptual pair of glasses.

### <span class="fac">[32b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

If someone says, “Now it is a face for me,” then one can ask him: “What kind of transformation are you alluding to?”

### <span class="fac">[32b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

The exclamation “A hare!” is related to the _statement_ “A hare.”

### <span class="fac">[32b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

What is the expression of astonishment? Can it be an immobile posture? Can astonishment therefore be a stationary state?

### <span class="fac">[32b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

Imagine someone asked: “Why is the experience of surprise not to be captured?”

### <span class="fac">[32b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/32b.webp)</span>

“The ef disappears & there is a cross; the cross disappears & there is a mirror-F; etc.” This is, after all, the expression of the change in perception.

### <span class="fac">[33a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp)</span>

_Forget_, forget that you yourself have had these experiences!

### <span class="fac">[33a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp)</span>

It is as if our eye drew a different figure each time in these strokes (on the paper).

### <span class="fac">[33a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp)</span>

What I see in the two cases is as different as possible. And what do you see? In one case, an ef, written in _this_ way: ‥‥; in the other, an ix _like_ this: ‥‥‥ And what is an ef & an ix? – One will not point to the ambiguous drawing. Rather, one might point to the paradigms ‥‥‥ . But must I not consider that F & X are not only forms, but also concepts for me?

### <span class="fac">[33a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp)</span>

“For me, it is no longer a cross; I do not write it ‥‥‥ For me, it is an F, I write it ‥‥‥”

### <span class="fac">[33a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp)</span>

Different pictures appear to me. But _how_ different? In what way different? I can only explain this through a genesis.

### <span class="fac">[33a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp)</span>

I say something; & it is correct; – but now I misunderstand the use to which this statement belongs.

### <span class="fac">[33a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33a.webp),[33b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33b.webp)</span>

How does one play the game “It could also be _that_”? _That_, which the figure could also be – & that is what it can be seen as – is not simply another figure. It did not make sense to say:

![](../graphics/180a,25r-1.svg)

could also be a

![](../graphics/230c,17.svg)

. Or also: – this could mean very different things. But one could probably play this game with a child. Together, one looks at a figure; or any object; & now it is said: “This is now a house” – & now it is reported and talked about, and one imagines it as if it were a house, and it is interpreted entirely as this. Then something else is imagined, a different fiction is woven around it.

### <span class="fac">[33b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33b.webp)</span>

How will you know if the child sees the thing as _that_? Well, perhaps it will say this spontaneously. For example, it says: “Yes, _now_ I see it as ‥‥”. And in _this_ situation, with the lively participation in the invention, it will certainly mean for us the seeing of the aspect.

### <span class="fac">[33b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/33b.webp),[34a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

I want to say that this game is _akin_ to the game of seeing the aspects of

![](../graphics/180a,25r-1.svg)

e.g. It is a condition for us that one can, as it were, play theater with the things, for that to be what we mean when we say with the words “Now I see it as ‥‥”.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

How does one teach a child, for example in arithmetic, “Now add these points together!” or “Now these belong together”? Apparently, “adding together” & “belonging together” must originally have had a different _meaning_ for him than simply to _see_ them in that way. – This is a remark about concepts, not about methods of instruction.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

Only someone who has learned & mastered something _can_ say that they have _experienced_ something; it makes _sense_ to say so.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

One can certainly say “See the figure now for 5 minutes as a ‥‥”, if this means: Maintain, balance it in this aspect.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

It must be in an imaginative atmosphere in order to be seen as something at all.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

What do you understand when someone says to you “I see it (namely the ordinary <math display="inline"><mi>F</mi></math>) as an ef”? – That he is dealing with aspects; that it is a labile state. That he thinks ‘it could also be that’.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp)</span>

The seeing of aspects is based on other games.

### <span class="fac">[34a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34a.webp),[34b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span>

“It is now the thing for me” – but why do you say it will be seen differently? What similarity is there between this experience & the experience of seeing? Now, why do we call imagining seeing? We certainly don’t do it for a _reason_ – but it may have a justification.

### <span class="fac">[34b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span>

“I can’t help but call it ‘seeing’.” Well; but is that _all_?

### <span class="fac">[34b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span>

You say you see different pictures. And that is indeed true, for an ‘e’ is something different from a cross. – But in the most important sense, it is the same picture: you copy it in the same way each time; it is only _described_ differently.

### <span class="fac">[34b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span>

One speaks of arithmetic in the imagination. So there is nothing surprising in the fact that the imagination can serve knowledge.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-34b.5) <span class="fac">[34b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span> {#34b5}

The beauty of a star figure – a six-pointed star, for example – is diminished if you see it symmetrically with respect to a certain axis.

### <span class="fac">[34b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span>

But I do not want to say that the aspect is an imagination. But that ‘seeing an aspect’ & ‘imagining something’ are related concepts.

### <span class="fac">[34b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp)</span>

One would like to ask, with regard to seeing aspects: “Is it seeing? Is it thinking?” The aspect is subject to the will: this alone makes it related to thinking.

### <span class="fac">[34b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/34b.webp),[35a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35a.webp)</span>

“The aspect is subject to the will” is not an empirical statement. It makes sense to say “See this circle as a hole, not as a disc”; but not “See it as a square”, or “See it as red”.

### <span class="fac">[35a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35a.webp)</span>

15.03.1948

[→ ] The figure

![](../graphics/233a,43.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">,</span>

the written <math display="inline"><mi>F</mi></math> once as F, once as the mirror image of an F. I want to call an F reversed in this way a mirror-F. (It could be that people would be indifferent to the position of their letters & that both a letter and its mirror image would be the same letter for them. This could be explained by the fact that their signs were not originally produced by writing, but were formed as pieces of some material, and then laid down in rows to form sentences.)

### <span class="fac">[35a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35a.webp)</span>

Do I really see something different each time, or do I only interpret what I see in different ways? I am inclined to say the first. But why? – Interpreting is thinking, doing; it can, for example, proceed in this way; one says “This is a F”, or the typesetter sets it as an F, or one thinks “What could this be? – It will be an F” etc. – _Seeing_, on the other hand, is not doing.

### <span class="fac">[35a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35a.webp),[35b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35b.webp)</span>

The cases in which we _interpret_ what we see are easy to recognize. When we interpret, we make a hypothesis that may prove to be false. “I see this figure as a…” can be verified as little as (or only in the sense of) the statement “I see a bright red.” Thus, there is a similarity in the uses of the word “to see” in both contexts.

### <span class="fac">[35b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35b.webp)</span>

Let us suppose someone asked: “Do we all see an F in the same way?” What could be meant by this? We could try the following experiment: we show different people an F and ask the question “Which way is it facing, to the right or to the left?” Or: “If you were to draw an eye and a nose on an F and a J, which way would it be facing?” However, some people might not understand these questions. Just as some people might not understand the question “What color is the sound _a_?” If someone didn’t understand it, or explained that it was nonsense, could we say that he doesn’t understand German, or the meanings of the words “color,” “sound,” etc.? On the contrary: if he has learned these words, then he can react to these questions with ‘understanding’ or ‘without understanding’.

### <span class="fac">[35b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/35b.webp),[36a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/36a.webp)</span>

Don’t think the question was posed as “In what direction does the letter … look?” – but rather (only) as: “If you were to draw an eye & a nose on an F & a J, – in what direction would it look?” This, too, would be a psychological question. And it does not speak of ‘seeing’ it one way or another. Instead, it speaks of a _tendency_ to do one thing or another. – <sup>(2)</sup> Thus, that kind of _seeing_ is related to a tendency. (<sup>(1)</sup> But it must be considered how he arrived at the answer “I would draw the nose _there_.”) <sup>(3)</sup> The tendency can change, or be completely absent.

### <span class="fac">[36a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/36a.webp)</span>

“With this arrangement of windows, the façade is facing _that_ way.” “The windows used to be arranged in such a way that the façade was facing _that_ way.” The first sentence is similar to a geometrical one. In the second, the concept of ‘the direction in which it is facing’ serves to describe the façade. Just as one describes a face using the terms ‘cheerful,’ ‘grumpy,’ ‘suspicious,’ or a movement with ‘fearful,’ ‘hesitant,’ ‘certain.’ And in so far as these are descriptions of the visually perceived, of the observed (one can tell someone who is copying a picture, “The face is not quite right, it is not sad enough”), they are also descriptions of the visual impression. One can therefore say: one _sees_ the hesitation.

### <span class="fac">[36a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/36a.webp),[36b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/36b.webp)</span>

March 24, 1948

Two uses of the statement “I see ‥‥‥.” _One_ language-game (a): “What do you see there?” – “I see ‥‥‥” – and then follows a description of what is seen in words, by a drawing, a model, and so on. Another language-game (b): “I see a similarity in these two faces.” In language-game (a), the description could, for example, be: “I see two faces that look like father and son.” One can call this a less complete description than one provided by an accurate drawing of the faces. However, it is conceivable that someone could give this more accurate description without noticing the similarity of the faces. Another person could see the drawing of the first person and recognize the family resemblance in it, or even a similarity in facial expression.

### <span class="fac">[36b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/36b.webp)</span>

One can have ‘an eye’ or ‘a knack’ for certain similarities, or acquire it through practice. If someone does not have the concept of hesitation, but observes a hesitant movement and can accurately reproduce it, should I say that he does not _see_ the movement as accurately as the other, who recognizes it as a _hesitant_ one? Certainly not: but rather that the first person does not have an eye for this particular quality of a movement.

### <span class="fac">[36b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/36b.webp),[37a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37a.webp)</span>

And whoever has an eye for family resemblances could recognize that two people are related, even without being able to say in what the similarity consists. (Think of the case of the calculating genius.)

### <span class="fac">[37a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37a.webp)</span>

Does the person who sees fear in a face see _more_ than the person who could accurately portray the face, but cannot recognize fear in it? The question is actually the same as: is recognizing fear in a face called ‘seeing’? It might be linguistically incorrect to say “I see fear in this face.” It would teach us that one can ‘_see_’ a fearful face; the fear in it, the similarity or difference of two faces, one ‘_notices_’.

### <span class="fac">[37a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37a.webp)</span>

And should one call this distinction merely a whim of language? The relatedness of the two concepts is evident in this explanation; to recognize their difference, consider what sense it might make to say that someone saw the similarity of two faces from one clock strike to the next.

Or think of the command: Pay attention to the similarity of ‥‥ to ‥‥.

### <span class="fac">[37a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37a.webp),[37b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37b.webp)</span>

The description of the visual impression can be a drawing. But it could also be specified at what distance from the eye we should hold it; yes, even which point of the drawing we should look at, or how our gaze should wander on it. What is above and what is below in the drawing is usually of the greatest importance.

### <span class="fac">[37b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37b.webp)</span>

I begin to see the similarity when it ‘strikes’ me; and do I then see it as long as I see the similar objects? Or only as long as I am _conscious_ of the similarity? If the similarity strikes me, then I notice something, but I do not need to remain conscious of it in order to observe whether it changes.

### <span class="fac">[37b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37b.webp)</span>

So far I can see, and no further.

### <span class="fac">[37b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/37b.webp),[38a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38a.webp)</span>

March 25, 1948

Two uses of the statement “I see ‥‥.” One language-game: “What do you see there?” – “I see ‥‥” & this is followed by a description of what is seen in words, through a drawing, a model, gestures, etc. – Another language-game: We are looking at two faces, & I say to the other: “I see a similarity in them.” In the first language-game, the description could, for example, be: “I see two faces that are similar to each other, like father & son.” – One could call this a much less complete description than one produced by a drawing. But one person could give this more complete description & still not notice that similarity. Another person could see the first person's drawing & discover the family resemblance in it; & in the same way, also a similarity in facial expression.

### <span class="fac">[38a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38a.webp)</span>

“When I spoke the word now, it meant to me ‥‥.” Why shouldn’t that simply be madness? Because _I_ experienced it? That is not a reason.

### <span class="fac">[38a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38a.webp)</span>

May 28, 1948

“I can never know what is going on in him.” – But why should you say that anything is going on in him? – And are you _always_ uncertain? And if you are _not_ uncertain, – do you know what is going on in him?

### <span class="fac">[38a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38a.webp)</span>

We fear _pretending_; – although pretending is not the only case in which the exterior deceives.

### <span class="fac">[38a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38a.webp)</span>

People from foreign cultures often seem wrong to us, simply because we do not understand them. Someone who smiles where we look grim does not necessarily have to be pretending.

### <span class="fac">[38a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38a.webp),[38b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp)</span>

When I talk to my friend, I don't keep telling myself that I don't know what's going on in him; & it is not thoughtlessness that I don't tell myself. In general, there is no assumption about processes in his mind. And yet I do not consider him a machine.

### <span class="fac">[38b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp)</span>

These are quite _special_ cases in which the inner seems hidden to me.

### <span class="fac">[38b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp)</span>

It is as if I only become aware that the inner (actually) is always hidden.

### <span class="fac">[38b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp)</span>

(One also says: The person is completely transparent to me.) So, sometimes a person is transparent to me, sometimes opaque.

### <span class="fac">[38b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp)</span>

And the uncertainty that expresses itself in this way is not a philosophical one, but a practical & primitive one.

### <span class="fac">[38b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp)</span>

One says “I don't know how I stand with him.” – If we were used to a very regular climate with easily predictable changes & came into an unpredictable climate, & we personified the weather, – could we not say that we cannot see into the soul of the weather god? But someone for whom the processes in the other person's soul seem hidden is uncertain about something present, not about something future. Their uncertainty relates to the present in the same sense in which the other person's statement “I thought back then ‥‥” relates to the past.

### <span class="fac">[38b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/38b.webp),[39a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp)</span>

“I don't know how I stand with the weather here: it sometimes seems to smile, but then immediately turns nasty.” – But the weather does not say: “When I smiled back then, I was planning to hail.” – But does it depend on the fact that a person _says_ something like that? And if he smiles & is already thinking of something bad, – what is _important_ about that? (Why should that – whatever it is – not be quite unimportant? As unimportant as one or another chemical reaction in his body?)

### <span class="fac">[39a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp)</span>

What is the importance of one making such a confession – does he have to be able to correctly assess his state? – It is not about an inner state that he assesses, but precisely about his confession.

### <span class="fac">[39a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp)</span>

His confession can explain certain things. It can, for example, distract from my suspicion of another.

### <span class="fac">[39a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp)</span>

“I can never know what is going on in him.” – But must something be going on in him? – And why should I care about that? – It is, however, a real, not dreamt-up, uncertainty that this picture suggests.

### <span class="fac">[39a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp)</span>

The uncertainty has something like the following expression: “How will he talk about me to his friends when I turn my back on him?” This uncertainty does not always exist.

### <span class="fac">[39a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp)</span>

There is a _fundamental_ uncertainty. What does it consist of?

### <span class="fac">[39a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39a.webp),[39b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39b.webp)</span>

For example, that one cannot say: If someone behaves _like this_, then he is happy & it _cannot_ be pretending. And with what natural facts is this conceptual indeterminacy connected? – It is _not_ equivalent to a continuous uncertainty about what the other person is thinking & feeling. However, it is related to the great variety of cases.

### <span class="fac">[39b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/39b.webp),[40a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

Could one now imagine that people have a more specific concept for a similar purpose? & under what circumstances would it be practical? – One could say: everything would have to be much simpler than with us. Is it not strange that I cannot describe more precisely how such people would have to behave? – Would it make sense to talk about ‘outside’ & ‘inside’ with them? – The certainty would express itself in the fact that sometimes a _certain_ conclusion could be drawn from the outside to the inside. And would this be an empirical proposition? – But what about the _first person_? Can I say: “If I behave _like this_, it cannot be pretending”? And is that supposed to be an empirical proposition? – What would the case look like in which I _believe_ that I am pretending, but I am not. I _say_ later, for example, that I only pretended to have terrible pain, but I felt nothing. However, changes are found in my organs that would actually justify the pain, & the later symptoms also correspond to it. One could then say in certain cases: “If a person behaves _like this_, he is not pretending, even if he would honestly confess it later.

### <span class="fac">[40a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

It is remarkable that this consideration is so tedious. I feel that it should be possible to shorten it considerably through a clear insight. It dwells far too much on details. A different method of calculation would quickly resolve it. It lacks the general, decisive method (Cartesian coordinates).

### <span class="fac">[40a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

If I ask: “Could a concept that differs from ours in this or that respect serve a similar purpose?” – then perhaps it would have to be shown that the conditional change would be much greater than I had expected, so that it might hardly be possible to speak of a related purpose.

### <span class="fac">[40a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

An important fact is: I can understand people more or less well. (Differences in upbringing & culture.)

### <span class="fac">[40a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

If I don't understand them, then I would say that I don't know what is going on in their inner being.

### <span class="fac">[40a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

This person smiles kindly, but then doesn't behave like I, or the people in my circle, would if we had smiled like that.

### <span class="fac">[40a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp)</span>

A person is ‘enigmatic’ to me.

### <span class="fac">[40a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40a.webp),[40b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40b.webp)</span>

Do I also say: I don't know what's going on in the sparrow, in the cat, in the wolf?

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-40b.2) <span class="fac">[40b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40b.webp)</span> {#40b2}

Bach said that he achieved everything only through diligence. But such diligence presupposes humility & immense resilience, i.e., strength. And when someone can then express himself perfectly, he speaks to us in the language of a great person.

### <span class="fac">[40b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40b.webp)</span>

“What is going on behind his forehead?” one says, and means it literally, but doesn't mean any physiological process, and also doesn't believe that the psychological process is going on behind the forehead.

### <span class="fac">[40b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40b.webp)</span>

The fundamental uncertainty: I don’t know what he’s thinking if he doesn’t express it. But imagine he does express it, but in a language that you don’t understand. He could tap the finger of one hand on the back of the other, in Morse code, or something similar. Then it’s still secret, isn’t it? And not _equally_ so as if it had never been expressed? The language could also be of a kind that I could never learn, for example, with extraordinarily complicated rules.

### <span class="fac">[40b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40b.webp)</span>

Thus, a person can hide his thoughts from me by speaking a language that is foreign to me. But where is the hidden mental process here?

### <span class="fac">[40b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/40b.webp),[41a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41a.webp)</span>

A person always thinks aloud, but keeps all his thoughts secret from me by (always) speaking to himself in a language that he knows I don't understand. This is also a form of concealment.

### <span class="fac">[41a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41a.webp)</span>

I can choose the language in which I think. But not as if I think, and then choose the language into which I want to translate my thoughts.

### <span class="fac">[41a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41a.webp)</span>

Rather, everything is different in psychology than it appears, because the explanations that naturally present themselves to us are all wrong; they are images that are very natural to us, but whose application we do not fully understand, which illustrate, illuminate, but do not solve any riddle. (The images of the alchemists.)

### <span class="fac">[41a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41a.webp)</span>

But what is the important thing about me saying this & that to myself? (tapping my hand?) And why should any other process be more important than this? Unless it is because of its consequences. (For one does indeed view thinking as a strange process, as a speaking, or a language-like process, whose sense is contained within itself, not in a technique & in its application. (The thought, the language of thought, a meta-language.) As if the sense of the words did not lie in the flow of application, but the act of thinking, whatever it is, has it once & for all within itself. (And this, of course, merely means that the meaning of “interpreting” is indeed _related_ to that of “speaking,” but the two are categorically different.)

### <span class="fac">[41b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41b.webp)</span>

I say to myself, “He deceives me.” At the same time, I glare, sitting in a brooding posture. Well, what’s so bad about that? — But I _think_ it. — The words are in a certain context of experiences & actions. Yes, how else could the word _“He”_ have a referent! And what is it that he deceives you about? — The words are in a stream. Only in a _life_ do they have their sense.

### <span class="fac">[41b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41b.webp)</span>

Yes: I not only said these words, but also thought them; but that was because of the role they played in my life, in a human life. Above all, I am a person who has learned this language, who has grown up in it; my life is, in a certain sense, merged with it. It and my life are so intertwined that my words are as meaningful as any other of my actions. Words & actions often connect and hold together like the warp and weft of a fabric.

### <span class="fac">[41b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/41b.webp),[42a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42a.webp)</span>

30.05.1948

I don't know whether what I saw was a stone or a fish, and I will never know. But I know what I would have to do to find out. But if I don't know what the other person is thinking, what steps would I have to take to find out? For simply asking him to say it wouldn't help. There is—would I say—no means of investigation. Think of the case in which you don't trust the other person and would give anything to find out what he means and what he is thinking.

### <span class="fac">[42a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42a.webp)</span>

I don’t know what he thinks, what he tells himself when he is alone. If I could listen in, it would make many aspects of his behaviour understandable to me. Or perhaps _not_. I would then no longer be surprised by his behaviour; I would know what kind of way I would have to talk to him. Knowing what he says to himself could have this effect.

### <span class="fac">[42a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42a.webp)</span>

A means which, if given to him, would make him express his thoughts. (In vino veritas.) He can only think aloud. – That could exist. Could one still say that his inner being is hidden from me?

### <span class="fac">[42a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42a.webp)</span>

There are different kinds of concealment.

### <span class="fac">[42a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42a.webp)</span>

[The afterimage of the window cross on closed eyes tilts when I tilt my head. This, I believe, is an important psychological fact, although I do not clearly see what follows from it.]

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-42a.6+42b.1) <span class="fac">[42a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42a.webp),[42b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42b.webp)</span> {#42a642b1}

I believe that the education of people today tends to reduce their capacity for suffering. A school is now considered good if the children have a good time. And that was not the _standard_ in the past. And the parents want the children to become like themselves (only more so), and yet they allow them to go through an education that is _completely_ different from their own. – No attention is paid to the capacity for suffering, because suffering is not supposed to exist; it is actually outdated.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-42b.2) <span class="fac">[42b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42b.webp)</span> {#42b2}

“The treachery of the object” – An unnecessary anthropomorphism. One could speak of a treachery of the _world_; one can easily imagine that the devil created the world, or a part of it. And it is _not_ necessary to imagine the intervention of the demon in each individual case; everything can happen ‘in accordance with the laws of nature’; then, it is simply the case that the entire plan was designed for the worst from the beginning. But man finds himself in this world in which things break, slip, and all sorts of misfortune are instigated. And he is, of course, one of the things. – The ‘treachery’ of the object is a foolish anthropomorphism. For the truth is much more serious than this fiction.

### <span class="fac">[42b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/42b.webp)</span>

“I can never know what the other person is feeling.” – Nonsense; I _can_ know it; just as I know that I have a book in front of me, that it will fall if I let it go, that I have not made a mistake in this multiplication. – Not really; you can _believe_ it, be convinced of it, but you cannot _know_ it. – Well, what _can_ I know? And your ‘can’ must be a logical one. You are making a distinction between categories, and I have nothing against that. Now there is no difference in degree, as it first appears.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-43a.1) <span class="fac">[43a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43a.webp)</span> {#43a1}

A stylistic device may be practical, but it is forbidden to me. Schopenhauer’s “as which,” for example. It would sometimes make expression more convenient, clearer, but it cannot be used by someone who perceives it as old-fashioned; and he must not override this perception.

### <span class="fac">[43a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43a.webp)</span>

You can be as _certain_ of the other person’s feeling as of _any_ fact. (Which ‘can’ is that?) This does not mean that the sentences “He is happy” and “2 × 2 = 4” have become similar instruments. To say “certainty is different” seems obvious, but it does not make anything clearer.

### <span class="fac">[43a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43a.webp)</span>

“But I cannot _know_ that he is happy.” – That is, to arouse doubt in oneself. I say to myself: “What if he were pretending, and inwardly unhappy!” I think of cases in which I have been deceived. And now must I succeed in seriously doubting his mood? No. But I know, so to speak, the way in which such a doubt could be achieved.

### <span class="fac">[43a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43a.webp),[43b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43b.webp)</span>

“But aren’t you simply closing your eyes to doubt when you are _certain_?” – Mine are closed to you. It is probably true: that doubt is arrived at in a completely different way than in an arithmetic sentence. Above all, there is the complete certainty, which is the borderline case of a _belief_ that varies in degrees. – And indeed, _everything_ is different.

### <span class="fac">[43b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43b.webp)</span>

“But I cannot _know_ that the other person is in pain.” The pain is in _him_; it is not in me; I do not see it. So can I _know_ that he is groaning? Not only that it seems so to me? “I cannot know that he is in pain; it can only be highly probable.” And if I see the other person writhing in pain in a concrete case, should that give me the _slightest_ doubt?

### <span class="fac">[43b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43b.webp)</span>

Should I, when confronted with a calculation, say: “Now it is not quite so _certain_”? Here, doubt is philosophical, not practical. The doubt, where it does not exist, is only, so to speak, _closer_.

### <span class="fac">[43b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/43b.webp),[44a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44a.webp)</span>

And now – may I say – there is the case of hopeless doubt. – If I say: “I have no idea what he is really thinking –”. He is a closed book to me. If the only way to understand the other person would be to go through the same education as he did, – which is impossible. And here there is no pretense. But think of people whose education is geared towards suppressing the expression of emotions on their face and in their gestures, and these people make themselves inaccessible to me by speaking a language that I do not understand. Now I say “I have no idea what is going on in them”, and yet it is there as an external fact.

### <span class="fac">[44a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44a.webp)</span>

May 31, 1948

One can also pretend by speaking the full truth, but knowing that the other person will misunderstand it.

### <span class="fac">[44a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44a.webp)</span>

June 1, 1948

One _sees_ emotion.” – In contrast to what? – Now, for example: one does not see the facial expressions and then _concludes_ that he is feeling joy, sadness, or boredom. One describes his face directly as sad, joyful, or bored, even if one is not able to give any other description of his face. – One might say that sadness is personified in the face. This is essential to what we call ‘emotion’.

### <span class="fac">[44a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44a.webp)</span>

I say the word “but” and ask “How did you understand it?” He: “As a conjunction.” – How did he do that? Well, it seemed to him that the word, when he heard it, already made that explanation necessary. As if it had the meaning within itself, like a reflection, and he was only bringing it to light. As if the word had its own sound, which identified it as a conjunction. As if the meaning was already reflected in the _surface_ of the word as a depth. And does it still need an explanation?

### <span class="fac">[44a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44a.webp),[44b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44b.webp)</span>

One can probably tell someone, whom I call meaning-blind, “Tell him to go to the bank, & I mean the garden bench,” but not: “Say the word bank & mean the garden bench by it.” He will also not be able to report: that he almost succeeded, but the word slipped into the wrong meaning. It also does not occur to him that the word has something in it, which, like a spelling, fixes the meaning; & also not that the spelling is as if a picture of the meaning. – One is, for example, strongly tempted to think that the other spelling corresponds to a slight difference in pronunciation, even where it certainly is not the case! This is the case, which serves as an example for many others: that one pronounces the two words (e.g. “für” & “führ”) and pronounces them _really_ somewhat differently, although one naturally does not do so in the flow of speech, if one does not think about such things; already because one then pronounces each of the two words differently on different occasions.

### <span class="fac">[44b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44b.webp)</span>

Different people experience the change in the spelling of a word very differently. (The lengthening-h.) And the feeling is not just piety for an old use. For someone for whom orthography is only a practical matter, a feeling is missing, similar to the one that would be missing for the ‘meaning-blind’.

### <span class="fac">[44b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/44b.webp)</span>

How could he hear the word in its meaning? How was it possible?! – Not at all – in _these_ dimensions.

### <span class="fac">[45a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45a.webp)</span>

But isn’t it true that the word means that to me now? Why not? After all, this sense does not conflict with the other uses of the word. Someone says: “Give him the command ‥․ & _intend_ it ‥‥!” What can that mean?

### <span class="fac">[45a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45a.webp)</span>

My eye fell on the word “Mach” at the beginning of a sentence. I took it to be the name of the physicist. – _How so?_ – There is no how so. It is as I say it is. – But what _does_ it say to me, what you say?

### <span class="fac">[45a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45a.webp)</span>

But why do you use this expression [my] for your experience, a badly fitting suit! – That is the expression of the experience, as is “it is yellow” and “I knew in my dream that…” expressions of other experiences. A badly fitting suit is only that if you misinterpret it. This expression belongs to the experience just as the natural expression of pain belongs to pain.

### <span class="fac">[45a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45a.webp)</span>

W. James: the thought is already complete at the beginning of the sentence. How can one know this? – But the _intention_ to express it can already exist before the first word is said. For if one asks someone “Do you know what you want to say?”, he will often answer in the affirmative. I have the intention to whistle this theme; does this mean that I have already whistled it in some sense, perhaps in thought?

### <span class="fac">[45a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45a.webp),[45b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45b.webp)</span>

For someone who answers yes to the question “Do you already know what you want to say?”, something may be in their mind; but if this were something objectively audible or visible, one could usually not infer the intended meaning from it. (Demonstrate.)

### <span class="fac">[45b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45b.webp)</span>

Not everyone who has an intention necessarily has a _plan_.

### <span class="fac">[45b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45b.webp)</span>

What forms of mental defects actually exist is not what interests _us_; but rather the possibilities of such forms. Not whether there are people who are incapable of the thought “I wanted to…” at that time, but rather: how this concept can be realized.

### <span class="fac">[45b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45b.webp)</span>

How could this assumption be consistently carried out? What would we call a consistent application of it? – If you assume that one person cannot do _that_, how is it with _the other_? Can he not do it either? Where does this concept lead us?

### <span class="fac">[45b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/45b.webp),[46a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46a.webp)</span>

Intention & thought. I can promise something to another, or even to myself, and that is an important action. And it is not the same whether I do it thoughtlessly, or whether I really mean my promise at the time. One also says that one can tell from the facial expression and tone whether this is the case. Am I now talking about ‘meaning’ as an internal process: what is the importance of this process? It can only lie in its consequences, and if external processes had the same consequences, then they would also have the same importance. – “When I looked at his face, I knew that he was serious.” So I conclude from his facial expression that I can rely on his word.

### <span class="fac">[46a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46a.webp)</span>

And does he recognize from what he feels that he will be able to rely on his own word? – Suppose he always gave himself a sincere promise in writing. This could lead him to keep it, while he has had the experience that he takes a merely verbal promise lightly when it comes to keeping it. So a particular experience could also have an effect when making a promise.

### <span class="fac">[46a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46a.webp)</span>

“One must solemnly promise oneself to do it, and then one will indeed do it.” A solemn promise includes, for example, reflecting on the matter; it requires a certain preparation. In the end, there may indeed be a formal promise, perhaps even spoken aloud, but that is only _one_ stone in this edifice. (A vow.)

### <span class="fac">[46a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46a.webp)</span>

One could call the vow a ceremony. (Baptism, even if it is not a Christian sacrament.) And a ceremony has its own importance.

### <span class="fac">[46a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46a.webp)</span>

“I intended to…” does not express the memory of an experience. (Just as little as: “I was about to…”)

### <span class="fac">[46a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46a.webp),[46b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46b.webp)</span>

“What a strange and terrible sound. I will never forget it.” And why should one not be able to say that about remembering (“What a strange experience…”) when one has seen into the past for the first time? –

### <span class="fac">[46b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46b.webp)</span>

The circumstances may be such that I know what is going on in him, i.e., that he is not a mystery to me, – or that he is a mystery to me.

### <span class="fac">[46b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46b.webp)</span>

June 2, 1948

The other person can do some calculations in their head while I do not know what he is calculating; so something is going on in him during this time, but not necessarily anything physical, and it is impossible to know what it is, unless he says it.

### <span class="fac">[46b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46b.webp)</span>

If he tells me immediately afterward, can he be mistaken? How does he even recognize what happened when the such-and-such multiplication took place, for example? (He doesn't recognize it.)

### <span class="fac">[46b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46b.webp)</span>

Could he not simply imagine that he has done the calculation? (This is not meant to contradict the fact that he now knows the result of the calculation. And he could also have made a mistake in the calculation.) And if there is no mistake here, is it not because certainty exists?

### <span class="fac">[46b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/46b.webp),[47a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47a.webp)</span>

Someone tells me that he just calculated in his head how much ‥‥ × ‥‥ is. He gives an obviously incorrect result, and when asked how he obtained it, he says the calculation; it is complete nonsense, although he now realizes it, but at the time, he says, it seemed completely correct to him. (Something similar happens in a dream.) Is this not possible? I mean, his mental calculation must first prove itself.

### <span class="fac">[47a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47a.webp),[47b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

‘He is concealing something from me, he can conceal it in such a way that it is not even _thinkable_ that I should become aware of it.’ I do not want to concede that! – But how if he were to give signs, without knowing it, that betray him? – But even if I were to derive a calculation from his movements of the larynx, for example, only he could decide whether he has actually done this calculation. – Could I not insist that he has forgotten what he calculated: that his statement (without claiming that he is lying) should not be accepted? That is to say: declare it worthless, or only attribute its value as a phenomenon from which conclusions about his overall state can be drawn. And furthermore: what is concealed? Is it not as if he has concealed a script, or rather something that looks like a script; its meaning, however, would lie only in the interpretation that he will then give it?

### <span class="fac">[47b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

If something is hidden, is it not as if a script is hidden, or rather something that resembles a script; its meaning lies only in what he then reads out of it, or into it?

### <span class="fac">[47b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

Of course, he can _mislead_ me, lead me to false conclusions. But it does not follow from this that he has something to hide, although his way of acting can be compared with hiding something.

### <span class="fac">[47b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

Could one speak of someone having something hidden in their nervous system: the _ability_ to state the solution to a calculation (for example)?

### <span class="fac">[47b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

“Why should…” and “Why shouldn’t…” are philosophical thought processes.

### <span class="fac">[47b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

06/03/1948

Am I not _justifiably_ convinced that he isn’t putting on an act against me? – And can I therefore not convince someone else of my point of view?

### <span class="fac">[47b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

If I tell him how my friend behaved, in large and small matters, will he reasonably doubt the authenticity of my friend's feelings?

### <span class="fac">[47b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/47b.webp)</span>

Does one doubt the authenticity of Lear's feelings?

### <span class="fac">[48a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48a.webp)</span>

Is it thoughtlessness not to keep the _possibility_ of pretending in mind?

### <span class="fac">[48a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48a.webp)</span>

04.06.1948

Remembrance: a seeing into the past. One might call _dreaming_ that, if it presents us with things from the past. But not remembrance; for even if it presents us with scenes in a hallucinatory way, it still teaches us that this is the past.

### <span class="fac">[48a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48a.webp)</span>

But if memory now shows us the past, how does it show us that it is the past? It does _not_ show us the past, just as our senses do not show us the present.

### <span class="fac">[48a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48a.webp)</span>
