One cannot say that it shares the past with us. For even if memory were an audible voice that spoke to us, how could we understand it? If it tells us, for example, “Yesterday the weather was nice,” how can I learn what “yesterday” means!

### <span class="fac">[48a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48a.webp),[48b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48b.webp)</span>

How do we learn what “yesterday” means? (Step by step?) We could have formed the particular sound “Yesterday” by chance while letting the memory of yesterday's experience come to the fore. Suppose it happens in such a way that adults in the presence of the child talk about an event from yesterday, use the word “yesterday,” and the child understands them; he can perhaps join in their conversation. That is roughly how it must happen, although not so quickly and easily. (But that does not, of course, mean that thinking or guessing, or any kind of explanation, brings people to an understanding of the word. Rather, a gesture.) Can I now assume that one day (without step-by-step instruction) the child can use the word, which he hears from the adult, correctly, so it could also happen that this word is, as it were, a natural sound to him when he expresses a memory for the first time.

### <span class="fac">[48b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48b.webp)</span>

It is a human reaction when one has learned to use words like “The aunt is coming,” to then use these words for the _past_ event. The mother says to the child, with exaggerated inflection, “Yesterday she was here.” The child repeats it. – But now it must use these words spontaneously (I did not say “_understand_”). It must once _tell_ about it, & how it came about is actually irrelevant. What could the adult teach it? – certainly not explain what the past is! One could say, although this would also be misleading: the child had to make a leap, & one could not help it during the leap, but only give it the necessary preparation.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-48b.3) <span class="fac">[48b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48b.webp)</span> {#48b3}

Religious belief & superstition are quite different. The one originates from _fear_ & is a kind of false science. The other is a form of trust.

### <span class="fac">[48b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/48b.webp)</span>

I only demonstrate something to myself in the same way that I would demonstrate it to another person.

### <span class="fac">[49a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span>

I can demonstrate my good memory to the other person, and also demonstrate it to myself. I can quiz myself. (Vocabulary, dates.)

### <span class="fac">[49a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span>

But how do I bring about the act of remembering? Well, I ask myself, “How did I spend this morning?” & answer that question myself. – But what exactly did I demonstrate to myself? Was it the act of remembering? What is it like: to remember something? Would I have demonstrated the act of remembering to _someone else_ by doing this?

### <span class="fac">[49a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span>

“To intend to do something is a special inner process.” – But what kind of process – even if you could imagine it – could achieve what we demand of intention?

### <span class="fac">[49a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span>

“When I took out the chessboard, I naturally intended to play chess.” Why should what I _say_ on such an occasion be more important than what I do?

### <span class="fac">[49a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span>

Again and again, when philosophizing, one comes up against a (new) prejudice that has ensnared us.

### <span class="fac">[49a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span>

I take the milk jug, say “Hmm, milk” & set out to fetch it. There was an intention here. But do these words express a thought? I.e., should I talk about a thought here?

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-49a.7) <span class="fac">[49a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49a.webp)</span> {#49a7}

(It would almost be strange if there weren’t animals with the mental life of plants. I.e., with the lacking mental life.)

### <span class="fac">[49b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49b.webp)</span>

“Yes! You had the thought that you should fetch milk.” – And why should one say that; what speaks for it? I said these words, did this & that; that is all I can report. And why should I add something to the report. – Of course, I could tell someone about the process, say “I thought I had to fetch milk, & went ‥‥”, but in doing so, I am not (now) reporting on an inner process that accompanied the words “Hmm, milk”.

### <span class="fac">[49b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49b.webp)</span>

One might think that I only pretend to solve problems, that I do so merely through rhetoric. And yet, my solution is a genuine one. I simply persuade one to adopt a certain _view_.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-49b.3) <span class="fac">[49b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49b.webp)</span> {#49b3}

One could, I believe, regard it as a fundamental principle of natural history that, wherever something in nature ‘has a function’, ‘serves a purpose’, this same thing also occurs where it does not serve any purpose, yes, where it is ‘useless’. If dreams sometimes preserve sleep, then one can expect that they sometimes disturb it; if dream hallucinations sometimes serve a _plausible_ purpose (that of imagined wish fulfilment), then one can expect that they also do the opposite. There is no ‘dynamic theory of dreams’.

### <span class="fac">[49b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/49b.webp),[50a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp)</span>

05.06.1948

Imagine people who only show compassion when they see the other person bleeding; otherwise, they laugh at his expressions of pain. That is how it is with them. Some smear themselves with animal blood in order to be pitied. If one catches them doing this, they are severely punished.

### <span class="fac">[50a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp)</span>

They do not know the question “could he not still be in pain?”

### <span class="fac">[50a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp)</span>

These people must not have certain scruples.

### <span class="fac">[50a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp)</span>

11.06.1948

Feel quite miserable; stupid, without ideas and as if I have reached a dead end. At the same time, my misery is entering the demonic realm. –

### <span class="fac">[50a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp)</span>

14.06.1948

“What do I know what is going on in him! but I don’t trust him.” “What is it to me what is going on in him! – I don’t trust him.”

### <span class="fac">[50a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp)</span>

Do I care about his inner self when I _trust_ him? If I don’t, I say “I don’t know what’s going on inside him”; but if I trust him, then I don’t: I know what’s going on inside him.

### <span class="fac">[50a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50a.webp),[50b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50b.webp)</span>

If I don’t distrust him, then I don’t care about what is going on in him. (Words & their meaning. The meaning of the words, what lies behind them, does not concern me in normal linguistic communication. They flow along & the transitions are made from words to actions & from actions to words. No one thinks about whether he is calculating thoughtfully or ‘parrot-fashion’ when he calculates. (Frege.))

### <span class="fac">[50b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50b.webp)</span>

Frege: A mathematical sentence should not be compared to a configuration of chess pieces, for it expresses a thought, & they do not. – But of course, it is precisely this that is so misleading, to say that the mathematical sentence expresses a thought. And the comparison with chess has _this_ advantage, that it eliminates the ‘thought’. The concept of ‘thought’ is an unnecessary burden in the philosophical investigation of mathematics; but the comparison with chess is also harmful.

### <span class="fac">[50b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/50b.webp),[51a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51a.webp)</span>

There may be people who talk a lot to themselves before, & during their actions, & others who say very little to themselves, who are, as it were, very taciturn even with themselves. If one asks him, “What did you think when you did that?” he might confess quite honestly, “Nothing at all,” although his action seems well-considered, even cunning. I say that I do not know what is going on in him, & in an important sense, nothing is going on in him. I do not understand him: I, for example, easily make false assumptions & am from time to time sorely disappointed in my expectations. I could form a picture of such a person by imagining that he is having conversations with himself, which, if one could hear them, would express all his states of mind. The monologues would be a construction, a hypothesis, by means of which I try to make his actions understandable to myself. Must I now assume that in him, _besides_ these monologues, there is also thinking going on? Are the monologues not quite sufficient? Can they not achieve everything that the inner life is supposed to achieve?

### <span class="fac">[51a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51a.webp)</span>

15.06.1948

One can easily imagine events & describe them in all details, which, if we were to see them occur, would make us judge everything wrongly. If, instead of the familiar surroundings, I saw a completely new environment outside my window, if the things in it behaved in ways they have never behaved before, I would perhaps utter the words “I have gone mad”, but that would only be an expression of the fact that I give up trying to understand. And the same thing could happen to me in mathematics. It might seem to me, for example, as if I am always making calculation errors, so that no solution seems reliable to me. But what is important for me about this is that there is no sharp boundary between such a state & the normal one.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-51a.3+51b.1) <span class="fac">[51a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51a.webp),[51b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp)</span> {#51a351b1}

In what lies the importance of precisely describing anomalies? If one cannot do it, that shows that one is not familiar with the concepts.

### <span class="fac">[51b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp)</span>

The cumulative evidence, the role of experience. The experienced person cannot list his experiences.

### <span class="fac">[51b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp)</span>

The statement “this behaviour makes pretence (or pain, etc.) unlikely” is justified & yet misleading.

### <span class="fac">[51b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp)</span>

17.06.1948

‘The evidence that gives me confidence is cumulative.’ That is, it accumulates unnoticed, and I cannot register it. To the question “Why do you trust him?”, the answer comes: “I know him,” “I have put him to the test,” and so on.

### <span class="fac">[51b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp)</span>

Whoever says “This behaviour makes the pain (or the pretending) likely” must surely assume that it can _turn out_ that someone is in pain (or is pretending). He must be able to say “You see, so he really was pretending”.

### <span class="fac">[51b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp)</span>

There is, perhaps, this: acquiring knowledge of people; one can also help someone in this, i.e., quasi give him instruction, but one only points to cases, points out certain characteristics, does not give fixed rules.

### <span class="fac">[51b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/51b.webp),[52a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52a.webp)</span>

I can perhaps say, “Let me talk with this person, spend time with them, & I will know whether they can be trusted,” & later: “I have the _impression_ ‥‥․.” But this is a prediction. The future may teach whether my impression was correct. Knowledge of people can convince us that this person truly feels what they claim to feel, but does it convince _them_ that others feel something?

### <span class="fac">[52a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52a.webp)</span>

21.06.1948

There is the case in which we cannot believe any evidence that this person is pretending; where we would speak, for example, of a kind of madness, but not of pretence. That is how pretence looks, we would say.

### <span class="fac">[52a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52a.webp)</span>

“One cannot put on airs like that.” – & this can be an experience, – namely that no one who behaves in _such_ a way will later behave in _such_ & such a way; but also a conceptual assertion; & the two can be related.

### <span class="fac">[52a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52a.webp)</span>

For one would not have said that the planets _**must**_ move in circles if it had not appeared that they move in circles.

### <span class="fac">[52a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52a.webp)</span>

It could be a form of human behaviour, the _unquestionable_ expression of sensation; another, the unquestionable expression of pretending; & between them would lie a realm of doubt. And I mean: – that people would be trained to behave _in this way_ towards the former, differently towards the latter, & to be uncertain in the intermediate realm.

### <span class="fac">[52a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52a.webp),[52b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp)</span>

I can point to someone during instruction & say, “Do you see, _he_ isn’t pretending.” And the student can learn from that. But if he were to ask me, “How does one actually recognize it?” then I wouldn’t know what else to answer other than something like, “Look at how he’s lying there, look at his features,” and so on.

### <span class="fac">[52b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp)</span>

Could this now be different in other people? – If, for example, they all had the same shape & facial features, then _much_ would be different.

### <span class="fac">[52b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp)</span>

“_That’s_ what pretending looks like” (I mean the whole case) – “not _like that_.”

### <span class="fac">[52b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp)</span>

And pretence is, of course, only a special case of someone expressing pain and not having pain. If this is possible at all, why should pretence always take place, this very specific psychological process? [And by psychological I do not mean “internal.”]

### <span class="fac">[52b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp)</span>

Yes, there might be a case in which we would say, “He _believes_ himself to be putting on an act.” (Pilgrim’s Progress. He _believes_ that he is uttering the curses that the Evil One utters.)

### <span class="fac">[52b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp)</span>

Human behaviour is unpredictable: is this not also the case with the ball in the Corinthian bagatelle?

### <span class="fac">[52b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/52b.webp),[53a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53a.webp)</span>

The sufficient evidence merges without a boundary into the insufficient. A natural basis for this formation of concepts is the complicated nature and the diversity of the cases. Thus, in the case of less diversity, a sharply defined concept would naturally appear. But why does it seem so difficult to imagine a simplified case? Is it as if one wanted to imagine a facial expression that would not be capable of gradual, subtle changes, but, say, only had five positions; in a change, one would abruptly pass into the other. Would this rigid smile really be a smile? And why not? – I might not behave towards it as I would towards a smile. It might not make me smile myself.

### <span class="fac">[53a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53a.webp)</span>

22.06.1948

A completely rigid facial expression could not be a friendly one. The variability and irregularity are part of the friendly expression. Irregularity is part of physiognomy.

### <span class="fac">[53a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53a.webp)</span>

The importance for us of the fine nuances of behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[53a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53a.webp)</span>

My concept here includes my relationship to the phenomenon.

### <span class="fac">[53a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53a.webp)</span>

“I don’t know _how_ I would call it. But it wouldn’t be what I call….” One can ask, and _why_ not? – It wouldn’t play the role in our life that… plays.

### <span class="fac">[53a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53a.webp),[53b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53b.webp)</span>

Can I _know_ whether a _dog_ is in pain when it is hit & cries? Should I say: “Yes, of course I cannot see its inner being (i.e., its mind/soul), but I only see external signs”?

### <span class="fac">[53b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53b.webp)</span>

Consider _this_ argument: Pain certainly has a degree. Now, no one would claim that I know every exact degree of another person’s pain; therefore, it may also have a degree of 0. But does he know the ‘exact degree’ of his pain? And what does it mean to know it?

### <span class="fac">[53b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53b.webp)</span>

Well, doesn’t he know how strong his pain is?” He has no doubt about it.

### <span class="fac">[53b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53b.webp)</span>

But I don’t know, for example, that his pain has decreased a little now. – Yes, I know it, if he tells me. What he says is also an expression.

### <span class="fac">[53b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53b.webp)</span>

The uncertainty does not stem from the fact that he does not display his pain outwardly. And it is not even an uncertainty _in the specific case_. If the border between two countries were disputed, it would follow that the national affiliation of each individual resident would be questionable.

### <span class="fac">[53b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/53b.webp)</span>

‘Sandheap’ is a vaguely defined concept – but why does one not use a sharply defined concept instead? Is the reason in the nature of the heaps? Whose nature determines our concept?

### <span class="fac">[54a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp)</span>

“A dog is more similar to a human than a being of human shape would be that behaved ‘mechanically.’” That is, behaved according to simple rules?

### <span class="fac">[54a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp)</span>

‘Lying’ requires a certain context. – Would someone who, in just _one_ instance, told a deliberate untruth, always with the same mischievous expression, be said to be lying?

### <span class="fac">[54a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp)</span>

The rigidity in and of itself would already be so abnormal that one could no longer react to the behaviour in a normal way.

### <span class="fac">[54a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp)</span>

We judge an action according to its background in human life, and this background is not uniform, but we could imagine it as a very complicated, intricate pattern, which we could not reproduce, but which we could recognize according to its general impression.

### <span class="fac">[54a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp)</span>

The backdrop is the machinery of life. And our concept refers to something _within_ that machinery.

### <span class="fac">[54a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp)</span>

And even the concept of “mechanism” implies indeterminacy. Because a mechanism only arises through constant repetition. And for ‘constant repetition’ there is no definite beginning.

### <span class="fac">[54a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54a.webp),[54b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp)</span>

Variability itself is a characteristic of behaviour, which cannot be lacking in it without making it something completely different for us. (The (particular) characteristic features of grief, for example, are no more significant than its mutability.)

### <span class="fac">[54b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp)</span>

24.06.1948

[How much of my work is vanity and how much is boredom? I do not want to estimate it.]

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-54b.3) <span class="fac">[54b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp)</span> {#54b3}

25.06.1948

[I am too soft, too weak, & therefore too lazy to achieve anything significant. The diligence of great people is, among other things, a sign of their _strength_, apart from their _inner_ richness.]

### <span class="fac">[54b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp)</span>

It is unnatural to draw a conceptual boundary there, where there is no particular justification for it, where similarities always draw us across the arbitrarily drawn line.

### <span class="fac">[54b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp)</span>

The background of life is as it were pointillist.

### <span class="fac">[54b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp)</span>

How could one describe human ways of acting? Only by showing the actions of the various people, as they interact. Not what _one now_ does, but the whole jumble is the background against which we see an action, & which determines our judgment, our concepts & reactions.

### <span class="fac">[54b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/54b.webp),[55a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55a.webp)</span>

How could one explain what it means to ‘feign pain’, to ‘pretend to be in pain’? (Of course, the question is: _to whom_?) _Whom should one demonstrate it to?_ And why could such a demonstration so easily lead to misunderstanding? One might say: “Live among us for a while & you will learn to understand it.”

### <span class="fac">[55a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55a.webp)</span>

How could you explain the concept of pretense to someone, and to whom could you explain it? And how would it show that he has understood it?

### <span class="fac">[55a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55a.webp)</span>

One could simply teach him to mime pain (e.g.), (not with the intention of deceiving). But could it be taught to everyone? I mean: he could certainly learn to produce certain crude signs of pain, without ever being able to give a finer imitation from his own understanding, from his own insight. (One could perhaps even teach a clever dog a kind of mournful howl; but it would never come from him in a conscious imitation.)

### <span class="fac">[55a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55a.webp),[55b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55b.webp)</span>

Should one now assume that these people, with their more sharply defined concepts, do not have more complex motives for expressing pain, or that they simply do not take note of such motives? Should one, for example, say: They simply pity & care for the one who complains under such circumstances, & not the others. And so they learn it from an early age. There are then also words for these situations, but they do not exactly correspond to ours for genuine & feigned feeling. Should one now say: “This is only possible with people who have very simple feelings”? How does simple feeling manifest itself? Or: “These people (still) do not see that one can feign pain, or experience genuine pain, under these circumstances”? Would one have to simply make these people aware of something they have not yet thought of, in order to teach them our way? And _how_ would one do that? They simply do not take note of our distinction here. Whoever wants to be pitied will be pitied by them; except in these & those specific cases. (Similar to payment according to principles foreign to us.)

### <span class="fac">[55b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55b.webp)</span>

I actually want to say that the intellectual scruples begin in instinct (have their roots there). Or also: The language-game does not originate in _reflection_. Reflection is a part of the language-game. And the concept is therefore at home in the language-game.

### <span class="fac">[55b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55b.webp)</span>

26.06.1948

“Couldn’t you imagine any other situation in which this could also be interpreted as pretense?” But what does it mean to say that it could always be pretense? Has experience taught us this? And how else could we be instructed about pretense?

### <span class="fac">[55b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55b.webp)</span>

“But can’t you also imagine that it is pretense here?” What does what I _think_ have to say?

### <span class="fac">[55b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/55b.webp),[56a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp)</span>

Isn't something similar present here, as in the relationship between Euclidean geometry and visual impression? (I mean: there is a profound similarity.) Because Euclidean geometry also corresponds to experience only in a very peculiar way, and not just ‘approximately. One could perhaps say that it corresponds just as much to our method of sign, as to other things, or that it corresponds to certain needs of _thinking_. Its concepts have their roots in widely scattered and remote areas.

### <span class="fac">[56a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp)</span>

For just as the verb “to believe” is conjugated like the verb “to hit,” so concepts for one area are formed by analogy with concepts from very remote areas. (The genders of the main words.)

### <span class="fac">[56a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp)</span>

Concept formation, for example, takes boundlessness into account, in which there are no sharp boundaries to be found in experience. (Boundless approximations.)

### <span class="fac">[56a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp)</span>

One could sometimes say that the concepts are formed according to a convenience of thought. (As the ruler is not only according to the things to be measured, but also according to the person.)

### <span class="fac">[56a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp)</span>

“But one cannot _certainly_ recognize pain from the expression.” – This must be due to the _concept_ of ‘pain’. Not in the nature of the pain experience, – which I am having right now.

### <span class="fac">[56a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp)</span>

Imagine a legislator who commands the people: “If someone behaves _like this_, you should not have any compassion for him. Such a person has no pain.”

### <span class="fac">[56a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56a.webp),[56b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56b.webp)</span>

But how can the legislator determine who has pain and who does not? – He determines a concept and a behavior, and he forbids a concept. But it may be that he does not succeed, and that the people have compassion where he has forbidden it, and that, for example, in their compassionate action, they imitate the other person’s complaint, as mothers and nurses do. (The sounds of pain of compassion.)

### <span class="fac">[56b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56b.webp)</span>

But damn it: everyone knows whether he has pain! – How could _everyone_ know? To do so, he would have to know, above all, that they all have the same thing.

### <span class="fac">[56b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56b.webp)</span>

A tribe has two concepts, related to our ‘pain’. One is applied to visible injuries and is associated with care, compassion, etc.; the other they apply to stomach pain, for example, and it is associated with amusement at the complainer. “But do they really not notice the similarity?” – Do we have a concept everywhere where a similarity exists? The question is: Is the similarity _important_ to them? And must it be?

### <span class="fac">[56b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56b.webp)</span>

If you think about the reasons why someone might grit their teeth and endure pain, or feign it, you will come up with countless reasons. Why is there this multitude? Life is very complicated. There are many possibilities. But couldn’t other people leave many of these _possibilities_ aside, as it were, shrug their shoulders at them?

### <span class="fac">[56b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/56b.webp),[57a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57a.webp)</span>

But doesn’t this person then overlook something that is there? – He does not take note of it, and why should he? – But then his concept is fundamentally different from ours. – **Fundamentally** different? Different. – But then it is as if his word could not _denote_ the same thing as ours. Or only part of it. – But that is how it must look if his concept is different. Because the indeterminacy of our concept can project itself onto the _object_ that the word denotes. So that, if the indeterminacy were lacking, it would not be ‘the same thing’ either. The image we use illustrates the indeterminacy.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-57a.2) <span class="fac">[57a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57a.webp)</span> {#57a2}

If God really _chooses_ the people to be saved, then there is no reason why he shouldn't choose them according to nations, races, or temperaments. Why shouldn't the choice manifest itself in the laws of nature? (He _could_ also choose in such a way that the choice follows a law.) I have read in excerpts from the writings of St. John of the Cross that people perished because they were not lucky enough to find a wise spiritual guide at the right moment. And how can one then say that God is not testing people beyond their limits? Here, I am inclined to say that flawed concepts have caused much harm, but the truth is that I don't even _know_ what brings salvation and what brings ruin.

### <span class="fac">[57a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57a.webp),[57b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57b.webp)</span>

June 28, 1948

Don't let grief upset you! You should let it into your heart and not be afraid of madness either! It may come to you as a friend and not as an enemy, and only your _resistance_ is the evil. Let grief into your heart, do not close the door to it. Standing outside the door in the realm of the mind, it is terrible, but in the _heart_ it is not. After all, _regret_ has never driven anyone mad.

### <span class="fac">[57b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57b.webp)</span>

June 29, 1948

When philosophizing, one must not _cut off_ a thought disorder. It must run its natural course, and the _slow_ healing is the most important thing.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-57b.3) <span class="fac">[57b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57b.webp)</span> {#57b3}

June 30, 1948

We must not forget: even our finer, more philosophical concerns have an instinctive basis. For example, 'One can never know...' The openness to further arguments. People whom one could not teach this would seem intellectually inferior to us, _still_ incapable of forming a certain concept.

### <span class="fac">[57b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57b.webp)</span>

'One can never know what is going on in one's soul' – this seems to be a matter of course. And it is so in the sense that the image used here already contains the sentence. But one must also question it together with the image.

### <span class="fac">[57b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/57b.webp)</span>

The indeterminacy is an infinitely essential characteristic of our concept. It means a great deal to us.

### <span class="fac">[58a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58a.webp)</span>

'Who knows what is going on in him!' Interpreting external events as consequences of unknown, or only suspected, inner states. The interest that is directed towards this inner state, as to the chemical composition from which the behavior emerges. One only has to (say to oneself) 'What do the inner processes matter to me, whatever they are?' in order to see that a different attitude is conceivable. – 'But everyone is always interested in _his_ inner world!' Nonsense. Would I even know that pain, etc., is something inner if I were not told?

### <span class="fac">[58a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58a.webp)</span>

The doubt about the inner process is an _expression_. But the _doubt_ is an instinctive behavior. A behavior towards others. And it does not stem from the fact that I know, from myself, _what_ pain, etc., is; I know that it is something inner and that it can be associated with something external. I know all this even more!

### <span class="fac">[58a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58a.webp)</span>

Remember: Most people say that one feels nothing during anesthesia. But some do say: He _could_ feel something, but he simply forgets it completely. So, if there are some who doubt and some for whom no doubt arises, then the certainty could also exist much more generally.

### <span class="fac">[58a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58a.webp),[58b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58b.webp)</span>

Or perhaps doubt could have a different and much less indeterminate form than in our world of thought.

### <span class="fac">[58b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58b.webp)</span>

What I still feel, however, is that these people overlook something real, that they take no notice of a non-external fact, something _important_. They overlook – I want to say – the actual feeling. They overlook that it can be there, even if the external signs are not the normal ones. And yet, I am making a mistake here; and yet I cannot help but make it!

### <span class="fac">[58b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58b.webp)</span>

These people really do overlook something. And I can also say that they overlook the actual feeling. Because they do not _have_ our concept. Wouldn't someone who can only count to five also overlook something, take no notice of something? Even if 'overlook' is a bad image. Because we would like to say: They overlook a phenomenon.

### <span class="fac">[58b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58b.webp)</span>

They use a word that we best translate as “pain”; but their laws of evidence do differ from ours.

### <span class="fac">[58b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/58b.webp),[59a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59a.webp)</span>

“I swear, I’m in pain; & they didn’t believe it!” – Didn’t believe it? They laughed at you, because there was (let’s say) no blood to be _seen_. Why should they not have believed something? They didn’t believe that you were _putting on an act_; because they also don’t have a concept of putting on an act.

### <span class="fac">[59a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59a.webp)</span>

How does it begin? The child cries, & no one speaks of pretending. If something were to look like pretending, it would be an animalistic form of pretending, a form of life. Then, at some point, a case arises where one thinks of pretending. It is, perhaps, a primitive form of pretending. But one does not even know if one is allowed to call it _that_. It is connected with the development of the child’s ‘_abilities_’. One does not know what it can already do until one has seen a certain _sequence_ of actions. (<math display="inline"><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>‥</mtext><mtext>․</mtext></math>) Here there is a conceptual indeterminacy: ‘the beginning of a custom’. – It is only in a certain way of life that one calls it ‥‥․ The question “What is going on in the child’s mind?” need not occur to anyone.

### <span class="fac">[59a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59a.webp)</span>

If you say to them: “What is probably going on in his mind –”, they answer: “Why should we care?”

### <span class="fac">[59a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59a.webp),[59b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59b.webp)</span>

Consider: We use the words “I don’t know” in a strange way; when we say, for example, that we do not know whether one person really feels more than another, or whether he simply expresses it more strongly. – Then it is not clear what method of investigation would decide the question. Of course, the statement is not entirely pointless: We want to say that we can compare the feelings of A & B, but the circumstances mislead us in a comparison of A with C.

### <span class="fac">[59b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59b.webp)</span>

Only God sees the innermost thoughts. But why should these be so important? And must all people believe that?

### <span class="fac">[59b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59b.webp)</span>

‘Imagine people who only think aloud.’ It is not self-evident that beings with the physical nature of human beings think; so they should only think by speaking, i.e., do nothing else that we would call thinking. (Their secret thoughts are monologues.)

### <span class="fac">[59b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59b.webp)</span>

The stages between instinctive cleverness & deliberate thought. An idiot might act cleverly, as we would describe it, & we would not believe that he is capable of _planning_ anything. When asked “What is probably going on in him?” we say “very little is probably going on in him.” But what do we know about that?! We form a picture of him based on his behaviour, his utterances, his capacity for thought.

### <span class="fac">[59b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59b.webp)</span>

We put different things together into a ‘pattern’, such as that of deceit. The image of the inner self completes the pattern.

### <span class="fac">[59b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/59b.webp),[60a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

If a concept depends on a way of life, then there must be an indeterminacy in it. Because if the pattern deviates from the normal, it becomes questionable what we should say here.

### <span class="fac">[60a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

Could certainty only exist where there are regular life patterns? But what do they do when an irregular case occurs? Perhaps they just shrug their shoulders.

### <span class="fac">[60a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

“He told me – & no doubt was possible about his credibility – that ‥‥‥” Under what circumstances is no doubt possible about his credibility? Can I specify them? No.

### <span class="fac">[60a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

You must think about the _purpose_ of words. What does language have to do with pain?

### <span class="fac">[60a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

The purpose of words is not to denote something. Just as the purpose of a machine part is not to be attached to something, but to move something.

### <span class="fac">[60a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

I _know_ that he is in pain. But _how_ do I know it, when his pain-behaviour is made questionable by the possibility of pretending? I _know_ it, because in _this_ case his behaviour is a reliable index. But by what is this case characterized? By circumstances.

### <span class="fac">[60a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60a.webp)</span>

In the case I am imagining, people have a word that serves a similar purpose (has a similar function) to the word “pain”. One cannot say that it “denotes” something similar. It takes hold in a different way, and yet similarly, in their lives.

### <span class="fac">[60b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60b.webp)</span>

A proper feeling has led people to classify logic within philosophy. For philosophy is a logical matter. Yes, it is a comfort to realize that a philosophical obscurity means that one is not yet familiar with logic. For there are only major problems there, and not minor ones.

### <span class="fac">[60b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60b.webp)</span>

“But one cannot, with _certainty_, recognize pain after it has been expressed.” – One can _only_ recognize it after it has been expressed. And the uncertainty is a constitutive one. It is not a deficiency. It is inherent in our concept that this uncertainty exists; in our instrument. Whether this concept is practical or impractical is not really what matters. One could say that it is appropriate to our nature, our feelings; but how can one prove that, what speaks for it? – _Thus_ we act and speak. We did not choose it.

### <span class="fac">[60b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60b.webp)</span>

01.07.1948

The concept is not only the way we _think_ about the matter. It is not only a kind of classification, a point of view for organizing. It is part of our actions.

### <span class="fac">[60b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/60b.webp),[61a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61a.webp)</span>

The indeterminacy does not consist _because_ pain is something psychic, something _inner_. Rather, both characterize our concept. – Is this now particularly appropriate to our needs? Is it appropriate to our needs that we count as we count? That is how we live. It is part of the human history of nature.

### <span class="fac">[61a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61a.webp)</span>

Who wants to say how our concepts are causally connected with the peculiarities of our environment and (with) our own? I certainly do not. Although one can make some of it plausible.

### <span class="fac">[61a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61a.webp),[61b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61b.webp)</span>

Colors might play a different role in another world than in ours. Think of different cases: They might be more bound to specific shapes. Only eggs would be yellow, only leaves green, only blood red, etc. Dyes might not be producible, and one could not dye things. Just catchphrases. _One_ of the colors would always be associated with an unpleasant smell, or with toxicity. Color blindness or a mismatch in color judgments would be much more common than with us. Different shades of gray would be very common, the other colors rarely seen. If our number system is related to the number of our fingers, why not our system of colors to the particular way in which colors appear. A color might only occur in a gradual transition into another. Colors might only occur in the color sequence of the rainbow. If one were to find that in one country every animal always gives birth to 6 offspring and every one has 6 legs, and the people have 5 fingers like us but a base-six system, one would explain this from those facts. And if they then had only 6-legged meters, one would also bring that into the context. – If 5 offspring were a sign that the mother animal is ill, and people became ill from touch, they could call _5_ “the bad number” and avoid it on other occasions; yes, that could be expressed in their arithmetic.

### <span class="fac">[61b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61b.webp)</span>

For a differently structured world, one would naturally find the use of other linguistic instruments.

### <span class="fac">[61b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61b.webp)</span>

People who count while singing along a scale & therefore _have_ a 7-system. But why do they sing this scale? “It is in their nature.” Since it is in ours, it does not seem remarkable to us.

### <span class="fac">[61b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61b.webp)</span>

We want to explain concepts from the need for them. But actually it would only be the need for a certain way of life, which includes the use of the concept.

### <span class="fac">[61b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61b.webp)</span>

By showing the language-game, one shows the connection of language with life. That is, the intertwining of language with other life processes, not a causal connection.

### <span class="fac">[61b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/61b.webp),[62a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

What do we need this indeterminacy for? – “We just have pain, and we want to denote it!” – One can only denote pain among sensations, that is, if one has the concept of sensation, and that _shows_ us no phenomenon.

### <span class="fac">[62a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

Think about the question of whether animals, especially lower animals, such as flies, feel pain.

### <span class="fac">[62a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

The word “probability” is useful here because probability allows for gradations and gradual transitions. “It is much less probable that lower animals experience pain than that humans do.”

### <span class="fac">[62a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

Yes, here we have conceptual indeterminacy, corresponding to an indeterminacy in our behaviour towards animals.

### <span class="fac">[62a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

The uncertainty of whether a fly feels pain is a philosophical one; but could it not also be an instinctive one? And how would that manifest itself? Yes, isn’t there an uncertainty in our behaviour towards animals? He doesn’t know: is he being cruel, or not?

### <span class="fac">[62a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

Because there is uncertainty in behaviour that is not based on uncertainty in thoughts.

### <span class="fac">[62a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp)</span>

Consider the question of the uncertainty of whether the other person feels pain, in the light of the question of whether an insect feels it.

### <span class="fac">[62a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62a.webp),[62b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62b.webp)</span>

It is in the nature of pretending, and therefore also of pain, that it cannot be stated with complete certainty in the other person. Because he might be in pain and, for whatever reasons or causes, behave in an uncharacteristic way. “And what is pain? – so that I can be quite sure what you are talking about.” – _“That”_ and he hits me, so that I can be sure.

### <span class="fac">[62b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62b.webp)</span>

There is trust and distrust in behaviour! For example, if someone complains, I can react with complete certainty, trustingly, or uncertainly, like someone who has suspicions. He doesn’t need words or thoughts for this.

### <span class="fac">[62b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62b.webp)</span>

The unpredictability of human behaviour. If it were not present, would one then also say that one could never know what is going on in him?

### <span class="fac">[62b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62b.webp)</span>

But what if human behaviour were not unpredictable? How is one to imagine that? (That is: how to picture what connections to assume?)

### <span class="fac">[62b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62b.webp)</span>

Someone is lying on the road – perhaps he is sick, perhaps he is just pretending. Of course: how am I supposed to know what is going on in him. – No. That is not what it is about. Above all, we do not know what to expect.

### <span class="fac">[62b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/62b.webp)</span>

What does it matter to me what is going on in the person who is lying in ambush and will attack me?

### <span class="fac">[63a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp)</span>

“I will do _that_. He will think... and will...”. Or: “he will say to himself... and will... What he says to himself is actually irrelevant, even if he were to say it aloud; it is only part of the mechanism, the last effect of which is what interests me.

### <span class="fac">[63a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp)</span>

“I don’t know what is going on in him now: one could say the same about a complicated clockwork; (for example) an art clock that triggers various external movements according to very complicated laws. When contemplating it, one might think: if I knew what it looked like inside, what was going on now, I would know what to expect.

### <span class="fac">[63a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp)</span>

In the case of humans, it is assumed that one _cannot_ gain insight into the mechanism. Therefore, indeterminacy is postulated.

### <span class="fac">[63a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp)</span>

But if I doubt whether a spider feels pain, then it is not because I do not know what to expect.

### <span class="fac">[63a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp)</span>

The philosopher wants to master the geography of concepts: to see every place in its immediate and in its farthest environment.

### <span class="fac">[63a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp)</span>

But we cannot help but form a picture of the mental process. And _not_ because we know it from ourselves!

### <span class="fac">[63a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63a.webp),[63b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63b.webp)</span>

02.07.1948

_One_ kind of uncertainty would be the one we might also have with an unfamiliar mechanism. In the other case, we might remember an event in our life. For example, someone who has just escaped from the fear of death might be reluctant to kill a fly, when otherwise he would do so without hesitation. Or, on the other hand, that he does it hesitantly, with this experience in mind, what he would otherwise do without hesitation.

### <span class="fac">[63b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63b.webp)</span>

Even if I do ‘not rest secure in my compassion’, I do not have to think about the uncertainty of his later behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[63b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63b.webp)</span>

One uncertainty, as it were, comes from you, the other from him. One could therefore say that the one is related to an analogy; not the other. But not as if I were drawing a conclusion from the analogy!

### <span class="fac">[63b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63b.webp)</span>

‘One cannot think oneself into it’: one cannot unlearn the language (technique) that one masters, arbitrarily.

### <span class="fac">[63b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63b.webp)</span>

If life were a carpet, then this pattern (such as pretending) is not always complete & varies greatly, but we, in our world of concepts, always see the same thing recurring with variations. That is how our concepts grasp it. The concepts are not for **one**-time use.

### <span class="fac">[63b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/63b.webp),[64a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

And the pattern is connected with many, many other patterns in the carpet.

### <span class="fac">[64a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

On the stage you can see what the pattern of pretending looks like. It is here the motif, etc., etc. The concept of pretending includes the concept of discovery.

### <span class="fac">[64a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

I _say_ for example, “He might still be putting on an act” – what am I thinking when I say that? – that is, what _explanation_ would I give for the word “putting on an act”; what kinds of cases would occur to me?

### <span class="fac">[64a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

How do I _apply_ the sentence? (For here it is as in certain areas of mathematics, where there is a ‘fantastic application’.)

### <span class="fac">[64a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

I conjure up an image that can then serve a purpose. (I could even look at a painted picture.)

### <span class="fac">[64a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

Just as one had to invent a new conceptual world to describe the fall of an object, the pendulum motion, etc., by forming the concepts of acceleration, uniform motion, accelerated motion, etc., so I should create new concepts to describe precisely psychological uncertainties.

### <span class="fac">[64a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64a.webp)</span>

Sometimes I treat him the way I treat myself & would like to be treated when I am in pain, and sometimes not.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp)</span>

Sharpness is sharpness, blurriness is blurriness. I do not want to reduce blurriness to sharpness; rather, I want to grasp it conceptually as blurriness.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp)</span>

“How can one be sure that he is not pretending? one does not know what is going on in him” means: How can one be sure that he is not pretending? one cannot know whether he is not pretending.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp)</span>

A game in which each player knows in advance whether he will win or lose. And yet that would be conceivable. The appeal could lie in observing _how_ this one wins, that one loses.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp)</span>

People who treat lying differently than we do. Who, for example, often regard it as harmless, even charming fiction. Always, except in wartime.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp)</span>

The idea is that unknowability lies in the nature of pain, & that is to say, not in the essence of a concept, but of a phenomenon, of an occurrence. So that, when we speak of this occurrence, we refer to it, & we must think about it in _this_ way. (Structure of the world.) For here is the fundamental point.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp)</span>

“Those people don’t share _our_ suspicion. – So, do they have something else in mind? They have something else in their sense.

### <span class="fac">[64b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/64b.webp),[65a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

We are accustomed to a certain division of things. It has become second nature to us through language, or languages.

### <span class="fac">[65a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

These are the fixed rails on which all our thinking takes place, and therefore also our judging and acting.

### <span class="fac">[65a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

It is also true that, as Pliny says, after every ten numbers, the numbers change. If, for example, one counts with one’s fingers, or with certain patterns.

### <span class="fac">[65a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

Must the concept of modesty and boasting be known everywhere there are modest and boastful people? Perhaps they do not attach any importance to this distinction there. Some differences are unimportant to us, and could be important.

### <span class="fac">[65a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

And others have concepts that cut across ours. And why should not their concept cut across our concept of ‘pain’?

### <span class="fac">[65a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

Imagine people saying: Whoever obeys _this_ command is _so_. Or: Whoever can endure this period of time without doing ‥‥ is ‥‥. – (A test of wisdom.)

### <span class="fac">[65a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp)</span>

Things would be different in a world where all people had the same shape and facial features.

### <span class="fac">[65a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65a.webp),[65b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65b.webp)</span>

An exam question. A description of a behaviour & the circumstances is given; the question is: “Did he put on an act?” The answer could be: “No description is sufficient to decide; if I were to see the person, I would know.” Or also: “If I were to _know_ & see the person, I would know.”

### <span class="fac">[65b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65b.webp)</span>

03.07.1948

Now imagine a concept of similar use to our ‘pretending’, but such that a test question could be answered. One would take such a test in school.

### <span class="fac">[65b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65b.webp)</span>

So am I wrong if I have absolutely no doubt about the other person’s disposition? But in a particular case, I can know the other person’s disposition, & that does not make the (entire) language-game one in which it can, with certainty, be recognized by all from certain signs.

### <span class="fac">[65b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65b.webp)</span>

The ‘uncertainty’ does not relate to the particular case, but to the method, to the rules of evidence.

### <span class="fac">[65b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65b.webp)</span>

Clearly defined concepts would require uniformity of behaviour. But it is the case that, where I am _certain_, the other person is uncertain. And that is a fact of nature. [Agreement among people, e.g., in mathematics, in calculation.]

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-65b.6) <span class="fac">[65b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/65b.webp)</span> {#65b6}

If nightmares have a similar function to daydreams, then they serve, in part, to prepare people for _every_ possibility (even the worst).

### <span class="fac">[66a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66a.webp)</span>

If one says that “evidence can only make the genuineness of an expression of feeling probable,” that does _not_ mean that instead of complete certainty, there is always only a more or less confident conjecture. “Only probable” cannot refer to the degree of our conviction, but only to the kind of its justification, to the character of the language-game.

### <span class="fac">[66a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66a.webp)</span>

This must help to determine the constitution of our concept: that there is no agreement among people with regard to the certainty of their conviction. (Compare my remark about the agreement in color judgments & in mathematics.)

### <span class="fac">[66a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66a.webp)</span>

One person may be completely convinced, & the other, with the same evidence, may not be. And we therefore exclude neither this nor that person from society as incapable of judgment, or as not accountable.

### <span class="fac">[66a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66a.webp)</span>

But could not a society do just that?

### <span class="fac">[66a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66a.webp)</span>

For words only have meaning in their context.

### <span class="fac">[66a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66a.webp),[66b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

I am certain, _certain_, that he is not pretending; but the other person is not. Can I convince him? And if not, – do I say that he cannot think? (One could call the conviction of this ‘intuitive’.)

### <span class="fac">[66b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

Instinct is the first, reasoning the second. There are only grounds within a language-game.

### <span class="fac">[66b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

Do I say, “& the mind is also just something in the body”? No. (I am not so poor in categories.)

### <span class="fac">[66b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

You can vary the concept, but then you may change it to the point of unrecognizability.

### <span class="fac">[66b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

“I would never call it _that_.” That is an _important_ utterance.

### <span class="fac">[66b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

I will no longer pay this price for that (i.e., for the changed thing).

### <span class="fac">[66b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

If we vary the concept of pretending, we must retain its inwardness, i.e., the possibility of confession. But we do not always have to believe the confession, & a false confession does not have to be deceit.

### <span class="fac">[66b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

Others, although related to our concepts, could seem _very_ strange to us: namely, a deviation from the usual _in an unusual direction_.

### <span class="fac">[66b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/66b.webp)</span>

“You don’t understand anything!” This is what one says when someone doubts that what we clearly recognize as genuine is genuine.

### <span class="fac">[67a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp)</span>

“You don’t understand anything” – but we cannot prove anything.

### <span class="fac">[67a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp)</span>

What does “You don’t understand anything” actually mean? How should I explain it? Would I not have to teach the other person understanding for the arts, and a thousand other things? That is, understanding that expression is only possible in a very specific life; which I cannot describe – although I recognize _deviations_ from it.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-67a.3) <span class="fac">[67a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp)</span> {#67a3}

If one person can believe in God with complete certainty, why not in the other person’s mind?

### <span class="fac">[67a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp)</span>

What I am doing here does not look like logic, and yet it is logic.

### <span class="fac">[67a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp)</span>

04.07.1948

The expressive musical phrase, it cannot be recognized by rules. And why can we not imagine that it is for other beings?

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-67a.6) <span class="fac">[67a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp)</span> {#67a6}

This musical phrase is a gesture for me. It creeps into my life. I make it my own.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-67a.7+67b.1) <span class="fac">[67a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67a.webp),[67b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67b.webp)</span> {#67a767b1}

The human variations of life are essential to our life, & thus to the custom of life. Expression _consists_ for us in unpredictability. If I knew exactly how he would contort his face & move it, then there would be no facial expression, no gesture. – But is that correct? – I can listen to a piece of music that I know (completely) by heart again & again; & it could also be played on a music box. His gestures would remain gestures for me, even though I always know what is coming. Yes, I can even be surprised again & again. (In a certain sense.)

### <span class="fac">[67b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67b.webp)</span>

But one does call something “hackneyed.” And one says, “This melody is always new.” So, would it be possible that these precisely predictable gestures always seem new to us? How should I put it? This is certain: a stiff, puppet-like gesture is not a gesture for us.

### <span class="fac">[67b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67b.webp)</span>

Even that would make a strange and deep impression on us if we were to come to people who only knew music box music. We might also expect some kind of gestures from them that we would not understand, to which we would not know how to react.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-67b.4) <span class="fac">[67b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67b.webp)</span> {#67b4}

05.07.1948

The honest religious thinker is like a tightrope walker. He walks, apparently, almost only on air. His ground is the narrowest that can be imagined. And yet one can really walk on it.

### <span class="fac">[67b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/67b.webp),[68a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

‘The authenticity of expression cannot be proven?’ ‘One must feel it.’ But what happens with it? If one says, “This is how a truly moved heart expresses itself,” and if he also converts another to his view, what further consequences does it have? Well, consequences can be imagined in a vague way. The attention of the other is directed differently.

### <span class="fac">[68a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

Could one imagine that, in other people, what cannot be proven in us, could be proven? Or would it, by that very fact, change its essence to the point of being unrecognizable?

### <span class="fac">[68a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

What is essential for us is the spontaneous agreement, the spontaneous sympathy.

### <span class="fac">[68a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

[I am very stupid in all these considerations; but I do not know how to get out of my stupidity.]

### <span class="fac">[68a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

Let us imagine who would not perceive this expression as genuine, who would not be convinced by it; these people would be placed in an institution for the mentally disabled. Whoever, at that and that age, distrusts _him_, or trusts _him_, is treated as mentally disabled.

### <span class="fac">[68a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

But what about pretending and confession? These could exist, but in such a way that they would change their position in human life. Distrust could turn into trust and trust into distrust, but not quite as it is with us, and according to stricter rules.

### <span class="fac">[68a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp)</span>

They would lack infinite flexibility.

### <span class="fac">[68a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68a.webp),[68b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

06.07.1948

‘These people would not have anything human.’ Why? We could not possibly communicate with them. Not even in the way we can with a dog. We could not find ourselves in them.

### <span class="fac">[68b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

And yet, there could be such beings, otherwise human.

### <span class="fac">[68b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

There are countless nuances in human life that are important to us, and our concepts seem to take them into account.

### <span class="fac">[68b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

“One cannot _know_ it. One can _believe_ it. Believe it with all one’s soul, but not know.” Then the difference does _not_ lie in the certainty of the convinced person. It must lie elsewhere; in the _logic_ of the question.

### <span class="fac">[68b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

How _can_ I know it, since _he_ feels it? But how does he know what he feels?

### <span class="fac">[68b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

But is my knowledge not necessarily fluctuating? Just a certain hunch and no knowledge? But how can I even _guess_ it? _What_ do I guess?

### <span class="fac">[68b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/68b.webp)</span>

Imagine that people could see the functioning of the nervous system in another. They would then distinguish between genuine and feigned sensation in a reliable way. Or could they doubt again that the other person is feeling something at these signs? One could, in any case, imagine that what they see determines their behavior without any scruples. And now one can transfer this to external behavior.

### <span class="fac">[69a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69a.webp)</span>

“The inner is hidden from us.” But how could it ever become apparent to us? And yet it can. It is as if it were wrapped in thick clothes, which sometimes allow us to recognize its shape, sometimes not. – But how could I know when I recognize the inner from the outer, and when not?

### <span class="fac">[69a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69a.webp)</span>

There is indeed the case that one person reveals his inner self to me later through a confession: but this cannot explain the nature of the outer and the inner, because I must, after all, believe the confession.

### <span class="fac">[69a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69a.webp)</span>

The confession is also something external.

### <span class="fac">[69a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69a.webp)</span>

People who can see how the nerves function: _Must_ I think that they could also have the best understanding of the inner workings? But does that mean: Can I not think of external signs that seem sufficient to me for a _reliable_ judgment about the inner workings?

### <span class="fac">[69a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69a.webp)</span>

Imagine a tribe of people who normally have several strictly separate natures, characters. Each could transform into a certain hour of the day, and each receives several names, one for each character. A novel could therefore begin with them: a man with the names Jekyll and Hyde has such and such natures.

### <span class="fac">[69b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69b.webp)</span>

Think that it would be physiologically impossible to forge the expression of feeling _exactly_, & that people – at least in general – are gifted with the ability to distinguish between the genuine & the fake. Then there would be various cases. Certain people might not be able to learn to perceive the nuances. Such people would be abnormal, like colorblind people. One could not deceive normal people in a certain way.

### <span class="fac">[69b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69b.webp)</span>

But now say: “It could be that something feels something, even if the physiological signs are completely against it;” well, then those people simply have a different concept and are not familiar with _these_ scruples.

### <span class="fac">[69b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69b.webp)</span>

I always make auxiliary constructions that are ultimately supposed to fall away from the consideration.

### <span class="fac">[69b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/69b.webp),[70a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp)</span>

Imagine that the people of a tribe from an earlier era are brought up to show _absolutely no_ emotional expression. It is something childish for them, to be dismissed. The training is extremely strict. One does not talk about ‘pain’; let alone in the form of a conjecture: “Perhaps he does indeed ‥‥”. The injured person, or the one showing symptoms of illness, is helped without any expression of compassion. If someone complains, they are laughed at or punished. There is no suspicion of pretense. Training to speak in an expressionless, monotonous way, to move in a stiff manner.

### <span class="fac">[70a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp)</span>

I mean: a completely different upbringing could also be the basis for completely different concepts.

### <span class="fac">[70a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp)</span>

Because life would unfold differently here. – What interests us would not interest _them_. Other concepts would no longer be unimaginable. Yes, other concepts are only imaginable in this way.

### <span class="fac">[70a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp)</span>

“Everyone knows more about himself than the other knows.” “Everyone knows himself better than the other knows him.” “Everyone can instruct the other about himself, if he wants.” This is not always the case.

### <span class="fac">[70a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp)</span>

Must one assume that the person who is putting on an act _knows_ that he is putting on an act? What if people considered him an idiot & therefore simply shrugged their shoulders at whatever might be going on in him?

### <span class="fac">[70a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp)</span>

07.07.1948

It is not the fact that the evidence merely makes the other person’s feeling probable that matters, but rather that we regard _this_ as evidence for something, that we base a statement on _this_ highly complicated kind of evidence, that _it_ therefore has a special importance in our lives & (for this reason) is highlighted by a concept.

### <span class="fac">[70a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70a.webp),[70b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span>

“Pretending,” some people might say, “what a ridiculous concept!”

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-70b.2) <span class="fac">[70b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span> {#70b2}

Firm belief. (In a promise, for example.) Is it less certain than the conviction of a mathematical truth? – But does this make the language-games more similar?

### <span class="fac">[70b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span>

Could not the behavior, conduct, of trust exist quite generally among certain people? So that a doubt about expressions of feeling is completely alien to them?

### <span class="fac">[70b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span>

Musical simultaneity and simultaneity according to the clock, the metronome.

### <span class="fac">[70b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span>

08.07.1948

Consider again the case of those who can see the workings of the nerves – or who understand how to read facial expressions more accurately than we do. – And it comes to the same thing if they only _believe_ they can. That is, they treat people helpfully, or with mockery, depending on their facial expression. – Or: depending on the expression & certain other circumstances.

### <span class="fac">[70b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span>
