But wouldn't it then only be loosely consistent with our own, in which we distinguish between those to be helped & those to be laughed at, according to genuine & feigned feeling? Of course.

### <span class="fac">[70b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp)</span>

09.07.1948

Is anything else to be expected?

### <span class="fac">[70b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/70b.webp),[71a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71a.webp)</span>

But the agreement in judgment could indeed be very precise. Only, I feel: it would then only be accidental. The basis for the distinction would be different. Namely, in our case, a completely different phenomenon than in theirs. But here I am probably in the old, bad waters. Where the word corresponds to the object, & a different object corresponds to the word with a different meaning. – Because, the fact that the word which, in their case, corresponds to our “pain” is _used differently_, is a prerequisite. (What else would we be talking about?) The fact that there is certainty in their case, where there is uncertainty in our case, is also a prerequisite.

### <span class="fac">[71a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71a.webp)</span>

But consider: why should someone have to pretend, are there not other possibilities? Can he not dream? Can the situation not become confused in other ways? (_Couvade._) Remember how often one cannot say: someone is honest, or dishonest; sincere, or insincere. (A politician, for example.) Well-meaning, or the opposite. How many stupid questions are asked about this! How often _the concepts do not fit_! (Was Hitler well-meaning or not, selfish or not? Is a crocodile cruel?)

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-71a.3) <span class="fac">[71a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71a.webp)</span> {#71a3}

It is important for our consideration that there are people, about whom someone feels that he will never know what is going on in them. He will never understand them. (Englishwomen for Europeans.)

### <span class="fac">[71a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71a.webp),[71b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp)</span>

11.07.1948

I think a lot about the recent time with Francis; about my disgust with him. I was very unhappy at the time; but _with a bad heart_. I cannot see how I can ever be relieved of this guilt in my life.

### <span class="fac">[71b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp)</span>

The most difficult thing is: to express the _ignorance_ correctly.

### <span class="fac">[71b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp)</span>

We are certainly _inclined_ to say that the complaint is only a sign, a symptom of the real thing, which is only empirically connected with it. And if we make a mistake here, this strong temptation must still have its justification & indeed in the law of evidence, which we allow.

### <span class="fac">[71b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp)</span>

The complaint, etc., points to something else. (Inner.)

### <span class="fac">[71b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp)</span>

One could ask the question: what kind of law must the law of permitted evidence be, so that this view seems obvious to us?

### <span class="fac">[71b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp)</span>

One would like to give the answer: the evidence must be fluctuating. Multifaceted?

### <span class="fac">[71b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/71b.webp),[72a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp)</span>

The complaint points to something, makes this probable. The connection between the external signs & our behaviour must be a very loose one. It would have to be very difficult to explain to someone who does not understand it. Namely, the connection between my trust & his behaviour.

### <span class="fac">[72a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp)</span>

There is feigned expression, but there must also be evidence for this pretense. Even if we often simply do not know what to say, we must sometimes tend towards an opinion, sometimes have certainty. So there must be external evidence.

### <span class="fac">[72a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp)</span>

“Voilà comment s'exprime un coeur vraiment épris.” How would such a sentence be used? “_This_ is what the real expression looks like.”

### <span class="fac">[72a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp)</span>

But that is really how it is. Inexperienced or visually impaired people are fooled by the unreal, experienced people are not. Of course, there are disagreements even among experienced people. But could one not imagine them away?

### <span class="fac">[72a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp)</span>

So that, for example, only children & mentally ill people are deceived. As one can make a false calculation for them.

### <span class="fac">[72a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp)</span>

You say that you care for the groaning person, because experience has taught you that you yourself groan when you feel this & that. But since you do not draw such a conclusion, we can leave out the justification by analogy.

### <span class="fac">[72a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72a.webp),[72b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp)</span>

Asked why you are helping him, you will say: “he groans” (not, or hardly ever, you exclude this from your own experience ‥‥․); or you will say, he is in _pain_. But how is this instrument used?

### <span class="fac">[72b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp)</span>

I believe it would be a significant deviation if people had not a language of sounds, but only a language of fingers or writing, that there was not a noun “table” & one for “pain.”

### <span class="fac">[72b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp)</span>

People for whom the recognition of the person is always more or less doubtful.

### <span class="fac">[72b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp)</span>

12.07.1948

The fact that one ‘cannot know it,’ & yet knows, that is the paradox.

### <span class="fac">[72b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp)</span>

“You don’t know it. Yes, you don’t doubt, you are certain, but you don’t know it!” And _why_ not? – Because you don’t see its inner nature. – The old difficulty. It is a language-game that presents itself to us for _comparison_.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-72b.6) <span class="fac">[72b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp)</span> {#72b6}

14.07.1948

I think it is an important & remarkable fact that a musical theme, when played in (very) different tempos, changes its _character_. Transition from quantity to quality.

### <span class="fac">[72b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/72b.webp),[73a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

17.07.1948

I don’t feel happy in Rosro. I have no good ideas, & I’m working at a snail’s pace. However, this cannot be explained by external circumstances, as I have worked better under worse circumstances. It is probably now a phenomenon of aging. But I cannot draw any conclusions from it right now. I _believe_ I must still _wait_. I am not wise enough to decide anything under the current circumstances. – I have decided to make improvements to the kitchen here. These are expensive, & it’s crazy that I’m having them done, as I will probably find it almost impossible to spend the winter here. But I have decided to _try_. The man on whom I depend _entirely_ here is unreliable! – I pray a lot. But whether in the right _spirit_, I don’t know. – Without the kindness of X & Y, I could _not_ live here.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

19.07.1948

I have been working better for three days. I understand what I have written better.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

20.07.1948

The manual work here in Rosro makes me _very_ tired. Now more than ever. And that is _probably_ bad for my work, but I don’t know.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

Hearing the _direction_ of a sound is a remarkably important example for this investigation. It can be brought together with the ‘feeling’ of the place of the sensation of pain, etc. & with the ‘feeling’ of the location & movement of body parts.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

Even from writing with the alphabet reversed, one could learn something, about arbitrary & involuntary movements, about the act of **will**.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

Similar to the concept of ‘tendency’ & ‘differential quotient’.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp)</span>

That this or that sentence has no sense is of importance in philosophy, but also that it sounds strange.

### <span class="fac">[73a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73a.webp),[73b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp)</span>

21.07.1948

Can one call ‘knowing one’s way around’ an experience? No. But there are experiences characteristic of the state of knowing one’s way around & of not knowing one’s way around. Not knowing one’s way around & lying.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-73b.2) <span class="fac">[73b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp)</span> {#73b2}

25.07.1948

The problems of life are insoluble on the surface, & can only be solved in the depths. In the dimensions of the surface, they are insoluble.

### <span class="fac">[73b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp)</span>

It seems that I am not able to learn wisdom. I always have the same unwise thoughts. I can only dive into the depths for short moments and otherwise swim on the surface.

### <span class="fac">[73b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp)</span>

Is “I hope ‥‥” a description of a state of mind? A state of mind has a duration. So if I say “I hoped all day ‥‥,” that is such a description. But if I say to someone “I hope you come” – how, if he asked me: “How long have you hoped it?”? Is the answer: “I hoped while I was saying it”? Suppose I had some answer to this question, would it not be completely irrelevant to the purpose of the words “I hope you will come”?

### <span class="fac">[73b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp)</span>

26.07.1948

A scream is not the description of a state of mind, although one can infer a state of mind from it.

### <span class="fac">[73b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp)</span>

Description is what requires attention.

### <span class="fac">[73b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/73b.webp),[74a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74a.webp)</span>

One does not scream for help because one is aware of one’s own anxiety.

### <span class="fac">[74a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74a.webp)</span>

And just as little are the utterances of fear, “I am afraid,” of wish, “I wish,” of hope, “I hope,” descriptions of states of mind. However, the sentences “I am less afraid now than I used to be,” “I have wished for it since yesterday,” “I keep hoping…” are such descriptions. (One describes a sequence of events.)

### <span class="fac">[74a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74a.webp)</span>

‘Paying attention’ is part of ‘describing’.

### <span class="fac">[74a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74a.webp)</span>

And just as little are the _linguistic_ utterances of fear, wish, and hope, _descriptions_ of states of mind. However, the sentences “I am less afraid of him now than I used to be,” “I have wished for it for a long time,” “I keep hoping…” are such descriptions. (One describes a sequence of events.)

### <span class="fac">[74a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74a.webp),[74b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

27.07.1948

Do I want to say that certain facts are favorable to, or unfavorable to, certain conceptual formations? And is that an empirical fact? It is an empirical fact that people change their concepts, switch them, when they become acquainted with new facts; when, as a result, what used to be important to them becomes unimportant, perhaps. (One discovers, for example, that what used to be regarded as a difference in kind is actually _only_ a difference in degree.) [For reflection on the concept of color, etc.]

### <span class="fac">[74b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

Suppose these two butterflies, which are playing with each other there, had only one _mind_, are _one_ individual.

### <span class="fac">[74b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

If it is not a description, then it is not the verbal expression that replaces it. The utterances of fear, hope, and wish are not descriptions. However, the sentences … are.

### <span class="fac">[74b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

The past tense is part of description and narration.

### <span class="fac">[74b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

“I am in pain.” – “Since when?” In contrast: “I hope you come.” – “Since when?”

Compare: “Uninterrupted hope” & “uninterrupted pain.”

### <span class="fac">[74b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

“I hope you come” – “Since when?” – Would it be correct to answer: “_Now_; at this moment”?

### <span class="fac">[74b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/74b.webp)</span>

“I am in pain now, and I have been all day.” – In contrast: “I hope you come, and I have been hoping all day.” – Why does that sound strange?

### <span class="fac">[75a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75a.webp)</span>

What is the past of “Don’t you come!”?

### <span class="fac">[75a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75a.webp)</span>

11.08.1948

If the word “violin” referred not only to the instrument but also to the violinist, the violin sound, the violin playing – – –.

### <span class="fac">[75a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75a.webp)</span>

The confused use of psychological words. “Thinking,” for example. If the word “violin” refers not only to the instrument but sometimes also to the violinist, the violin sound, the violin playing.

### <span class="fac">[75a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75a.webp)</span>

20.08.1948

Newborn kittens are blind; should I say that jellyfish are blind? And are people seeing? Could one animal not explain to another: “Caterpillars see, earthworms do not.” Consider the _evidence_ for such a statement.

### <span class="fac">[75a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75a.webp)</span>

23.08.1948

“If p, then q” could be called a conditional prediction. That is, in the case of ~p, I make _no_ prediction. But that does not mean that what I say is made true by ~p . ~q. Or also _like_ this: There are conditional predictions, and “p ⊃ q” is _not_ one of them. [→]

### <span class="fac">[75a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75a.webp),[75b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span>

[→] Let me call the sentence “If p, then q” “S.” S⌵~S is a tautology: but is it also the law of the excluded middle? Or also like this: If I want to say that the prediction “S” can be true, false, or undecided, is that expressed by “~(S⌵~S)”?

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-75b.2) <span class="fac">[75b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span> {#75b2}

In a conversation: One throws a ball; the other does not know whether he should throw it back, or throw it to a third person, or leave it, or pick it up and put it in his pocket, etc.

### <span class="fac">[75b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span>

25.08.1948

The use of the words “to consider,” “to observe.” And now of the expression “to consider oneself”(!).

### <span class="fac">[75b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span>

“I am afraid!” & “It seems to me that I am afraid.”

### <span class="fac">[75b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span>

A: “I am afraid of him.” B: “Do you mean in general, or just now?” A: “Both.”

### <span class="fac">[75b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span>

“I am afraid of him” & “I usually am afraid of him.” But even the expression “I usually” could mean _various_ things here. However, there might be a language in whose conjugations _much_ more differences are taken into account than in ours.

### <span class="fac">[75b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp)</span>

Difference of _purpose_ between the utterance of fear “I am afraid!” & the report of fear “I am afraid.”

### <span class="fac">[75b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/75b.webp),[76a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span>

Can one explain: “If someone has _such_ thoughts & behaves in such & such a way (a conglomerate), then we say that he is afraid (expects someone, etc.)”? But does “I am afraid” mean: I have this kind of thought, I behave in such & such a way? – Fearful behaviour, fearful thoughts, fearful sensations certainly form a conglomerate, but this does not mean “fear”.

### <span class="fac">[76a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span>

“Knowing” can mean something similar to “being able” (knowing by heart), or it can mean “being certain.”

### <span class="fac">[76a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span>

“I know” can mean something similar to “I can,” but also something similar to “I am certain.”

### <span class="fac">[76a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span>

No one except a philosopher would say “I know that I have two hands”; however, one can say: “I am not in a position to doubt that I have two hands.” But “knowing” is not usually used in this sense.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-76a.5) <span class="fac">[76a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span> {#76a5}

19.10.1948

The great architect in a bad period (Van der Nüll) has a completely different task than the great architect in a good period. One must not again be misled by the general term. Take not the comparability, but the incomparability as something self-evident.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-76a.6) <span class="fac">[76a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp)</span> {#76a6}

22.10.1948

A language in which there is a word “to be afraid” which means: to torment oneself with fearful thoughts. – And now one could, for example, assume that this verb does not have a first person in the present tense. The English “I am …ing.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-76a.7+76b.1) <span class="fac">[76a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76a.webp),[76b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#76a776b1}

If I say to someone “I hope you will come,” is it less urgent if the hoping lasted only 30 seconds than if it lasted 2 minutes? “I am glad that you succeeded!” – “How long are you glad?” A strange question. But it could have sense. The answer could be: “Whenever I think of it,” or “At first, I wasn’t glad about it, but then I was,” or “I keep thinking about it and I’m glad,” or “It only occurs to me for moments, but then I’m glad,” etc. One also says “It is a lasting joy to me,” and “For a moment, I was glad about his misfortune.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-76b.2) <span class="fac">[76b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#76b2}

“I move the knight” – “How long do you move it?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-76b.3) <span class="fac">[76b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#76b3}

As an example of the sentential form “If p, then q,” consider: “If he comes, I will tell him.” If he now _does not_ come, – have I thereby kept my promise? – have I broken it? – Can one say that this sentence asserts a ‘connection’? Would I answer him with “It _doesn’t_ have to be”? It is not as if the sentence were: “If _these_ two meet, there will be a fight.” Here, that answer would be possible.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-76b.4) <span class="fac">[76b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp)</span> {#76b4}

But if the material implication is asserted (& there is such a case!) – can one also answer “It does not have to be” to “p ⊃ q”? And what does that mean here?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-76b.5+77a.1) <span class="fac">[76b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/76b.webp),[77a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp)</span> {#76b577a1}

“When the two poles come close together, a spark jumps across” – What does one consider to be a verification of the sentence? The observation that they never come close together? – Can what we want to say here be expressed with the material implication? Certainly not; but perhaps with the ‘formal’ one? But no more than that. – What we want to state is after all a kind of law of nature; the kind of observation that leads to it is easy enough to imagine. One has observed that a spark always jumps across when they come close to each other. – Is the sentence perhaps of the kind “(x)․φx ⊃ ψx:(∃x)․φx”? If not, then this sentence must have some application, even if not the same one.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77a.2) <span class="fac">[77a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp)</span> {#77a2}

“If he comes, I will tell him…” is an _intention_, a _promise_. If it is not to be a false promise, it must not be based on the certainty that he will not come. It is neither a material nor a formal implication.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77a.3) <span class="fac">[77a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp)</span> {#77a3}

In the case of a scientific conditional prediction, one could distinguish between _warrant_ & _correctness_. One could call it “warranted” if it follows from a theory that is justified in such & such a way. So, if the antecedent does not hold true, then one can say: _were_ it to hold true, then _it would be_ ‥‥‥ But that does not give one the right to say that the antecedent has not come true.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77a.4+77b.1) <span class="fac">[77a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77a.webp),[77b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#77a477b1}

Must a sentence like “Every body is in motion…” (the law of inertia) be formulated in the form “if – then”? “If something is a body, then it is in motion…” – Or must it say: “There are bodies; and if something is a body, then…?” (No one would think of expressing it that way.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77b.2) <span class="fac">[77b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#77b2}

23.10.1948

It is obvious that one could simply have an _application_ of the concept of “fear” to animals, & that the term would lack the first person. Its third person would be used very similarly to the third person of “to fear.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77b.3) <span class="fac">[77b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#77b3}

24.10.1948

Remember that the subjunctive has no sense except in a conditional sentence. If someone says “I _would have_ won this game,” one will ask: “_If_ –?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77b.4) <span class="fac">[77b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#77b4}

There is nothing more difficult than considering concepts without prejudice. For prejudice is a kind of understanding. And to give up on that, when it is precisely what matters to us, –.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77b.5) <span class="fac">[77b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp)</span> {#77b5}

The English “I’m furious” is not an expression of self-observation. Similarly in German, “Ich bin wütend”; but not “Ich bin zornig”. (“Horribly, my fury changes in my breast ‥‥”. It is a trembling of fury.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-77b.6+78a.1) <span class="fac">[77b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/77b.webp),[78a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#77b678a1}

One asks oneself: “What does ‘I am afraid’ actually mean? What am I thinking?” And, of course, no answer comes, or one that clearly does not suffice. The question is: “In what kind of context is it?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78a.2) <span class="fac">[78a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#78a2}

One could also say: “I just say it.” For this only means: Don’t worry about something that accompanies the speaking.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78a.3) <span class="fac">[78a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#78a3}

Can the utterance not stand in different contexts? Which give it one face one time, and another face another time?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78a.4) <span class="fac">[78a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp)</span> {#78a4}

I say “I am afraid…,” the other person asks me: “What did you mean by that? Was it like an exclamation; or did you allude to your state in the last few hours; did you simply want to communicate something?” Can I always give him a clear answer? Can I never give him one? – I will sometimes have to say: “I thought about how I spent today and, as it were, shook my head at myself” – but sometimes: “It was: Oh God! If only I weren’t so afraid!” – or: “It was just a cry of fear” – or: “I wanted you to know how I feel.” – Such explanations really do sometimes follow the utterance. But one could not _always_ give them.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78a.5+78b.1) <span class="fac">[78a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78a.webp),[78b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#78a578b1}

One could imagine people who, as it were, think much more precisely than we do, and who use a lot of different words, sometimes the one, sometimes the other.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78b.2), [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-78b.2) <span class="fac">[78b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#78b2}

Nothing is more important than the formation of fictional concepts, which teach us to understand our own concepts.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78b.3) <span class="fac">[78b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#78b3}

“What is fear?” – “The _phenomena_ & occasions of fear are _these_: – – –” “So is ‘I am afraid – – –’ a description of my state?” It can be used in such a context & with such an intention. But if I simply want to communicate my fear to someone, then it is not such a description.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78b.4) <span class="fac">[78b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#78b4}

October 25, 1948

“I am afraid” can, for example, simply be said to explain my way of acting. It is then far from being a moan; it can even be said with a smile.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78b.5) <span class="fac">[78b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp)</span> {#78b5}

One asks oneself: “What does ‘I am afraid’ actually mean, what do I aim at (with it)?” And, of course, no answer comes, or one that is not sufficient. The question is: “In what kind of context is it?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-78b.6+79a.1) <span class="fac">[78b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/78b.webp),[79a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#78b679a1}

No answer comes if one wants to answer the question “What do I aim at,” “What am I thinking,” etc., by saying the words and, as it were, paying attention to oneself from the corner of one’s eye, observing one’s mind. However, I can, in a particular case, ask: “Why did I say that, what did I want with it?” and could also answer the question, but not on the basis of an observation of the accompanying phenomena of speaking. And my answer would supplement the earlier utterance, paraphrase it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79a.2) <span class="fac">[79a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#79a2}

What is fear? What does it mean to “be afraid”? If I wanted to explain it by means of _one_ example, I would _play_ fear.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79a.3) <span class="fac">[79a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#79a3}

Could I also represent hope in this way? Hardly. Or even belief?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79a.4) <span class="fac">[79a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#79a4}

“I think he will come.” “I keep saying to myself: ‘He will come.’” For the second one, people might have their own verb.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79a.5) <span class="fac">[79a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#79a5}

Describing my state of mind (e.g., fear) is something I do in a very specific context. (Just as a particular action is only an experiment in a specific context. Is it really so surprising that I use the same expression in different games? And sometimes even as if between the games? “I was thinking of him” & “I was thinking about him” means something quite different.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79a.6) <span class="fac">[79a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79a.webp)</span> {#79a6}

And do I always speak with a very specific intention? – And is what I say therefore senseless?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.1) <span class="fac">[79b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#79b1}

“Now that you mention it: I think he’ll come.” I believe now, you are right: he would come.” “No. I am convinced: he will come.” One can imagine a context for all such expressions.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.2) <span class="fac">[79b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#79b2}

What does it take to describe a state of mind? – Or might I ask: what does it take to _want_ to describe one’s state of mind?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.3) <span class="fac">[79b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#79b3}

One could also ask: “What must it depend on then?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.4) <span class="fac">[79b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#79b4}

I wanted to describe my state of mind to you” – as opposed to “I just wanted to give vent to my feelings.” So I wanted him to know ‘how I feel’ (which often involves stating the duration of the state).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.5) <span class="fac">[79b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#79b5}

It is, after all, something else: calmly _admitting_ one’s fear – & giving it free _expression_. The words may be the same, but the tone & the gestures are different.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.6) <span class="fac">[79b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp)</span> {#79b6}

When, in a eulogy, it says “We mourn our ‥‥․,” this is meant to give expression to the grief; not to communicate something to us. But in a different context, these words are a communication. In a _prayer_ at the graveside, they could also be a _kind_ of communication.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-79b.7+80a.1) <span class="fac">[79b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/79b.webp),[80a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#79b780a1}

We don’t necessarily say that one is _complaining_ simply because one says one is in pain. Thus, the words “I am in pain” can be a complaint & also something else. (And it is similar with the expression of fear & other emotional states.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80a.2) <span class="fac">[80a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#80a2}

But if “I am afraid” is always something similar to a complaint, why should it always be a description of my state of mind? What is a complaint?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80a.3) <span class="fac">[80a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#80a3}

Because what distinguishes the complaint “I have pain” from a mere communication? It is the intention. And this will perhaps also be expressed in the tone.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80a.4) <span class="fac">[80a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#80a4}

The circumstances in which a sentence stands are best represented in a drama, hence the best example of expression in a specific meaning is a quote from a drama. And who asks the person in the drama what he experiences while speaking?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80a.5) <span class="fac">[80a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp)</span> {#80a5}

October 26, 1948

“You must know – I am afraid.” “You must know – I am apprehensive.” Yes, one can also say it in a smiling tone.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80a.6+80b.1) <span class="fac">[80a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80a.webp),[80b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#80a680b1}

And do you want to tell me that he doesn’t feel it? How else would he _know_ it? – But even if it is a communication, he doesn’t read it off from his inner self. He couldn’t even then use his _sensations_ as proof for his statement. _They_ don’t teach it to him.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80b.2) <span class="fac">[80b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#80b2}

For consider the sensations evoked by the _gestures_ of horror: the words “I am horrified” are also a gesture, & when I hear & feel their utterance, this belongs to those other sensations. Why should the unspoken gesture motivate the spoken one?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80b.3) <span class="fac">[80b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#80b3}

[→ ] We learn to use the word “thinking” _under certain circumstances_. If the circumstances are different, we do not know how to use it. – But that does not mean that we must be able to describe those circumstances.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80b.4) <span class="fac">[80b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp)</span> {#80b4}

“If people strongly disagreed in their color statements, they could not use our concept of color.” – If people strongly disagreed in their color statements, then, precisely because of that, they would not use _our concept of color_. They would not be playing our language-game: for consider how one would have to compare theirs and ours!

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-80b.5+81a.1) <span class="fac">[80b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/80b.webp),[81a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#80b581a1}

So, when I hear someone say “I am afraid,” how can I know whether this is a ‘description of a state of mind’ or something else? Should I ask him, & will he certainly understand the question? – But he could answer it. How? For example, _like this_: “No; I just let off some steam,” or “Yes; I want you to know how I feel.” But such a question is hardly ever asked. Isn’t it because the tone & the context must provide the answer? For from these, one can see whether he is, for example, making fun of his own fear; whether he is, as it were, discovering it within himself; whether he is confessing it to me unwillingly but for the sake of openness; whether he is expressing it like a cry, etc. – & don’t the words, however they are uttered, instruct me about the same state of affairs, namely his state of mind?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81a.2) <span class="fac">[81a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#81a2}

Does the sentence “Napoleon was crowned in 1804” have a different meaning depending on whether I say it to someone to inform him, or in a history exam to show what I know, or etc. etc.? In order to understand it, the meanings of its words must be explained to me in the same way for all these purposes. And if the meaning of the words and their combination constitute the meaning of the sentence, – – –.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81a.3) <span class="fac">[81a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#81a3}

The problem is this: the cry, which cannot be called a description, which is more primitive than any description, nevertheless serves the purpose of a description of the state of mind.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81a.4) <span class="fac">[81a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp)</span> {#81a4}

October 27, 1948.

Whoever can cry can’t necessarily communicate something to someone in conversation by doing so.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81a.5+81b.1) <span class="fac">[81a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81a.webp),[81b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#81a581b1}

I hear someone say, “I am afraid.” I ask: “In what context did you say that? Was it a gasp, was it a confession, was it self-observation, ‥‥․?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81b.2) <span class="fac">[81b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#81b2}

Does someone who cries “Help!” want to describe how they feel? Nothing is further from their intention than to describe something.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81b.3) <span class="fac">[81b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#81b3}

But there are transitions from what we would not call a description to what we would call a description.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81b.4) <span class="fac">[81b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#81b4}

The phrase “description of a state of mind” characterizes a certain _game_. And if I only hear the words “I am afraid,” I may indeed _guess_ what game is being played here (from the tone, for example), but I will only know it when I know the context.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-81b.5) <span class="fac">[81b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp)</span> {#81b5}

For what we call “describing” includes one or another of a class of characteristics. The observing, considering, remembering behavior, an effort for accuracy, the ability to improve, the comparing. A cry is not a description. But there are transitions. And the words “I am afraid” can be closer to or further from a cry. They can be very close to it and _very_ far from it.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-81b.6+82a.1) <span class="fac">[81b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/81b.webp),[82a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#81b682a1}

“Thinking is difficult” (Ward). What does that really mean? Why is it difficult? – It is almost as if one said, “Looking is difficult.” For strained looking is difficult. And one can look intently and yet see nothing, or believe that one is seeing something again and again, and yet not be able to see clearly. One can get tired from looking, even if one sees nothing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82a.2) <span class="fac">[82a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#82a2}

[→] If a careful listening shows me that in that game I experience the word “soft” sometimes _so_, sometimes _so_, – doesn’t it also show me that, in the context of an entire sentence, which I understand and experience in some sense, I often don’t experience that word itself?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82a.3) <span class="fac">[82a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#82a3}

“The meaning of the word was present in my mind.” – Would one say that if the word is in an unambiguous context?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82a.4) <span class="fac">[82a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#82a4}

A script in which the crossed-out word, the crossed-out sentence, is a sign.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82a.5) <span class="fac">[82a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#82a5}

You claim that you experienced the word, as you pronounced it just now, in that way. Then, with the same keen feeling, say whether you intend this word in the correct context, _in this sense_. For the fact that you intend it in another sense _so_ and not _so_, and will probably explain that later, is clear.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82a.6) <span class="fac">[82a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82a.webp)</span> {#82a6}

But then the question remains, why, in the _game_ of intending, we also talk about an _‘intention’_. – That is a question of a different kind. It is precisely the phenomenon of the game that we talk about an ‘intention’ only in a finite sense.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82b.1) <span class="fac">[82b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#82b1}

This is a question of a different kind. It is precisely the phenomenon of the language-game that in _this_ situation we say that we meant the word _in that way_ & transfer this expression from another language-game. _A question must have preceded it._ This is a question of a different kind. This is an improper question; as if it were of a different _race_.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82b.2) <span class="fac">[82b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#82b2}

28.10.1948

Is it as if one were posing a question of physics in a book on pure mathematics, as if that were the question you wanted to ask? Is it a misunderstanding? I am not using the word _for_ something else, but in a different situation. [Just as I do not use the word “knowledge” in two different ways when I say “I knew it in a dream.” Cf. also the feeling of unreality.] Is the technique of its application taught to me differently here?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82b.3) <span class="fac">[82b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#82b3}

I hear a Beethoven piece & say “Beethoven!” – Does the word have a different meaning here than in the sentence “Beethoven was born in Bonn in 1770”? (If one did not understand the tone of the exclamation, one could perhaps explain it: “Only Beethoven would write like that.”)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82b.4) <span class="fac">[82b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#82b4}

Would it be more correct to say that _e_ ‘corresponds’ to yellow, rather than “_e_ is yellow”? Isn’t it precisely the trick of the language-game that we say _e is_ yellow? Yes, if there were someone who was _inclined_ to say that _e_ ‘_corresponds_’ to yellow & not that it is yellow, would he not be almost as different from the other as someone for whom vowels & colors are not connected? And similarly for the experience of meaning.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-82b.5) <span class="fac">[82b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/82b.webp)</span> {#82b5}

If, when learning the word “bank,” & in order to impress its double meaning upon myself, I alternately looked at the picture of a bench & a money bank & always said “bank,” or “This is a bank,” – would the ‘experience of meaning’ take place here? Certainly not, I would say. But if, for example, it seemed to me that the tone of my pronunciation indicated that I meant one or the other, – then yes.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83a.1) <span class="fac">[83a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#83a1}

[→] It is not as if two things were persistently designated with the same word, & one asked: Why does one do this, if they are _really_ different? – The new use consists precisely in the fact that the old expression is used in a new situation; not to designate something new.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83a.2) <span class="fac">[83a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#83a2}

The experience of the ‘appropriate word’. Is this the same as the experience of ‘meaning’?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83a.3) <span class="fac">[83a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#83a3}

“Why does one call _this_ a ‘knowledge’ in a dream?” – One does not call anything a knowledge in a dream, but says “I knew in my dream ‥‥” Why does one call this ‘meaning’ & ‘meaning,’ when it is not about meaning? – What do I call a ‘meaning’ in the language-game: I _say_ “I meant this with the word now ‥‥․”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83a.4) <span class="fac">[83a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#83a4}

So I cannot say: I simply designate two related things with the same word. (For otherwise the problem would never have arisen.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83a.5) <span class="fac">[83a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp)</span> {#83a5}

But what do I call it? – An experience? And which experience? Can I describe it in any other way than through the expression: I ‘mean’ this word _in this way_ now?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83a.6+83b.1) <span class="fac">[83a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83a.webp),[83b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#83a683b1}

“Why do we also talk about a ‘meaning’ in that language-game?” – What am I asking for? For a reason, for a cause? – Certainly not for a consideration that determines me to speak like this; nor for a justification; for it is not about anything of the sort.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83b.2) <span class="fac">[83b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#83b2}

29.10.1948

[→] Call it a dream!

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83b.3) <span class="fac">[83b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#83b3}

But then the question remains, why does one, in the _game_ of ‘meaning’, also use the word “meaning”? Could one use a different word? Does one use the same word _for_: something else? Could one give a different explanation of it?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83b.4) <span class="fac">[83b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#83b4}

Call it a dream. It changes nothing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83b.5) <span class="fac">[83b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp)</span> {#83b5}

30.10.1948

“Schubert” – It is as if the name were an adjective.

[→ ] One cannot say, “Look at all that _fits_. For example, the name also fits the bearer.” [→ ] An addition would be an extension; & an extension is precisely what is _not_ the case here. Because one does not call a ‘fitting together’ what is _actually_ not a fitting together. It is as if one were merely extending this concept. Rather, there is something like a _deception_, a reflection. We believe we are seeing what is not there. But it is only _as if_ so. – We know very well that the name “Schubert” has no relationship of fitting to its bearer & to Schubert’s works; & yet we are under compulsion to express ourselves in this way.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-83b.6+84a.1) <span class="fac">[83b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/83b.webp),[84a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp)</span> {#83b684a1}

One sees something under the image, under the concept, under the idea of fitting. One can see one thing as a variation of another. And now, in the most extreme case, what one sees _as_ a variation may have no similarity at all with what it is seen as a variation of. – Let's put it this way: first, _this_ figure is a simple projection of _that_. Then the projection rays curve a little; but for me, it is still a projection. Finally, they twist to the point of being unrecognizable, but for me, it is still the projection (As some still see the young in the old, in the completely changed person, they still see the former person). It may seem odd to connect the case of the personal name with this. But one can make a connection. Namely, one _sees_ the personal name as an image.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84a.2) <span class="fac">[84a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp)</span> {#84a2}

Suppose I see a triangle as a square, by seeing it as the end of a change:

![](../graphics/137,84a.svg)

– Then the type of variation belongs to the seen aspect. But this is not what is happening when the name seems to be the image of the bearer.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84a.3) <span class="fac">[84a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp)</span> {#84a3}

I say something (e.g. “The name ‘Schubert’ fits perfectly with Schubert”) – it means nothing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84a.4+84b.1) <span class="fac">[84a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84a.webp),[84b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#84a484b1}

The sentence “The name ‥‥ fits ‥‥” is, as we use it, not a communication about the name, or its bearer. It is a pathological communication about the communicator. – One does not teach a child that this name fits the person.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84b.2) <span class="fac">[84b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#84b2}

October 31, 1948

Someone waves (to) me. “What did you want?” – “I wanted you to come.” That is the intention at the time of the action. The sign was the origin of a movement. Was it not also the origin of the explanation? Could this explanation itself _be_: “The waving with the hand was the origin of the explanation that I am giving you now: ‘Come to me’?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84b.3) <span class="fac">[84b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#84b3}

02.11.1948

One could not say here “He [the name] does not quite _fit_”, or “He does not seem to quite _fit_”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84b.4) <span class="fac">[84b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#84b4}

It is not as if “to fit” is not _quite_ the right word. One could, however, also use other words; for example, “There is a kinship there.”

“What is always associated, one readily takes to be related. Is that the right expression? Not quite. But it is as if they are related.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-84b.5) <span class="fac">[84b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/84b.webp)</span> {#84b5}

It is not that: “I consider them to be related, even though they are not” – for I need only, as it were, wake up in order to know that they are not. But I see them under the image of kinship. I use the word, the image. –

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85a.1) <span class="fac">[85a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#85a1}

One can, of course, explain: Fitting together and association often go together in use; and therefore that deception (if one wants to call it a deception).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85a.2) <span class="fac">[85a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#85a2}

I think a physiological explanation of the strange phenomenon has been found. One now sees how the deception came about. In the brain, something happens that also happens when ‥‥․ Joyful excitement: Now we understand why one always said ‥‥! And if the explanation has now been given, the riddle solved, what does this leave us with? It has only removed a question that does not interest us; it leaves us with the fact that we use that expression, that image, and would like to use it, where the normal reason is lacking.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85a.3) <span class="fac">[85a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#85a3}

[→ ] But then the question remains as to why, in that _game_ of believing, we also speak of a ‘_belief_’. The question does not (really) belong here. We use the word (here) because it has _this_ meaning. No other meaning would do for us. It is a fact to be accepted.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85a.4) <span class="fac">[85a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85a.webp)</span> {#85a4}

03.11.1948

But is the word now used in two meanings? No. (Otherwise, we would be obliged to provide an explanation.) Do we teach the use in two different ways?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85b.1) <span class="fac">[85b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85b.webp)</span> {#85b1}

But doesn't an explanation of the strange phenomenon still interest us? Think of other related phenomena, & what their explanation achieves. Yes, it is indeed interesting to understand _why_ I, on this walk, have the impression that the city must be _there_; even though a simple consideration can convince me that this is not the case. I now want to assume that I know how the deception can arise: I drew false conclusions from similarities between the area and another area, and so on. – But I didn't explicitly draw the false conclusion, and it is also not explained why those similarities led me to this hasty conclusion. The explanation leaves the strangeness intact. (Likewise for the phenomenon of seeing colors, etc.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85b.2) <span class="fac">[85b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85b.webp)</span> {#85b2}

If someone, when asked “Will N. return?” answers in the affirmative again and again, one can express it by saying that he is in a _state_ of believing. But no one will say that the answer “N. will return” describes the state of the person asked.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-85b.3+86a.1) <span class="fac">[85b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/85b.webp),[86a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#85b386a1}

If “I believe p” states that I am in a certain state, then so does “rp”. For the presence of the words “I believe” cannot bring it about, it can at most indicate it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.2) <span class="fac">[86a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#86a2}

A language in which “I believe that p” is expressed only through the tone of the assertion “p”. Instead of “He believes ‥‥”, it says there, “He is inclined to say ‥‥”, and there is also the assumption “Suppose I am inclined to say ‥‥”, but not an utterance “I am inclined to say ‥‥”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.3) <span class="fac">[86a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#86a3}

There are anomalies in other cases as well. One says “It will probably rain,” but not “Suppose it will probably rain, ‥‥”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.4) <span class="fac">[86a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#86a4}

Moore's paradox would not exist in that language; instead, there would be a verb that does not have a first-person present tense.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.5) <span class="fac">[86a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#86a5}

But that shouldn't surprise us. Remember that one can predict one's own future action in the utterance of an intention.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.6) <span class="fac">[86a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#86a6}

Think of the expression “I say, ‥‥” e.g. “I say, it will rain today,” which is simply equivalent to the assertion “It will be ‥‥ today.” “He says, it will ‥‥” incidentally means “He believes, it will ‥‥”. “Suppose I say ‥‥” does _not_ mean “Suppose it will be ‥‥ today.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.7) <span class="fac">[86a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp)</span> {#86a7}

Various concepts touch here and run along a path together. One must not assume that the lines are all _circles_.

![](../graphics/137,86a.svg)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86a.8+86b.1) <span class="fac">[86a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86a.webp),[86b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#86a886b1}

Whoever sees something according to a certain interpretation does not, for that reason, experience an interpretation.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86b.2) <span class="fac">[86b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#86b2}

Everyone who thinks knows how notes, pictures, which would mean nothing to another person, and which one cannot even explain oneself, can stand for thoughts, or individual features of thoughts. (The notation of the computer.) [Not yet successful.] Can stand for a thought, or individual features of the thought.

### <span class="fac">[86b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span>

[→ ] “Did you, as you said the word, mean _that_?” – “No, I was actually thinking of ‥‥.” – Is that an experience? No. Such an experience would only be of interest for the exploration of the _unconscious_ intention. [→ ]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86b.4) <span class="fac">[86b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#86b4}

That is: If I, for example, learn that he saw this and that in front of him when uttering the word, it would be possible for me to draw a conclusion about a tendency in his unconscious – What he had in mind was not his intention in the word.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-86b.5) <span class="fac">[86b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/86b.webp)</span> {#86b5}

“Did you, as you said the word, mean _that_?” – No, I was actually thinking of ….” – Is that an experience? No. An experience would not have the same interest. It could serve the psychologist in exploring the _unconscious_ intention.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87a.1) <span class="fac">[87a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#87a1}

“This plant grows from the seed, not that plant.” Imagine that one would actually express oneself in that way in a language!

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87a.2) <span class="fac">[87a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#87a2}

But what is the germ? – The experience at the time of speaking. (Also, for example, an image – as indeed it often is.) But it is not, by its nature, the germ. Nor does something become the germ through later development. So, the only thing left is that the image of the germ forces itself upon us. (Quite natural, because we want in the experience

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87a.3) <span class="fac">[87a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#87a3}

The question that must interest us is, therefore: What is the purpose of the reference to the time of speaking? What does it communicate to one?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87a.4) <span class="fac">[87a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#87a4}

“I _thought_ that you would think of him in that context.” It was not in the picture he saw before him (which was not clearly discernible), nor in the name he imagined (which could also belong to someone else). It was the chain of interpretations, of explanations. For when he says “I was thinking of ‥․,” “I meant ‥․,” he is, _thereby_, connecting to that point in time. (He is not, for example, recalling a mental image that he saw before him & now showing it to him, to demonstrate that he was thinking of ‥‥.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87a.5) <span class="fac">[87a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp)</span> {#87a5}

He does not read it from the mental image of what he was thinking.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87a.6+87b.1) <span class="fac">[87a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87a.webp),[87b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87a687b1}

He said something, and I had to think of N. When did N. occur to me? At what moment, during which of his words? – If I know at which point, what happened to me at that word? The thoughts took their origin from the word. That is where the chain began. But what makes it the chain? That I say it?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.2) <span class="fac">[87b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b2}

“I noticed that you became thoughtful at this word.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.3) <span class="fac">[87b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b3}

“As soon as I heard this word, it occurred to me.” What is the practical _meaning_ of this moment? – For I want to say: _It came to me_ as if it occurred to me upon hearing this word,” – so the subjective element does not make any difference. The question is still the same: “What are the consequences of such a _communication_?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.4) <span class="fac">[87b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b4}

04.11.1948

“I thought of him at this word.” Where lies the interest of this communication? What is the primitive reaction that corresponds to such words?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.5) <span class="fac">[87b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b5}

“By the way…” “How did you suddenly come to think of him?” – “You said… and that reminded me of him.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.6) <span class="fac">[87b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b6}

When does one say, “I am writing to _this_ person”? How does it manifest itself? How does one know it oneself?! – Am I writing to _him_ while I am writing?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.7) <span class="fac">[87b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b7}

It would almost be strange to say: “I was thinking of him while I was writing to him.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-87b.8) <span class="fac">[87b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/87b.webp)</span> {#87b8}

“We were just talking about _him_, about this person I am now pointing to. How was the speech related to him? Did I not, through these very words, establish a connection with him?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88a.1) <span class="fac">[88a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#88a1}

“I knew who they had been talking about.” – How did I know? & What was _that_ state of mind, this ‘knowledge that the conversation was about _this_ person’?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88a.2) <span class="fac">[88a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#88a2}

“Who were you talking about?” – “N.” – “My friend N.” – “The person in this photograph.” – “The one who is coming through the door now.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88a.3) <span class="fac">[88a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#88a3}

God, if he had looked into our minds, he would not have been able to see who we were talking about.

### <span class="fac">[88a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span>

Don't be afraid to write down childish things if _that_ is what you have to think about. If you haven't moved beyond these thoughts, then you can simply admit it calmly.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-88a.5) <span class="fac">[88a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#88a5}

If one cannot untangle a skein, then the wisest thing one can do is to realize it; & the most decent thing is to admit it. [Antisemitism.] What one should do to remedy the evil is _not_ clear. What one must _not_ do is clear from case to case.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88a.6) <span class="fac">[88a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#88a6}

In philosophy, one must distinguish between the sentences that express the inclination and those that provide the solution.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88a.7) <span class="fac">[88a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp)</span> {#88a7}

Incurable illness is the rule, not the exception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88a.8+88b.1) <span class="fac">[88a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88a.webp),[88b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#88a888b1}

You refer with this statement to the time of speaking. It makes a difference whether you refer to this time or to that time. (The explanation of the word does not refer to any time.)

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-88b.2) <span class="fac">[88b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#88b2}

It is remarkable that one can often call Busch’s drawings ‘metaphysical’. So, there is a way of drawing that is metaphysical. – “Seen, with the Eternal as a background,” one could say. But these lines only mean that in an entire language. And it is a language without grammar; one could not state its rules.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88b.3) <span class="fac">[88b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#88b3}

How does one begin to say that? (In what lies the importance of this question?)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88b.4) <span class="fac">[88b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#88b4}

“Why did you look at me when I said that word; were you thinking of…?” So, there is a reaction at this moment, “I was thinking of…” explains the reaction.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88b.5) <span class="fac">[88b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp)</span> {#88b5}

November 5, 1948

“With this word, both of us thought of him.” Let us assume that each of us said the same words to ourselves – & surely, that is all it can mean. But would these very words not also just be a _seed_? They must belong to a language & to a context in order to truly be the expression of the thought of him.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-88b.6+89a.1) <span class="fac">[88b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/88b.webp),[89a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#88b689a1}

It does happen that a word spoken aloud stands in the middle of an unexpressed train of thought. And this could be reported. Just as one can generally report that one has thought this & that at that time, without speaking. Whatever the interest of _this_ report is, it must also be the interest of that report; & thus also: one has thought of ‥‥ when saying the word ‥‥.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89a.2) <span class="fac">[89a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#89a2}

“At that time, I thought: I wonder if he will come.” “You looked at it so doubtfully; what did you think?” – “I thought: I wonder if he will come.” – – “Did you say these words, or similar ones, in your mind at the time?” – “No. I was strangely thinking of Piccolomini, of the scene in which…”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89a.3) <span class="fac">[89a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#89a3}

Speaking in the imagination is, in an important way, not comparable to speaking, but our language-games are similar to both. (Tennis with a ball & tennis without a ball.) And also, some mental image plays in them the role of a real picture, which can also stand in the _context_ of sentences & explanations.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89a.4) <span class="fac">[89a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp)</span> {#89a4}

That is: our language-game refers to a mental image approximately as it refers to a spoken sentence. For this is also just a sequence of sounds and does not immediately refer to anything.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89a.5+89b.1) <span class="fac">[89a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89a.webp),[89b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89a589b1}

Now the question arises: If a word is used in a specific context, can one nevertheless, in one’s thought, establish a _different_ connection – if I don’t do this, nothing abnormal happens – does my thinking then follow along with my speech?

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-89b.2) <span class="fac">[89b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89b2}

Charlemagne tried in vain in old age to learn to write: and so one can also try in vain to learn a train of thought. It will never become familiar to him.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89b.3) <span class="fac">[89b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89b3}

If my thinking once deviates from the path of speech, it normally follows the path.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89b.4) <span class="fac">[89b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89b4}

If everything proceeds in its normal course, no one thinks of something that accompanies the speech.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89b.5) <span class="fac">[89b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89b5}

06.11.1948

Philosophy is not a description of linguistic usage, and yet one can learn it by constantly paying attention to all the utterances of language.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89b.6) <span class="fac">[89b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89b6}

Knowledge, belief, hope, fear (and others) are all such different concepts that a classification, an arrangement into categories, is of no use to us. But we want to recognize the differences and similarities among them.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-89b.7) <span class="fac">[89b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/89b.webp)</span> {#89b7}

Compare: “I believe that when you spoke of ‘N.’, you meant…” and “When you spoke of N., I knew that you meant…” Does the latter involve a specific experience? And why, then, the former?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90a.1) <span class="fac">[90a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#90a1}

The expression: “It occurred to me.” “While reading, our conversation of yesterday occurred to me.” Does what I am reading also occur to me while I am reading attentively?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90a.2) <span class="fac">[90a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#90a2}

“No, when I said ‘bank,’ I thought for a moment of our garden bench.” – Would I also say that the money-bank occurred to me if everything had proceeded normally?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90a.3) <span class="fac">[90a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#90a3}

Imagine someone, whenever they make an utterance, tells us (always) about what was going on in their mind at the time. (It is a habit.) Would we be interested in this under all circumstances?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90a.4) <span class="fac">[90a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#90a4}

“I mean, when I said ‘bank,’ I of course meant the bank that you are supposed to go to.” Did an experience of meaning have to accompany the word? (Nonsense!) – But why then, if I – against the context – thought of our garden bench at the time?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90a.5) <span class="fac">[90a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp)</span> {#90a5}

“I have already remembered three times today that I have to write to N.” What importance does what happened at the time have?! – But on the other hand, what importance, what interest does the report itself have? It allows certain conclusions.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90a.6+90b.1) <span class="fac">[90a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90a.webp),[90b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#90a690b1}

“It hadn’t completely slipped my mind; I’ve remembered it three times today.” – “Yes, I know: you got worked up about the word ‥‥ once.” – His state of mind is being examined, & it has certain consequences. Others, for example, would say: “It was _completely_ forgotten; I no longer thought about it.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90b.2) <span class="fac">[90b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#90b2}

07.11.1948

I pursued the word in _this_ direction. (So it is as if one were to indicate the tangent to a curve at this point.) But that is again only an image. (As if one were to describe tennis without a ball by means of tennis with a ball.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90b.3) <span class="fac">[90b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#90b3}

The language-game “I mean (or meant) _that_” (a subsequent explanation of a word) is quite different from the language-game “I was thinking of ‥‥”. This is related to: “It reminded me of ‥‥”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90b.4) <span class="fac">[90b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#90b4}

Here, during the time of believing, remembering, or recalling, a characteristic _reaction_ is possible.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90b.5) <span class="fac">[90b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#90b5}

What is the primitive reaction with which the language-game begins? Which can then be expressed in words. How does it come about that people use these words?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90b.6) <span class="fac">[90b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp)</span> {#90b6}

“You said the word as if something else suddenly occurred to you.” One does not learn this reaction. The primitive reaction could also have been a word-reaction.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-90b.7+91a.1) <span class="fac">[90b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/90b.webp),[91a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#90b791a1}

Suppose I am talking to someone about Dr. N. In the middle of the conversation, I say, “When I said the name ‘N,’ I was just thinking about Dr. N.” The other person would not understand me. If I had said, “I meant ‘N’ to refer to Dr. N., who…,” the answer might have been: “Of course. Who else could you have meant!” If I had said, “When I said the name ‘N,’ I now see Dr. N. before me,” that might not have been relevant.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-91a.2) <span class="fac">[91a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#91a2}

“When asked about the word, I thought of…” – If one were to answer the question “What was going on in your mind at the time?” with _nothing_, would that utterance be invalid? – He could have answered, “I forgot,” & it only _seemed_ to him as if he had ever known.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-91a.3) <span class="fac">[91a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#91a3}

Is “I wanted to give you to understand by this sign…” comparable to: “When I used to open my mouth, I wanted to say…?” That is, is that sentence the expression of a past intention?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-91a.4) <span class="fac">[91a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#91a4}

08.11.1948

“I wanted to give you to understand by this sign that you should…” “This does not express a rule of signs (no agreement); but the purpose of that sign in my action.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-91a.5) <span class="fac">[91a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#91a5}

Of course, I could have used a sign established for this purpose.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-91a.6) <span class="fac">[91a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91a.webp)</span> {#91a6}

[→ ] “_This_ number is the correct continuation of this sequence.” By means of this expression, I can get someone to call this & that the “correct continuation” in the future. What ‘this & that’ is, I can only show by means of examples. – I.e., I can teach him to continue a sequence (basic sequence), to use an expression for the law of the sequence, rather as a substrate for the use of algebraic rules, or something similar.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-91b.1) <span class="fac">[91b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/91b.webp)</span> {#91b1}

I can now, however, use the word “equal” when teaching the basic series, which he perhaps already knows in other contexts, i.e., in another, albeit related, meaning: it may turn out that he learns to form the basic series more easily if I say to him “You must always do that, always add that,” i.e., if I state a rule; but it does not yet function here as a rule, there is not yet any algebra.
