### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-111a.8+111b.1) <span class="fac">[111a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111a.webp),[111b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#111a8111b1}

Is it capricious, or not, that a word has a different meaning on each day of the week? or in the first person, something different than in the second?

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-111b.2) <span class="fac">[111b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#111b2}

Always descend from the barren heights of cleverness into the verdant valleys of stupidity.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-111b.3) <span class="fac">[111b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#111b3}

‘Meaning’ is a primitive concept. Its form includes: “The word means _that_”; i.e., the explanation of meaning through demonstration. This works well under certain circumstances & with certain words. However, difficulties arise when one extends the concept to other words.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-111b.4) <span class="fac">[111b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#111b4}

The explanation of a word is not an analysis of what goes on in me (or is supposed to go on) when I utter it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-111b.5) <span class="fac">[111b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp)</span> {#111b5}

“Two soldiers stand on each two meters.” “He sat in the bank on a bench.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-111b.6+112a.1) <span class="fac">[111b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/111b.webp),[112a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#111b6112a1}

“For this word in our language, I will assign two meanings; one I explain _like this_: ‥‥‥, the other _like this_: ‥‥․” I could also have said: This word in our language has two meanings: ‥‥‥” Here, one could not ask: “But are these really two meanings?” – Or perhaps – if that means: “Isn’t this distinction completely arbitrary, completely pointless?”

“Why do you distinguish them, what is the point of this distinction?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112a.2) <span class="fac">[112a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#112a2}

“I cannot see its purpose.” But what does the explanation of a purpose look like? I cannot give a general answer to that.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112a.3) <span class="fac">[112a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#112a3}

You set yourself tasks & then solve them, like a mathematician. The task: non & ne.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112a.4) <span class="fac">[112a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#112a4}

The one who describes word uses naively will also describe those of “non” & those of “ne,” & he can also make the observation that they are almost the same. – But that is not all: Can he not say that the two words are only different in very specific language-games, & otherwise have the same use?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112a.5) <span class="fac">[112a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#112a5}

Must he not be able to say that in a certain language-game, one word can be replaced by another?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112a.6) <span class="fac">[112a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp)</span> {#112a6}

If the language-game, the activity, e.g., building a house (as in No. 2), fixes the use of a word, then the concept of use is elastic with that of the activity. But this lies in the _nature_ of language.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112a.7+112b.1) <span class="fac">[112a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112a.webp),[112b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#112a7112b1}

Let us imagine _this_ use of “non” & “ne”: The two words are used like our “not”; on the same occasion, one or the other is used, they behave in this respect just like synonymous words; only in the rare case of doubling is a distinction made. I will therefore be tempted to distinguish the word use ‘as _a whole_’ from a partial use. Yes, here the partial use may seem more important than the ‘whole’.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112b.2) <span class="fac">[112b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#112b2}

So I say: “The use here & here is the same. In all these cases, one can substitute one for the other.” But what does that actually mean?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112b.3) <span class="fac">[112b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#112b3}

The naive describer – does he know the concept of ‘being able to replace one word with another’? – He certainly knows the one concerning the mixed _use_ of two words.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112b.4) <span class="fac">[112b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#112b4}

Or also like this: The traveler, who travels through the land where “non” & “ne” are used & tries to translate the language into his own, will have no reason to translate each into a separate word in his language – until he comes to a case of double negation (then _he_ may find an equivalent in his language).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112b.5) <span class="fac">[112b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#112b5}

The traveler could also say: “As far as I can see, the use is the same.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-112b.6) <span class="fac">[112b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp)</span> {#112b6}

“‘ne’ & ‘non’” has exactly the same meaning in all these cases.” One could say this, for example, if in their language the words are _treated_ like synonyms in these cases. (And we know what that looks like.) – But it could also be that the language does not treat them like synonyms, does not ‘_mix_’ them, & they would still be synonyms for us.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-112b.7+113a.1) <span class="fac">[112b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/112b.webp),[113a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#112b7113a1}

29.11.1948

I have one of those talents that always has to turn necessity into a virtue.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-113a.2) <span class="fac">[113a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#113a2}

Tradition is not something that everyone can adopt, it is not a thread that one can pick up if one likes; just as it is not possible to choose one's own ancestors. Whoever does not have a tradition & wants to have one is like an unhappy lover.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-113a.3) <span class="fac">[113a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#113a3}

The happily in love person & the unhappily in love person each have their own pathos. But it is more difficult to be happily unhappily in love than to be happily happily in love.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-113a.4) <span class="fac">[113a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#113a4}

_---_

The greatest difficulty in these investigations is finding a way of representation for vagueness.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-113a.5) <span class="fac">[113a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp)</span> {#113a5}

One can speak of the function of the word in the sentence, in the language-game, in language. But “function” means in each of these cases _Technique_. It therefore refers to a _general_ explanation & training.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-113a.6+113b.1) <span class="fac">[113a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113a.webp),[113b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp)</span> {#113a6113b1}

Whoever teaches someone a sign for negation trains them in a certain way. (The double negation need not appear at all in the training.) Now, however, he can use it once, or hear it & perceive it in this way or that. This perception need not be connected with his earlier training, although one might imagine it that way. Should I say that the training has taught him the meaning of the double negation? I don't have to say that. And if it has taught me to use two words in the same way as a negation, certainly not, nor to discriminate between them in the case of doubling. I certainly did not learn this distinction through the training. But I did learn a meaning through it, & therefore, the _same_ meaning.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-113b.2) <span class="fac">[113b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp)</span> {#113b2}

One can distinguish between different kinds of training in a training session. And thus, one can distinguish between different kinds of use in a use of words.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-113b.3) <span class="fac">[113b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp)</span> {#113b3}

_---_

November 30, 1948. So psychology is concerned with behaviour; not with the mental? What does the psychologist report? – What does he observe? Not the behaviour of people & in particular their utterances? But these do not report on their behaviour.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-113b.4+114a.1) <span class="fac">[113b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/113b.webp),[114a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#113b4114a1}

The doctor asks, “How is he doing?” The nurse says, “He is groaning.” A report about behaviour. But does the question of whether the groaning is genuine even need to arise? Couldn’t it be that this question simply doesn’t exist? Couldn’t one, for example, conclude: “If he is groaning, then we must give him another pain-relieving medication”? Couldn’t the report about behaviour in this world of thought simply be _used_ as a report about the psychological, _as_ such? Couldn’t it be used for this purpose, & isn’t it precisely the purpose that matters?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114a.2) <span class="fac">[114a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#114a2}

“But these then involve a tacit assumption.” Then the technique of the use of our words is always a tacit assumption.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114a.3) <span class="fac">[114a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#114a3}

“In doing so, we always make an assumption; if it is not correct, then, of course, everything is different.” Let us say, for example, when we send someone to do some shopping. Is the assumption that he is a human being & that the shop is not a mirage? Assumptions have an end.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114a.4) <span class="fac">[114a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#114a4}

But couldn't what is not an ‘assumption’ here be something else in another case? Isn’t an assumption simply where there is a _doubt_? And doubt can be completely absent; & it can be present to the slightest degree & to the greatest extent.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114a.5) <span class="fac">[114a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#114a5}

Imagine someone saying, “I’m scared, I’m scared all the time,” – but what he means is that he can play chess. He gives a capability the expression of an experience.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114a.6) <span class="fac">[114a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp)</span> {#114a6}

Even if he could do this only when & for as long as he feels it, feeling would not be the ability.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114a.7+114b.1) <span class="fac">[114a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114a.webp),[114b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#114a7114b1}

01.12.1948

How does one compare the behaviour of anger, joy, hope, expectation, belief, love, understanding? – Depict an angry person! That is easy. A joyful one – that would depend: what kind of joy? The joy of reunion, or the joy of listening to music…? – Hope? That would be difficult. Why? There are no gestures of hope. How does hope express itself, that he will return?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114b.2) <span class="fac">[114b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#114b2}

It is easy to imagine an animal angry, fearful, sad, joyful, or frightened. But hopeful?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114b.3) <span class="fac">[114b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#114b3}

Surely, hoping is a calm, joyful anticipation. (Although such an analysis has something rather unappealing about it.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114b.4) <span class="fac">[114b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#114b4}

A dog can expect its master, but can it expect that its master will come the day after tomorrow? & what can’t it do? – How am I to do this? What am I supposed to answer?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114b.5) <span class="fac">[114b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp)</span> {#114b5}

_---_

‘Meaning’ is not the experience one has when uttering or hearing the word; & the ‘sense’ of the sentence is not the complex of experiences to which the words belong. How is the sense of “I have still not seen him” composed of the meanings of the words? The sentence is composed of the words, & that is enough.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-114b.6+115a.1) <span class="fac">[114b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/114b.webp),[115a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#114b6115a1}

The feeling for a word. Imagine we found a person who, when we talk to them about the feeling for words, says that for them, “if” & “but” have the _same_ feeling. Shouldn’t we believe them? – Or should we simply say that they aren’t playing our game? It would be similar to if someone didn’t associate each vowel with its own color, but rather, let’s say, one with a, e, i, & another with o & u. Perhaps such people exist.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115a.2) <span class="fac">[115a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#115a2}

They would, one might say, be much different from us, more so than those who don’t associate any colors with the vowels. One might almost call them colorblind.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115a.3) <span class="fac">[115a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#115a3}

And would he therefore confuse the _use_ of “if” & “that”?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115a.4) <span class="fac">[115a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#115a4}

Can only someone who can speak also hope? Only someone who has mastered the use of language. The signs of hope are modifications of a more complex pattern of life. (If a concept has application to the character of handwriting, then it has no application to essences that do not write.)

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115a.5) <span class="fac">[115a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#115a5}

The glance that the word casts upon us in a certain context. The way in which it looks at us naturally depends on the environment in which it stands.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115a.6) <span class="fac">[115a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#115a6}

Isn’t the if-feeling _this_, in _this_ tone & _this_ environment?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115a.7) <span class="fac">[115a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115a.webp)</span> {#115a7}

The if-feeling cannot be something that the word “if” _accompanies_.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.1) <span class="fac">[115b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b1}

Otherwise, it could also accompany something else.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.2) <span class="fac">[115b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b2}

Imagine I’m talking about an if-gesture. Could another word make the same gesture? – Or would it then not be the same?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.3) <span class="fac">[115b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b3}

The if-gesture also includes the sound of the word “if”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.4) <span class="fac">[115b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b4}

Can two faces have the _same_ expression? (Yes & No.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.5) <span class="fac">[115b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b5}

The if-feeling should be comparable to the specific ‘feeling’ that a musical phrase gives us. (One might speak of a ‘half-cadence _feeling_’.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.6) <span class="fac">[115b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b6}

But can this feeling be separated from the phrase? And yet it is not the phrase itself, because one can hear it without this feeling.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.7) <span class="fac">[115b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b7}

Is it similar to the ‘expression’ with which it is perhaps played?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.8) <span class="fac">[115b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b8}

For one does not _mean_ a feeling that accompanies it, at most the phrase _with_ the feeling.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.9) <span class="fac">[115b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b9}

“He looked at me with a peculiar smile.” – With what kind of smile? – Perhaps I have to draw his face.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.10) <span class="fac">[115b\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp)</span> {#115b10}

02.12.1948

The if-feeling is not a feeling that accompanies the utterance of the word “if”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-115b.11+116a.1) <span class="fac">[115b\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/115b.webp),[116a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp)</span> {#115b11116a1}

We say that this passage gives us a very special feeling. We sing it to ourselves, and in doing so, we make a certain movement, and perhaps have some special sensation. But these accompaniments – the movement, the sensation – we would not recognize again in another context. They would be quite empty, and they are only not so when we sing this musical phrase.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116a.2) <span class="fac">[116a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp)</span> {#116a2}

Let’s say, “I sing it with a very specific expression,” then “expression” does not refer to something that I can separate from it. One could probably imagine that, in a different sense, I could play a _different_ phrase with the same expression.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116a.3) <span class="fac">[116a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp)</span> {#116a3}

The special feeling that this passage gives me belongs to the passage, yes, to it in this context.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116a.4) <span class="fac">[116a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp)</span> {#116a4}

I can talk about the expression with which someone plays a passage, even without thinking that another passage could have the same expression. The concept here serves only for the comparison of performances of this passage.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116a.5) <span class="fac">[116a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp)</span> {#116a5}

_---_

December 3, 1948. The fact that we understand a sentence shows us that we could, under certain circumstances, use it (even if only in a fairy tale), but it does not show us _what_, & _how much_ we can do with it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116a.6) <span class="fac">[116a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp)</span> {#116a6}

_---_

04.12.1948 [non & ne] It has the same purpose, the same _use_ – except for one stipulation.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116a.7+116b.1) <span class="fac">[116a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116a.webp),[116b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116b.webp)</span> {#116a7116b1}

Thus, are there essential & inessential differences between the uses of words? This distinction only arises when one speaks of the _purpose_ of the word.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116b.2) <span class="fac">[116b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116b.webp)</span> {#116b2}

My kinesthetic sensations inform me about the movements and positions of my limbs.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116b.3) <span class="fac">[116b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116b.webp)</span> {#116b3}

Now I let my index finger make a slight back-and-forth pendulum motion. I hardly feel it, or not at all. Perhaps a little in the fingertip, like a stretching of the skin (not at all in the joint). And does this sensation inform me about the movement? Because I can describe it accurately.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116b.4) <span class="fac">[116b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116b.webp)</span> {#116b4}

“You must really feel the movement, otherwise you couldn’t know how the finger is moving.” But “to know” just means: to be able to describe it. – I might only be able to indicate the direction from which a sound comes because it affects one ear more strongly than the other; but I don’t actually _hear_ it. It just results in: I _know_ where the sound is coming from, I look in that direction, for example.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116b.5) <span class="fac">[116b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116b.webp)</span> {#116b5}

The same applies to the idea that a characteristic of the sensation of pain must inform us about the location of the pain; or a characteristic of the memory-image about the location of an event in time.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-116b.6+117a.1) <span class="fac">[116b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/116b.webp),[117a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp)</span> {#116b6117a1}

A sensation _can_ inform us about the movement or position of a limb. For example, someone who, unlike a normal person, is unable to state with their eyes closed whether their arm is extended, could be informed about this by a pain in the elbow. – And the character of a pain _can_ also inform us about the location of the injury.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117a.2) <span class="fac">[117a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp)</span> {#117a2}

How do I know that the sense of touch informs the blind person, and sight informs the seeing person, about the shape and location of things?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117a.3) <span class="fac">[117a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp)</span> {#117a3}

Do I only know it from my own _experience_, & merely suspect it in others?

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117a.4) <span class="fac">[117a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp)</span> {#117a4}

The evolution of higher animals and humans, and the awakening of the mind, consciousness at a certain stage. Our language first describes a picture. What is to happen with the picture, how is it to be used, remains in the dark. But it is clear that this must be investigated if one wants to understand the meaning of our statements. But the picture seems to transcend this task; it already points to a (quite) specific use. In this way, it has done us a favor.

### <span class="fac">[117a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp)</span>

But the picture is something like this: the world is dark, despite the ether waves that permeate it. But one day, human beings open their seeing eyes, and it becomes bright.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117a.6) <span class="fac">[117a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp)</span> {#117a6}

_---_

December 6, 1948. What is the criterion for a sense impression informing me about shape & color?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117a.7+117b.1) <span class="fac">[117a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117a.webp),[117b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117b.webp)</span> {#117a7117b1}

_Which_ sense impression? Well, _this one_. I can describe it: “it is the same as the one, ‥‥” – or show it in a picture. And now: what do you feel when your fingers are in this position? – “How is one supposed to explain a feeling? One can only know it in oneself.” But surely one can teach the use of words!

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117b.2) <span class="fac">[117b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117b.webp)</span> {#117b2}

I am now looking for the grammatical difference.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117b.3) <span class="fac">[117b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117b.webp)</span> {#117b3}

Color, sound, taste, temperature, these have a subjective and an objective side. This probably means: they sometimes indicate what I feel, sometimes they describe the external world. – Well, the subjective component seems to be lacking in my knowledge of body position.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117b.4) <span class="fac">[117b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117b.webp)</span> {#117b4}

Can one describe a feeling? Of course, one can. One does it every day. But how? Well, we must consider the specific cases.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-117b.5) <span class="fac">[117b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/117b.webp)</span> {#117b5}

If someone told me that he felt at the time what one feels when one holds one’s fingers in _this_ position, or moves them _like this_, I would imitate the position or movement and then perhaps ask him, “Do you mean the feeling in the fingertips, or in the muscles, or at _this_ spot?” That is, it would not yet be clear to me what feeling he is talking about; indeed, I could even say to him, “I don’t feel anything at all with this movement.” Consider: I could also ask him, “Is it a strong feeling, or a very weak one?!” (But this remark is only on the periphery, not at the center of the matter.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118a.1) <span class="fac">[118a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118a.webp)</span> {#118a1}

And what is the _location_ of the kinesthetic feeling? Can you point to it? (Because the fact that there are receptors in the joints and muscles is not relevant here.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118a.2) <span class="fac">[118a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118a.webp)</span> {#118a2}

Let’s put aside the kinesthetic feeling for a moment! – I want to describe a feeling to someone, & I tell him, “Do it _like this_, then you’ll have it.” I hold my arm, or my head, in a certain position. Is this a description of a feeling, & when will I say that he has understood what feeling I meant? He would then have to give a _further_ description of the feeling. And what kind must it be? – Suppose he says to me, “Yes, I’ve got it. It’s a very peculiar feeling.” To the question, “What kind? Where?” he says that he can’t say, – it is quite peculiar. How would we know that it is a feeling?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118a.3) <span class="fac">[118a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118a.webp)</span> {#118a3}

The ‘further description’ will relate the feeling to other feelings: it will have a location, it will remain constant, or change, become stronger or weaker.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118a.4) <span class="fac">[118a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118a.webp)</span> {#118a4}

“Do it _like this_, then you’ll have it.” I had my arm, or my head in a certain position. Can there not be a doubt? Must there not be one, if a feeling is meant?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118b.1) <span class="fac">[118b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118b.webp)</span> {#118b1}

What would we say if someone told us that he saw a color on a certain thing that he could not describe? Must he express himself correctly? Must he mean a color?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118b.2) <span class="fac">[118b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118b.webp)</span> {#118b2}

_That_ looks _like this_; _that_ tastes _like this_; _that_ feels _like this_: “that” & “like this” must be explained differently.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118b.3) <span class="fac">[118b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118b.webp)</span> {#118b3}

December 6, 1948

A ‘feeling’ has a quite _specific_ significance for us. And this includes, for example, a ‘degree of feeling,’ the susceptibility of one feeling to being overshadowed by another.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118b.4) <span class="fac">[118b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118b.webp)</span> {#118b4}

“Sorrow” describes a kind of recurring pattern in the tapestry of life. Well, this pattern also includes a _course_. If the _bodily expression_ of grief & joy in a person alternated, say, with the ticking of a metronome, this would not produce the grief or joy pattern. (This does not mean that joy or sorrow is behaviour.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118b.5) <span class="fac">[118b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118b.webp)</span> {#118b5}

Whoever observes his own sorrow, with what senses does he observe it? With his own sense? With one that _feels_ the sorrow? Does he feel it _differently_ when he observes it? And which one does he observe, the one that is only there while it is being observed? – ‘Observation’ does not create the observed. (This is a conceptual statement.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-118b.6) <span class="fac">[118b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/118b.webp)</span> {#118b6}

But I can observe my sorrow. I ask myself, for example, “Am I as sad today as I was yesterday?” & I answer it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119a.1) <span class="fac">[119a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119a.webp)</span> {#119a1}

I say (to myself), for example: “A month ago, I could not have thought about it without horror.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119a.2) <span class="fac">[119a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119a.webp)</span> {#119a2}

07.12.1948

If someone had been trained to utter a certain sound when looking at something red, another when looking at something yellow, & so on for other colours, one would not say that he could describe objects according to their colours, although he could help us to describe them. In order to describe, he must be able to make pictures of colour distributions in space according to some rule of projection. (Language-game <math display="inline"></math>?)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119a.3) <span class="fac">[119a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119a.webp)</span> {#119a3}

If someone, for example, let his gaze wander around a room, suddenly it falls on an object of striking red colour & he exclaims “Red!” – he would not have given a description, even if he could give one.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119a.4) <span class="fac">[119a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119a.webp)</span> {#119a4}

08.12.1948

Are the words “I am afraid” a description of a state of mind? It depends on what game they are in.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119a.5+119b.1) <span class="fac">[119a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119a.webp),[119b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119a5119b1}

We naturally assume certain physiological accompaniments in the one who expresses fear: the rapid pulse, the panting breath, perhaps increased blood pressure & a series of, difficult to observe, phenomena of the nervous system, all of which is accompanied by some characteristic feelings. If someone breaks out in a cold sweat, then he has the characteristic sensations of sweating.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119b.2) <span class="fac">[119b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119b2}

And furthermore: it is probably possible that the one who imitates certain typical mien, gestures, sounds of fear, & thereby obtains the one or other typical feeling that these gestures produce, – that by this he induces other of the physiological fear phenomena in his body & obtains further fear sensations with these.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119b.3) <span class="fac">[119b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119b3}

Yes, it _can_ also be that playing creates fear. (It does _not_ have to be, it is not in the nature of fear.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119b.4) <span class="fac">[119b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119b4}

_---_

09.12.1948 The language-game of reporting can be used in such a way that the report is not intended to inform us about the object of the report, but about the reporter. For example, when the teacher examines the student. (One can measure in order to test the standard.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119b.5) <span class="fac">[119b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119b5}

“If my senses do not deceive me, he is coming there.” “If I am not mistaken, he is coming there.” What is the assumption form for this?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119b.6) <span class="fac">[119b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119b6}

One can very well say: “It seems to me that he is coming, but he is not coming.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-119b.7) <span class="fac">[119b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/119b.webp)</span> {#119b7}

One can distrust one’s own senses, but not one’s own belief.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120a.1) <span class="fac">[120a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp)</span> {#120a1}

One can even say: “It makes the impression on me that he is coming, but he is not coming.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120a.2) <span class="fac">[120a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp)</span> {#120a2}

Suppose I introduce an expression, e.g., “I believe,” in such a way that it is placed before the report where it serves to provide information about the reporter. (There is no need to attach uncertainty to “I believe.”) What would “I believe it is so, and it is not so” mean? Also, consider that uncertainty can also be expressed impersonally: “He is probably coming today.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120a.3) <span class="fac">[120a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp)</span> {#120a3}

“I believe…” illuminates my state. Conclusions about my behaviour can be drawn from this utterance. Thus, there is a similarity with the expressions of emotional states such as mood, etc.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120a.4) <span class="fac">[120a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp)</span> {#120a4}

If there were a verb “to seem to believe,” it would lack a meaningful first person in the indicative present tense. (Our word “to dream” might also lack it.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120a.5) <span class="fac">[120a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp)</span> {#120a5}

10.12.1948

The best example of an expression with a very specific meaning is a passage in a drama.

_---_

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-120a.6) <span class="fac">[120a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp)</span> {#120a6}

Moore has stirred up a philosophical hornet’s nest with his paradox; and if the hornets have not properly flown at him, it is only because they were too sluggish.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120a.7+120b.1) <span class="fac">[120a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120a.webp),[120b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp)</span> {#120a7120b1}

The instantaneous movement. Whoever sees a movement does not see positions at points in time. He could not depict it, imitate it.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120b.2) <span class="fac">[120b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp)</span> {#120b2}

“I used to believe that the Earth was flat.” A belief has a basis: the experiences, reports, and relationships on which it is founded. It stands on a ground.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120b.3) <span class="fac">[120b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp)</span> {#120b3}

The line “X is mistaken” has no real point for X = I. The line disappears into the darkness here.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120b.4) <span class="fac">[120b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp)</span> {#120b4}

One can ask: Is a state that I infer from the utterances of a person really the same as the one that someone else does not recognize in this way? And the answer is a decision.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120b.5) <span class="fac">[120b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp)</span> {#120b5}

The phenomenon we are talking about is the lighting up of the aspect.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120b.6) <span class="fac">[120b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp)</span> {#120b6}

One says to oneself, for example, “It could also be _that_” (gives a new interpretation), and the aspect may light up.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-120b.7+121a.1) <span class="fac">[120b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/120b.webp),[121a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#120b7121a1}

11.12.1948

Two uses of “_to see_”. One: “I see _this_” – whereby I refer to a description, or show a picture, a copy. With this, I may communicate to another: there, where his gaze does not reach, is this and that. An example of the other use: “I see a similarity in these two faces.” The person to whom I make the communication may see the faces as clearly as I do.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.2) <span class="fac">[121a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a2}

One person could accurately portray the faces, the other might notice their similarity in these depictions, which the first did not see.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.3) <span class="fac">[121a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a3}

I may look at two faces that do not change: suddenly their similarity lights up. I call this experience the lighting up of an aspect.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.4) <span class="fac">[121a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a4}

The psychologist is not interested in its causes; I am.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.5) <span class="fac">[121a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a5}

We are interested in the concept and its position in the concepts of experience.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.6) <span class="fac">[121a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a6}

One can bring about the lighting up of the aspect by, for example, following certain lines of the face (with the gaze).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.7) <span class="fac">[121a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a7}

What is the characteristic expression of the lighting up? How do I know that someone has this experience? – The expression is similar to that of surprise.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.8) <span class="fac">[121a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp)</span> {#121a8}

An aspect lights up and fades away. If we want it to remain conscious to us, we must strike it again and again.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121a.9+121b.1) <span class="fac">[121a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121a.webp),[121b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121b.webp)</span> {#121a9121b1}

I suddenly see the solution to a puzzle picture. Where before there were branches and twigs, now there is a person. My visual impression has changed, and I now realize that it not only had color and shape, but also a very specific organization and now has another. – My visual impression has changed; – what was it like before; what is it like now. – If I represent it by an exact copy – and is that not a good representation? – then no change appears.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121b.2) <span class="fac">[121b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121b.webp)</span> {#121b2}

And don't just say, “My visual impression is not the _drawing_! It is _this_, something I cannot show anyone.” Of course, it is not the drawing, but also not something of the same category that I carry within me.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121b.3) <span class="fac">[121b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121b.webp)</span> {#121b3}

So, can the copy not reproduce the aspect? – Very different things are called “copy.” – The way of copying _could_ indicate the seen aspect. It can, for example, bring together what belongs together. Also, the particular _errors_ that one makes when copying can indicate the aspect that one saw.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121b.4) <span class="fac">[121b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121b.webp)</span> {#121b4}

The concept of the ‘inner picture’ is misleading, because the model for this concept is the _outer_ picture, & yet their applications are quite different.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-121b.5+122a.1) <span class="fac">[121b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/121b.webp),[122a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122a.webp)</span> {#121b5122a1}

Whoever ascribes the organization of the visual impression to shapes and colors proceeds from the visual impression as an inner object. This object, however, becomes something absurd, a strangely fluctuating entity. For the similarity with the image is now disturbed.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122a.2) <span class="fac">[122a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122a.webp)</span> {#122a2}

Whoever sees a series of equidistant points as a series of pairs of points whose internal distances are smaller than the external ones can say that he sees the series as being organized in a special way, because the image that he would construct of the series would indeed have a special organization. It could also be a mistake: he _believes_ that the series is organized in this way.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122a.3) <span class="fac">[122a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122a.webp)</span> {#122a3}

The organization: these are roughly the spatial relationships. The representation of spatial relationships in the visual impression are spatial relationships in the representation of the visual impression. The change of the aspect _can_ manifest itself through a change in spatial relationships in the representation of what is seen. Example: the aspects of the cube diagram. The drawn copy is always the same, but the spatial arrangement is different.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122a.4) <span class="fac">[122a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122a.webp)</span> {#122a4}

The concept of the representation of what is seen, the copy, is very flexible, & therefore also the concept of what is seen. But the two are intimately connected. (That is to say, _not_ that they are similar to each other.)

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-122a.5) <span class="fac">[122a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122a.webp)</span> {#122a5}

In the mental realm, an enterprise cannot usually be continued, nor is it meant to be continued. These thoughts fertilize the soil for new thoughts.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122a.6+122b.1) <span class="fac">[122a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122a.webp),[122b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122b.webp)</span> {#122a6122b1}

Whoever, when considering the cube schema, expressed himself thus: “I now see a cube in _this_ position, now one in _that_” – he might mean very different things. Something subjective, & something objective. His words alone do not reveal this. – The account of the aspect-switch essentially has the form of an account of the perceived object. But its further application is different.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122b.2) <span class="fac">[122b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122b.webp)</span> {#122b2}

If the aspect is a kind of organization, and the organization is comparable to the characteristics of shape and color, then the change of aspect is like the change of apparent color.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122b.3) <span class="fac">[122b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122b.webp)</span> {#122b3}

‘Color’ & ‘form’ must be learned objectively.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122b.3.2) <span class="fac">[122b\[3\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122b.webp)</span> {#122b32}

The expression of the aspect then follows the expression of perception, just as the expression of the _image_ follows the expression of perception. But here one must remember that the visual _image_ cannot always be represented by the description of a visual _impression_. For example, I imagine a closed box, but the _picture_ of the closed box could also represent many other things. (This is reminiscent of the expression in dream narration: “And I knew that ‥‥․”)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-122b.4+123a.1) <span class="fac">[122b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/122b.webp),[123a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123a.webp)</span> {#122b4123a1}

The _seeing_ of an aspect is an act of will. One can ask someone: Look at it now _in this way_. Consider the similarity, try to see it again, 12.12.1948. Listen to the theme _in this way_, etc. But does this mean that seeing is an act of will? Or is it rather the way of looking that brings about this seeing? I can, for example, see the cube diagram _in this way_ by directing my gaze particularly to _these_ edges. If I do this, then the shift in aspect follows. Here, I _know_ how I _bring it about_. On the other hand, if I

![](../graphics/233a,43.svg)

look at it in this way and that way, then I am not conscious of it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123a.2) <span class="fac">[123a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123a.webp)</span> {#123a2}

The aspect is dependent on the will. In this it is like the mental image.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123a.3) <span class="fac">[123a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123a.webp)</span> {#123a3}

But isn't visual _perception_ also dependent on the will? If one looks more closely, one sees something different, & one can evoke the other visual impression at will. This certainly doesn't make the impression an aspect – but isn't it also dependent on the will?

### <span class="fac">[123a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123a.webp)</span>

[Perhaps this good period ends here.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123a.6) <span class="fac">[123a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123a.webp)</span> {#123a6}

Whoever has always regarded a figure as a duck-rabbit need never have had the experience that the words “Now I see it as a rabbit” express. This aspect did not need to ‘dawn’ on him.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123a.7+123b.1) <span class="fac">[123a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123a.webp),[123b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123a7123b1}

Whoever looks at the H.-E. _image_ & reflects on the facial expression of H., perhaps trying to find the right word for it, is looking at the image in the H. aspect, but this H. aspect does not become clear. Is it correct to say that he sees the image in this aspect all the time? Now, he describes what he sees as an H. head, because that is how he talks about what he sees, for example.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.2) <span class="fac">[123b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b2}

Now, do not ask yourself “How is it with _me_?” Instead, ask “What do I know about the Other?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.3) <span class="fac">[123b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b3}

Don’t ask yourself “Do _I_ see it in such a case?” – but rather “What makes me say that _he_ sees it in such a case?”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.4) <span class="fac">[123b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b4}

If I heard someone talking about the H-E picture, & _now_ in a certain way about the particular expression of the H-face, I would say “He is now considering the picture as an H-head,” or “in the H-aspect.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.5) <span class="fac">[123b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b5}

The greatest danger is wanting to observe oneself here.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.6) <span class="fac">[123b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b6}

When I say “These two forms did not seem to have any _similarity_ with each other”, can I use a stronger _expression_ to convey that I saw something different each time?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.7) <span class="fac">[123b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b7}

He looks at, for example, two pictures; in one, the H.-E. head is surrounded by rabbits, in the other, by ducks. He does not notice the sameness. Does it _follow_ from this that he _sees_ something different each time? – This gives us a reason to say so.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-123b.8) <span class="fac">[123b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/123b.webp)</span> {#123b8}

And what about his utterance “I saw it completely differently!”? Well, that shows, among other things, that this concept comes naturally to one, & that is also understandable.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.1) <span class="fac">[124a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a1}

So I had ‘seen’ it like that – although this aspect had never dawned on me.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.2) <span class="fac">[124a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a2}

And how does this chronic ‘seeing it like that’ compare with colors and shapes? Did my visual image always have these colors, these shapes, this organization? So far, it is only a form of expression; but how similar are these concepts?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.3) <span class="fac">[124a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a3}

You had learned: _this_ is ‘red’; _this_ is ‘round’, _this_ is a ‘rabbit’.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.4) <span class="fac">[124a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a4}

One can, of course, say that there are certain things that fall under both the concept of ‘picture-rabbit’ & ‘picture’. And such a thing is a picture, a drawing. – But the _impression_ is not at the same time the impression of a picture-rabbit & of a picture.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.5) <span class="fac">[124a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a5}

I learned the concepts ‘red’, ‘round’, ‘picture-rabbit’, ‘duckling’ – to a certain extent, they are all roughly on _the same_ level. I can learn them from samples.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.6) <span class="fac">[124a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a6}

A picture-rabbit is _something like_ this: & now I will show examples. A picture is therefore something _different_, even if _one_ example is the same.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.7) <span class="fac">[124a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a7}

So if I saw the duck-rabbit head as a duck, then I saw: this shape and color (I draw it exactly – and also something like this: while showing a lot of different duck pictures. This shows the difference of the concepts.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.8) <span class="fac">[124a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp)</span> {#124a8}

“I saw it _completely_ differently; I would never have recognized it!” Now, that is an exclamation. And it also has a justification.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124a.9+124b.1) <span class="fac">[124a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124a.webp),[124b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124a9124b1}

You must have been copying it all this time through this face (the imitation of a rabbit), so you did see it in _a_ sense _as_ such.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.2) <span class="fac">[124b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b2}

And if I happen to see it now as H, & now as E, then I see it _like that_, & _like that_ (whereby each time I imitate a different animal & look in a different direction).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.3) <span class="fac">[124b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b3}

From whom does one say that he enjoys the expressive appearance of this picture? Well, whoever looks at it like this, talks about it like this, and reacts to it like this.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.4) <span class="fac">[124b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b4}

I have always seen it as a rabbit; one might even say: it has always been a rabbit for me, I have always spoken to it as one would speak to a rabbit. A child does this. I have always _treated_ it as a rabbit; that is what it means.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.5) <span class="fac">[124b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b5}

If the child now treats the picture of the rabbit as a real rabbit, does that reveal something about the organization of the visual image? Is _that_ a proof that the child does not only see colors & shapes?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.6) <span class="fac">[124b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b6}

And now, the change of aspect. The experience of the _new_ aspect. Or: the experience of the aspect _emerging_. And its expression is an exclamation. A “H!” etc.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.7) <span class="fac">[124b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b7}

“You would say that the picture has changed completely now!”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-124b.8) <span class="fac">[124b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/124b.webp)</span> {#124b8}

But what is different: my impression? My statement? – Can I say it? I _describe_ the change as one would describe a perception; it is as if the object before my eyes had changed.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125a.1) <span class="fac">[125a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125a.webp)</span> {#125a1}

Imagine the H-E head fully fleshed out, & a child treats it like a doll, sometimes _this_ way, sometimes _that_ way.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125a.2) <span class="fac">[125a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125a.webp)</span> {#125a2}

One is shown a picture-rabbit & asked what it is; I say “That is an H.” Not “That is now an H.” I communicate the perception. One is shown the H.-E. head & asked what it is; then one _can_ say “that is the H.-E. head.” But I can also react quite differently. – If I say that it is the H.-E. head, then it is again the communication of the perception; if, however, I say “Now there is an H.,” then it is not. If I had said “It is a rabbit,” I would not have noticed the ambiguity & would have reported the perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125a.3) <span class="fac">[125a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125a.webp)</span> {#125a3}

However, isn’t there also a difference between the _first_ “Now it’s a hare” & the aspect that now arises?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125a.4) <span class="fac">[125a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125a.webp)</span> {#125a4}

13.12.1948

A mottled wall; & I am occupied with seeing faces in it; but not in order to study the nature of the aspect, but because those _shapes_ interest me & the fate that leads me from one to the other. Aspects keep appearing, others fade away, sometimes I stare blindly at the wall.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125a.5) <span class="fac">[125a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125a.webp)</span> {#125a5}

Under the spot could also be the double cross and the duck-rabbit, and they could be seen as the others are, and with them in different aspects.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125a.6) <span class="fac">[125a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125a.webp)</span> {#125a6}

The aspect seems to belong to the structure of the inner materialization.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.1) <span class="fac">[125b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b1}

We learn language-games. We learn to arrange objects according to their colors, to report the colors of things, to produce colors, to compare shapes, to measure, etc., etc. – Do we learn this from imagining them?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.2) <span class="fac">[125b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b2}

There is a language-game: “Report whether this figure occurs in that one (also ‘how often’ and ‘where’).” What you report is a perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.3) <span class="fac">[125b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b3}

One could also say, “Report whether a mirror-F occurs here,” and it can suddenly strike one. This could be of great importance.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.4) <span class="fac">[125b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b4}

But the report “Now I see it as…” does not report a perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.5) <span class="fac">[125b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b5}

“One can, at one time, think of _that_, at another time of _that_, at one time see it as _that_, at another time as that, & then one will see it at one time _like this_, at another time _like that_.” _How_ then? There is, after all, no further determination.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.6) <span class="fac">[125b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b6}

I can switch the aspects of the figure, and not be conscious of any other act of will in doing so.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.7) <span class="fac">[125b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b7}

It is useful in these considerations to introduce the concept of ‘picture-rabbit’, ‘picture-human’, etc. A picture-face, for example, is the _figure_.

![](../graphics/137,125b.svg)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-125b.8) <span class="fac">[125b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/125b.webp)</span> {#125b8}

“I can now say that I see _that_.” It is the report of a _new_ experience.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126a.1) <span class="fac">[126a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126a.webp)</span> {#126a1}

But what if I first draw the perceived thing accurately, and then say, “I now see that it is a rabbit,” or “Ah, it is a rabbit!” Now I am expressing an experience at the time of the exclamation.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126a.2) <span class="fac">[126a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126a.webp)</span> {#126a2}

December 14, 1948

The perceiving of the internal relation and the lighting up of the aspect of the internal relation. Whoever has always seen the duck-rabbit as an H, and then sees it once as an E, may learn that a duck-rabbit and an E-shape can have the same contour. Under certain circumstances, this can be an important discovery. (I am thinking of a cipher in which a rabbit-head is a sign.) – But the lighting up of the H-aspect is not the perceiving of that relation. Wouldn't it be possible for someone to perceive it without experiencing the change of aspect, or the lighting up?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126a.3) <span class="fac">[126a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126a.webp)</span> {#126a3}

December 15, 1948

At one point, it says: “What I have before me is _the_ [copy]. I can also describe it as a rabbit.” – The other time: Earlier, I saw something different; now I see a rabbit.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126a.4) <span class="fac">[126a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126a.webp)</span> {#126a4}

The expression of the change of aspect is simultaneously the expression of a _new_ perception & the expression of the unchanged perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126a.5+126b.1) <span class="fac">[126a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126a.webp),[126b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126a5126b1}

The copy describes perception _completely_. The model, to which I also refer, is a kind of _my_ view; one could also say: the experience of seeing. As a report of perception, the copy is then more precise. But when the aspect _becomes apparent_, then the expression of it (pointing to the model, for example) is _essentially_ the expression of a new perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126a.5+126b.1.2) <span class="fac">[126a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126a.webp),[126b\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126a5126b12}

Thus, as if this expression now had to correspond to a new copy. But that is not the case.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126b.2) <span class="fac">[126b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126b2}

I ask: “What do you see?” The other person begins to draw, then gives up and says, “I can’t draw it well; it is a sitting rabbit.” To this, I might perhaps improve his drawing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126b.3) <span class="fac">[126b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126b3}

“I see a rabbit. And that is _exactly_ what I see [now I'm drawing it].”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126b.4) <span class="fac">[126b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126b4}

Is the copy now an imperfect description of my experience of seeing? No. It depends on the circumstances, which further determinations I need to make. It _can_ be imperfect; if a question remains. (Example: Cube schema.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126b.5) <span class="fac">[126b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126b5}

Therefore, pointing to the model, in addition to the copy, may also be part of describing the experience. However, it is not part of describing visual perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-126b.6) <span class="fac">[126b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/126b.webp)</span> {#126b6}

December 16, 1948

If I know that there are different aspects of the cube schema, I can, in order to find out what the other person sees, not only draw it but also let him look at a cube; even if _he_ doesn't know why. _It_ describes to him what he sees. But with the change of aspect, it shifts. It becomes the only possible expression of the experience, which before may have seemed like an unnecessary determination after the copy.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127a.1) <span class="fac">[127a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127a.webp)</span> {#127a1}

If I know that there are different aspects of the cube schema, I can, in order to find out what the other person sees, in addition to the copy, also create or show a model of what has been seen; even if _he_ doesn't know what this double demonstration is supposed to be. But with the change of aspect, it shifts. It becomes the only possible expression of the experience, which before may have been an unnecessary determination after the copy, or (also) was.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127a.2) <span class="fac">[127a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127a.webp)</span> {#127a2}

And that alone eliminates for us the comparison of the ‘organization of the visual impression’ with color & form.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-127a.3) <span class="fac">[127a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127a.webp)</span> {#127a3}

So, you are a bad philosopher if what you write is difficult to understand. If you were better, you would make the difficult understandable. – But who says that is possible?! [Tolstoy.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127a.4) <span class="fac">[127a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127a.webp)</span> {#127a4}

Yes, I confess, nothing seems more likely to me than that people will one day come to the conclusion that the _individual_ thought, the _individual_ image, memory, does not correspond to any image in the physiological, in the nervous system.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127a.5) <span class="fac">[127a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127a.webp)</span> {#127a5}

What would it be like, what would it look like, if the aspect were completely removed from arbitrariness?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127a.6+127b.1) <span class="fac">[127a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127a.webp),[127b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127a6127b1}

Does “seeing the aspect” mean perceiving the internal relation? What do I have against it?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.2) <span class="fac">[127b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127b2}

Whoever looks for a figure (1) in the other (2) & then finds it, can say that (2) is now seen differently. He can not only give a new kind of description of it, but that noticing was a new experience of seeing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.3) <span class="fac">[127b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127b3}

But it doesn't have to happen that he wants to say: “The figure (2) now looks completely different, it also has no _similarity_ with the previous one; although it is congruent with it!”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.4) <span class="fac">[127b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127b4}

“The inner image has colors, shapes, & moreover a certain organization.” From this it would follow: it looks _like this_, & not _like that_.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.5) <span class="fac">[127b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127b5}

You _notice_ an organization of the _object_ (the object of perception). Or rather: You notice something about its organization; a feature of this organization.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.6) <span class="fac">[127b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127b6}

The noticing is an experience of seeing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.7) <span class="fac">[127b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp)</span> {#127b7}

One copies the color & the form. One shows a sample of the color & form. One does not show a sample of the organization of the visual impression.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-127b.8+128a.1) <span class="fac">[127b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/127b.webp),[128a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128a.webp)</span> {#127b8128a1}

But one could say, for example: “In order to get the impression that I have, you must look at this figure, especially at this part, & in such a way that _this_ strikes you about it.” But one does not do that. One does not call that “describing the visual impression”, just as one does not prescribe for this purpose how the other person's gaze should wander over the object. This shows us that “visual impression” is meant to denote something like “visual **image**”, & this something is related to an image.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128a.2) <span class="fac">[128a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128a.webp)</span> {#128a2}

If you ask me what I saw, I will perhaps be able to produce a sketch that shows it, but in most cases I will not remember how my gaze wandered.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128a.3) <span class="fac">[128a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128a.webp)</span> {#128a3}

The color of the object corresponds to the color in the visual impression, the form of the object to the form in the visual impression. But the organization of the object does not correspond to the aspect of the visual impression, because _it_ can change while the same organization is being looked at. In the aspect, I notice a feature of the organization.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128a.4) <span class="fac">[128a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128a.webp)</span> {#128a4}

The color of the object corresponds to the color in the visual impression (this piece of paper seems pink to me, & it is pink) – the form of the object to the form in the visual impression (it seems rectangular to me, & it is rectangular) – but what I see in the aspect is not a property of the object, it is an internal relation between it & other objects.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128a.5+128b.1) <span class="fac">[128a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128a.webp),[128b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128a5128b1}

Imagine the H-E head hidden in a collection of lines. Now, I notice it in the picture, simply as an H. Later, I look at the same picture & notice the same line, but as an E, & I don't even need to know that it was the same line both times. If later I see the aspect change, can I say that the aspects of H & E are seen quite differently than when I noticed them individually in the jumble of lines? No. But the change evokes a sense of wonder that the noticing did not evoke.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.2) <span class="fac">[128b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b2}

(I seem to be doing ‘thought experiments’. Well, they are not experiments. Rather, they are calculations.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.3) <span class="fac">[128b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b3}

17.12.1948

The aspect only lights up, it does not remain. But this must be a conceptual remark, not a psychological one. The expression of seeing the aspect is the expression of the _new_ perception.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.4) <span class="fac">[128b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b4}

The expression of the lighting up of the aspect is: “Now it is _this_ – now it is _this_. The expression of noticing the H-head in the jumble of lines is: “There is an H-head here.” We had not noticed something & now we notice it; there is nothing paradoxical about that. We do not want to say: the old has disappeared – something new is here; & yet it is quite the old.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.5) <span class="fac">[128b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b5}

“Now it is _this_” one does not say _before_ the first change of the aspect.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.6) <span class="fac">[128b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b6}

_---_

18.12.1948 The hesitant assertion is not an assertion of hesitancy.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.7) <span class="fac">[128b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b7}

Think of the hesitant command.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.8) <span class="fac">[128b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp)</span> {#128b8}

And one must be careful not to say that “It might rain” _actually_ means “I believe it will rain”. Why not the other way around?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-128b.9+129a.1) <span class="fac">[128b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/128b.webp),[129a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129a.webp)</span> {#128b9129a1}

Aristotelian logic tabulates the contradiction as an un-sentence that must be excluded from language. However, this logic only deals with a very small area of the logic of our language. (It is as if the first system of geometry had been trigonometry; & as if we now believe that trigonometry is the true foundation, if not even the whole of geometry.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129a.2) <span class="fac">[129a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129a.webp)</span> {#129a2}

Do not consider the hesitant assertion as an assertion of hesitancy.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129a.3) <span class="fac">[129a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129a.webp)</span> {#129a3}

“I may have noticed the similarity between the two for five minutes.” – That would mean: they struck me for five minutes, they occupied me for 5 minutes, I had to think about them again and again during that time. “They only struck me for five minutes, then no longer.” “The similarity puzzled me for five minutes. I had to keep exclaiming…” That does not mean: I observed them for 5 minutes, then they disappeared. “The similarity struck me for 5 minutes” “The similarity puzzled me for 5 minutes. After that, I no longer noticed it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129a.4) <span class="fac">[129a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129a.webp)</span> {#129a4}

“I observed this similarity for 5 minutes” would mean: I observed the changing faces with regard to their similarity.

### <span class="fac">[129a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129a.webp)</span>

Philosophy serves to remove misconceptions. [The role of philosophy in thinking about religion.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129a.6+129b.1) <span class="fac">[129a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129a.webp),[129b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129a6129b1}

The organization of the visual image: _this_ belongs together, _this_ does not. So, organization involves bringing together & separating. Well, one can do that, for example, when drawing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129b.2) <span class="fac">[129b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129b2}

There are very different kinds of ‘aspects’. One kind could be called “aspects of organization”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129b.3) <span class="fac">[129b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129b3}

The lines are connected differently. What used to belong together now does not belong together.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129b.4) <span class="fac">[129b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129b4}

_---_

20.12.1948 I could therefore see the H-E head from the beginning as a picture-H. That is, when asked: “What is that?” or “What do you see there?” I would have answered “A picture-H”. If I had been asked further what a picture-H was, I would have had to explain it by pointing to various rabbit pictures, also to real rabbits, and I could have talked about the life of these animals & imitated them.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129b.5) <span class="fac">[129b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129b5}

I wouldn’t have said, “I see that as a picture-H,” or “I see that now as a picture-H.” I would simply have described the perception; no differently than if I had said, “I see a red circle there.” Nevertheless, another person could have said of me, “He sees this figure as an H.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129b.6) <span class="fac">[129b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129b6}

21.12.1948

To say, “I see that now as…” would make as little sense to me as saying, when looking at a bottle of wine, “I see that now as a bottle.” One would not understand this utterance. “Just as little as the utterance from healthy skin: ‘That is now a bottle for me,’ or even this: ‘That could also be a bottle.’”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-129b.7) <span class="fac">[129b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/129b.webp)</span> {#129b7}

One could not normally say, “I consider this to be a knife and fork.”

### <span class="fac">[130a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span>

I consider a knife & fork at the table to be as unnecessary as I try or attempt to eat while eating.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-130a.2) <span class="fac">[130a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span> {#130a2}
