One also does not consider what one recognizes as a knife and fork at the table to be a knife and fork; just as little as one usually tries to eat while eating, or intends to eat.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130a.3) <span class="fac">[130a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span> {#130a3}

22.12.1948

The greatest happiness of man is love. Suppose you say of the schizophrenic: he does not love, he cannot love, he does not want to love – where is the difference?!

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130a.4) <span class="fac">[130a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span> {#130a4}

“He doesn’t want to…” means: it is within his power. And _who_ would dare say otherwise?!

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130a.5) <span class="fac">[130a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span> {#130a5}

What does one say when one says “it is within my power”? – One says it where one wants to make a difference. _This_ weight I can lift, but I do not want to lift it; that one I _cannot_ lift.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130a.6) <span class="fac">[130a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span> {#130a6}

“God has commanded it, therefore one must be able to do it.” That means absolutely nothing. There is no ‘_also_’ here. The two expressions could at most mean the _same_ thing.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130a.7) <span class="fac">[130a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp)</span> {#130a7}

“He commanded it,” here means approximately: he will punish whoever does not do it. And nothing follows from that about ability. And _that_ is the sense of ‘election by grace.’

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130a.8+130b.1) <span class="fac">[130a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130a.webp),[130b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130b.webp)</span> {#130a8130b1}

That does not mean, however, that it is correct to say: “He punishes, although one cannot do otherwise.” – One could, however, say: Here, punishment is administered where humans are not supposed to punish. And the concept of ‘punishment’ changes here. For the old illustrations can no longer be applied here, or must now be applied in a completely different way. Just look at an allegory like “The Pilgrim's Progress” & how everything – in a human sense – is wrong here. – But doesn’t it still make sense? i.e., can it not be applied? It has, after all, been applied. (At train stations, there are dials with two pointers, indicating when the next train departs. They look like clocks & are not; but they have a use.) (There would be a better simile here.)

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-130b.2) <span class="fac">[130b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130b.webp)</span> {#130b2}

To the person who finds this allegory unsettling, one could say: use it differently or simply ignore it! (But for _some_, it will likely be far more confusing than helpful.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-130b.3) <span class="fac">[130b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130b.webp)</span> {#130b3}

23.12.1948

Does the dog, when it suddenly becomes aware of a rabbit, _think_ about it?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-130b.4) <span class="fac">[130b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130b.webp)</span> {#130b4}

If a person is now walking and an animal suddenly runs across his path: I see him looking at it in surprise – what do I know about his experience? If asked, he could say, “I was suddenly startled by something; I don’t know what.” Or also: “I suddenly saw something rush past – that was all.” Or: “It was a hare!”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-130b.5) <span class="fac">[130b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130b.webp)</span> {#130b5}

Imagine that he has never seen an animal before: Would his visual experience then be different from that of one to whom the animal form of the thing rushing past is familiar? (I would like to answer the question in the affirmative, but I do not know why.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-130b.6+131a.1) <span class="fac">[130b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/130b.webp),[131a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131a.webp)</span> {#130b6131a1}

One can also ask the question differently: Someone suddenly sees an object in front of him that he does not know; (it may be an object that is familiar to him, but in an unusual position or lighting); the not-recognizing may last only a few seconds. Is it correct: he has a different visual experience than the one to whom the object is immediately known?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131a.2) <span class="fac">[131a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131a.webp)</span> {#131a2}

Can we not imagine that someone could describe the completely unfamiliar form that appears before him just as _accurately_ as I, to whom it is familiar? And isn't that the answer? Of course, in general, it will not be so. Also, his description will look quite different. I will, for example, say “The animal had long ears” – he: “There were two long, leaf-shaped appendages.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131a.3) <span class="fac">[131a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131a.webp)</span> {#131a3}

One must be careful not to think here in the conventional psychological categories. For example, to simply break down the experience into seeing and thinking, or something similar.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131a.4) <span class="fac">[131a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131a.webp)</span> {#131a4}

One might ask: “Is recognizing a part of seeing?” And the question is wrongly posed. What are the signs of recognition? What are the signs of seeing? If one suddenly sees a friend in the crowd and calls out his name, what does this signify?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131a.5) <span class="fac">[131a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131a.webp)</span> {#131a5}

Or, I see someone I haven’t seen for years, I see him clearly, but I don’t recognize him. Suddenly, I recognize him, I see his old self in his changed face. I think I could now depict him differently.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131b.1) <span class="fac">[131b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131b.webp)</span> {#131b1}

It is clear that there is a kinship of concepts here.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131b.2) <span class="fac">[131b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131b.webp)</span> {#131b2}

Isn’t it possible that someone can describe a completely unfamiliar shape more accurately than I can describe a shape that is very familiar to me?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131b.3) <span class="fac">[131b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131b.webp)</span> {#131b3}

(And here one must distinguish between the experience of recognizing something again and the recognition which is simply a feeling of familiarity.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131b.4) <span class="fac">[131b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131b.webp)</span> {#131b4}

Do not try to analyze the experience in yourself!

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131b.5) <span class="fac">[131b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131b.webp)</span> {#131b5}

December 24, 1948

I look at an animal in a cage. Someone asks me: “What do you see?” I answer: “A rabbit.” – I look at the landscape; suddenly, a rabbit runs past. I exclaim: “A rabbit!” Both, the statement and the exclamation, can be called the expression of perception and of the visual experience. But the exclamation is in a different sense than the statement; it bursts forth from us. It is related to the experience in a way similar to how a scream is related to pain.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-131b.6) <span class="fac">[131b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/131b.webp)</span> {#131b6}

But isn’t it simply this: the exclamation, i.e., the particular tone of the words, is simply an expression of surprise. The words themselves, just like those of the statement, are the expression of visual perception, etc. The surprise could also have been expressed in an unarticulated sound; if asked “Why were you startled?” I could say: “A rabbit ran across the path.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.1) <span class="fac">[132a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a1}

Another exclamation could have been: “What was that?!”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.1.2) <span class="fac">[132a\[1\].2](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a12}

But is it also the same experience, whose expression was the inarticulate sound, whose expression was the exclamation “A rabbit!”? How am I supposed to judge it? (I didn’t mean the same.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.2) <span class="fac">[132a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a2}

But since it is a description of a perception, one can also call it an expression of a thought. And one can therefore say: whoever looks at the object and sees it does not necessarily have to think about it; but whoever has the visual experience, whose expression is that exclamation, also thinks about what he sees.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.3) <span class="fac">[132a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a3}

And therefore, the experience of the change of aspect seems to be half visual, half a thought experience.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.4) <span class="fac">[132a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a4}

When seeing the change of aspect, I must engage with the object.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.5) <span class="fac">[132a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a5}

I engage with what I now notice, with what strikes me. In this respect, experiencing the change of aspect is also like an action.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.6) <span class="fac">[132a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a6}

Does the exclamation “What was that?” express a particular visual experience?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.7) <span class="fac">[132a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a7}

Could one not say: Yes and no?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.8) <span class="fac">[132a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a8}

“I just saw a shadow flitting past.” Isn’t this the expression of the visual experience?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.9) <span class="fac">[132a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a9}

I see a ‘questionable’ shape.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132a.10) <span class="fac">[132a\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132a.webp)</span> {#132a10}

But can one really say that the questionableness _&_ the shape is seen?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.1) <span class="fac">[132b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b1}

Question: _What speaks in favor of it_? Well, that the description that one gives of the phenomenon is also modeled by its problematic nature.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.2) <span class="fac">[132b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b2}

What is the criterion of the experience of seeing? What should the criterion be? The representation of ‘what is seen’.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.3) <span class="fac">[132b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b3}

Can I now, when the aspect changes, separate a visual experience from a thought experience? – If you separate them, then the change of aspect seems to be lost.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.4) <span class="fac">[132b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b4}

I believe one could also put it this way: The essential thing about the change of aspect is a sense of _wonder_. And wonder is thinking.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.5) <span class="fac">[132b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b5}

But isn’t that just _my_ understanding of the change of aspect?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.6) <span class="fac">[132b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b6}

_What_ is illuminated? The aspect of it, for example. And the thought lay in the fact that one could only express it in this way.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.7) <span class="fac">[132b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp)</span> {#132b7}

Something passing by could surprise me, so to speak, _physically_ surprise me, & yet I don’t _think_ about it. That is, I could, for example, continue in a train of thought, even though I flinched.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-132b.8+133a.1) <span class="fac">[132b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/132b.webp),[133a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp)</span> {#132b8133a1}

Now, however, consider the aspects of the rotating drum. When they change, it is as if the _motion_ has changed. One does not necessarily know here whether the mode of motion or the aspect has changed. Here, therefore, one does not have the experience of the change of aspect in the same sense.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133a.2) <span class="fac">[133a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp)</span> {#133a2}

Imagine two lights, blue & red, alternating in front of my eyes. It is my task to press a button when the blue light appears, and another button when the other light appears. Surely, one could do this completely mechanically. – And now imagine this game being played with the two aspects of the black-and-white cross. Is it impossible that there could be an equally mechanical, thoughtless reaction?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133a.3) <span class="fac">[133a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp)</span> {#133a3}

If now I recognize this person in the crowd, after perhaps having looked in their direction for some time, – is it seeing? Is it thinking? The expression of the experience is “Look, there is the ‥‥!” – but it could, of course, also be a sketch. The fact that I recognize this person might also be expressed in the sketch & in the sketching. (But the sudden recognition is not expressed in it.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133a.4+133b.1) <span class="fac">[133a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133a.webp),[133b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133a4133b1}

Suppose a child suddenly recognizes a person. Let it be the first time that it suddenly recognizes someone. – It is as if their eyes have suddenly opened. One could, for example, ask: If it suddenly recognizes N.N., – could it have the same visual experience, but without recognizing him? It could, for example, _mistake_ him.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.2) <span class="fac">[133b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b2}

Imagine someone asked: “Am I doing this with my eyes?”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.3) <span class="fac">[133b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b3}

The same expression, which previously reported what had happened, is now an exclamation of recognition.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.4) <span class="fac">[133b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b4}

A hare runs across the path. One person does not know him & says: “Something strange has just rushed past” & now describes the phenomenon. Another person exclaims “A hare!” & cannot describe it so precisely. And why do I still want to say that the one who recognizes him sees him differently than the one who does not recognize him?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.5) <span class="fac">[133b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b5}

If one sees a smile that one does not recognize as a smile, does not understand it, is it seen differently than when it is understood? For example, one might imitate it differently.

_(Understanding of the modes of church music.)_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.6) <span class="fac">[133b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b6}

_What is to be said_ that he sees it differently?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.7) <span class="fac">[133b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b7}

“When one knows what it is, it looks different.” – Why?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.8) <span class="fac">[133b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b8}

What if someone does not know the thing rushing past, but immediately understands it? Does he then see it like the one who knows it?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.9) <span class="fac">[133b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b9}

It is a question of defining concepts.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.10) <span class="fac">[133b\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp)</span> {#133b10}

I mention these types of aspects in order to show what kind of variety we are dealing with here.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-133b.11+134a.1) <span class="fac">[133b\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/133b.webp),[134a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#133b11134a1}

There is a large number of interrelated phenomena & possible concepts here.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.2) <span class="fac">[134a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a2}

Sometimes the conceptual is predominant in the aspect. That is: Sometimes the expression of the aspect experience is only possible through a conceptual explanation. And this | can again be of a very different kind.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.3) <span class="fac">[134a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a3}

25.12.1948

The different types of aspects.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.4) <span class="fac">[134a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a4}

Hearing a melody & the movements with which one perceives it in a certain way, or hears it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.5) <span class="fac">[134a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a5}

Why does it seem so difficult to separate _doing_ & experiencing here?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.6) <span class="fac">[134a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a6}

It is as if doing & impression did not proceed side by side, but the doing shaped the impression.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.7) <span class="fac">[134a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a7}

I hear it differently, I can now play it differently. That is, reproduce it differently.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.8) <span class="fac">[134a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp)</span> {#134a8}

There are many types of aspectual experience. They all have in common the expression: “I see it now as _that_”; or “I see it now _like this_”; or “Now it is _that_, – now _this_”: or “I hear it now as _that_; “I hear it now as …”. The _explanation_, however, of this “that” & “_like this_” is, in different cases, quite different.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134a.9+134b.1) <span class="fac">[134a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134a.webp),[134b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134a9134b1}

But what if I suddenly become aware of a lion outdoors? I assume I only see a part of its head, but I immediately recognize it & shout “A lion!”. The strongest thing in me is the fear. – And now I ask again: What was the visual impression like? Was _it_ of a different kind than the one I receive in the zoo? (Apart from the fact that this one is much more complete. –)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.2) <span class="fac">[134b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b2}

(I cannot yet rise above the mass of phenomena.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.3) <span class="fac">[134b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b3}

It is _difficult_ to see here that it is about definitions. The _concept_ forces itself upon you. (You must not forget that.)

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-134b.4) <span class="fac">[134b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b4}

Whatever the reader can do, leave it to the reader.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.5) <span class="fac">[134b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b5}

26.12.1948

The visual impression seems to organize itself into this form.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.6) <span class="fac">[134b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b6}

That is, it actually means: it changed, & it did not change.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-134b.7) <span class="fac">[134b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b7}

(I almost always write soliloquies, things that I say to myself in private.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.8) <span class="fac">[134b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b8}

When I suddenly recognized it, my visual impression seemed to suddenly become _that_.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.9) <span class="fac">[134b\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b9}

Was it understanding? Was it seeing?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.10) <span class="fac">[134b\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp)</span> {#134b10}

What, if anything, justifies me in speaking of seeing here?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-134b.11+135a.1) <span class="fac">[134b\[11\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/134b.webp),[135a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#134b11135a1}

Imagine, someone told me: “It was as if my visual impression suddenly organized itself into this face & its surroundings.” I would understand him. I would grasp why he expresses himself this way. That is, I would also be inclined to use the image.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135a.2) <span class="fac">[135a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#135a2}

This _figure_

![](../graphics/137,135a-1.svg)

is the reversal of

![](../graphics/137,135a-2.svg)

& this:

![](../graphics/137,135a-3.svg)

[must be written in mirror writing] the reversal of

this:

![](../graphics/137,135a-4.svg)<span class="post-block-punct">.</span>

One is inclined to say that one sees the inverted word differently than the upright one. This is easy to copy, that one is difficult.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135a.3) <span class="fac">[135a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#135a3}

The figure a) 🖵 is the reversal of the figure b) 🖵, as the figure c 🖵 is of d)🖵. But there is still another difference between my impression of c & d – I would like to say – than between a & b, (d, for example, looks neat, c messy. Cf. Lewis Carroll ‘Looking glass’) d is easy to copy, c difficult.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-135a.4) <span class="fac">[135a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#135a4}

27.12.1948

Ambition is the death of thinking.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135a.5) <span class="fac">[135a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#135a5}

What formerly fell apart in the visual impression now belongs together.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-135a.6) <span class="fac">[135a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp)</span> {#135a6}

28.12.1948

Humor is not a mood; but a world-view. And therefore, if it is correct to say that humor was destroyed in Nazi Germany, that does not mean something like, one was not in a good mood, but something much deeper & more important.

### <span class="fac">[135a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135a.webp),[135b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span>

It would be interesting to study my writings during both good & bad times. In good times, it naturally has momentum & the mere act of writing gives me pleasure. In bad, i.e., unproductive, times, it is listless & petty. (As is the case now.) And this does not only come from the fact that I prefer to write down good _Gedanke_ rather than miserable ones, but I believe it is also related to the general functioning of the nervous system.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135b.2) <span class="fac">[135b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#135b2}

29.12.1948

How would this explanation be: “I can see something as _that_, of which it can be a _picture_”? – But is this an explanation, or a pleonasm? –

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135b.3) <span class="fac">[135b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#135b3}

It says that the aspects in the change of aspect are those that the figure might, under certain circumstances, have in a picture in a _static_ way.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135b.4) <span class="fac">[135b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#135b4}

A triangle can indeed be in a picture, hanging in another, representing something overturned in a third. – So that I, the observer, do not say “This can also represent something overturned”, but “This jug has fallen over & lies in shards” … That is how we react to the picture.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135b.5) <span class="fac">[135b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#135b5}

Could I say what a picture must be like so that one reacts to it in that way? No. There are, for example, styles of painting that do not communicate anything to me in this direct way, but do to someone else. I believe that habit & education have something to do with it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135b.6) <span class="fac">[135b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp)</span> {#135b6}

30.12.1948

Consider now, as an example, the aspects of the triangle. The triangle

![](../graphics/137,135b.svg)

can be seen as a triangular hole, as a body, as a geometric drawing; standing on its base, hanging from its point; as a mountain, as a wedge, as an arrow or pointer; as an overturned body that should (e.g.) stand on the shorter leg, as half a parallelogram, and other things.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-135b.7+136a.1) <span class="fac">[135b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/135b.webp),[136a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#135b7136a1}

What does it mean that I see the sphere floating in the picture? Does it already lie in the fact that I describe the picture in this way? That this description is the most obvious, most natural one for me? No; it could be for various reasons. It could, for example, simply be the conventional one.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136a.2) <span class="fac">[136a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#136a2}

What, then, is the _expression_ for the fact that I understand the _image_ not merely in the sense of ‘e.g.’ like this (know what it is supposed to represent), but see it like this? Such an expression is: “The sphere appears to float,” “One sees it floating,” or also in a particular tone of voice, “It is floating!” This, then, is the expression of the attitude. But not used as such.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136a.3) <span class="fac">[136a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#136a3}

We are not asking here what the causes are & what produces this impression in a particular case.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136a.4) <span class="fac">[136a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#136a4}

And _is_ it a different impression? – “I do see something _different_ when I see the sphere floating, compared to when I simply see it lying there.” – This actually means: This expression is justified! (Because, taken literally, it is merely a repetition.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136a.5) <span class="fac">[136a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp)</span> {#136a5}

(And yet, my impression is also not that of a real, floating sphere. Compare different kinds of ‘spatial’ seeing; the spatial quality of ordinary photography & that of what one sees through a stereoscope.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136a.6+136b.1) <span class="fac">[136a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136a.webp),[136b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#136a6136b1}

“And is it really a different impression?” In order to answer this, I would like to ask myself whether something different actually exists within me. But how can I convince myself of this – I _describe_ what I see differently.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136b.2) <span class="fac">[136b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#136b2}

We can bring about the change in aspect, and it can also occur against our will. It can follow our will as our gaze does.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-136b.3) <span class="fac">[136b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#136b3}

31.12.1948

Two people together, laughing about a joke, for example. One of them said some rather unusual words, & now they both break out in a kind of grumbling. This could seem _very_ strange to someone coming from a different background. Whereas we find it quite _reasonable_. (I observed this scene the other day on a bus & was able to imagine what it would be like for someone who isn't used to it. It seemed completely irrational to me & like the _reactions_ of an animal foreign to us.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136b.4) <span class="fac">[136b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp)</span> {#136b4}

When one is riding in a bus at night & it turns a corner, & one looks at the front wall of the bus (which does not move relative to the passenger), one believes that one _sees_ it making the turn. One naturally feels that the vehicle is making the movement, & perhaps one also sees some indication of it in the outer darkness, which one still sees out of the corner of one’s eye, even if unconsciously. But one believes that one sees the front wall describing the curve & _at the same time_ naturally that it is not changing relative to oneself.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-136b.5+137a.1) <span class="fac">[136b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/136b.webp),[137a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#136b5137a1}

01.01.1949

(Rhees) When someone describes his current mood, for example, saying that it is like a gray cloud, – is he not observing it, even if it is perhaps modified by this observation? & does what I said about ‘descriptions’ in general apply to this description?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137a.2) <span class="fac">[137a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#137a2}

Don’t I look within myself & say: “What is the right word for this feeling, this mood? – And is it clear that it is not, for example, intensified by my looking? Can’t I _indulge_ in a mood? And can’t self-observation be part of indulging?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137a.3) <span class="fac">[137a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#137a3}

Is this similar to the fact that I evoke a bodily pain (in whatever way) in myself and then try to describe its character accurately.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-137a.4) <span class="fac">[137a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#137a4}

The concept of ‘celebration’. For us, it is associated with pleasure; at another time, it may be associated only with fear and horror. What we call “wit” and what we call “humor” certainly did not exist in other times. And these two are constantly changing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137a.5) <span class="fac">[137a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp)</span> {#137a5}

Imagine I say in such a case: “Yes, this pain is like a blazing flame.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137a.6+137b.1) <span class="fac">[137a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137a.webp),[137b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#137a6137b1}

In what way & in what sense do I observe the pain? (For it seems to me that there is no difference whether one observes one’s sadness or one’s own pain.) I put myself in the position of feeling it. But _which_ one? The one _of that_ kind, or the one that is brought about _in this_ way? Do I say, “I would like to create this same burning pain again, in order to see _how_ it is”? What is the point of observing it if I can identify it in this way? Well, one could say: “If I only feel this same pain again & again, I will finally find the right word or even the right colored image (perhaps that of a flame) for it.” & now I can simplify the case. He does not need to intentionally bring about the pain; rather, let there be a persistent pain (in the head, or stomach) & he thinks about the right description of his feeling.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137b.2) <span class="fac">[137b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#137b2}

What I want to say is that when I look, I don’t observe the _visual impression_, but rather what is being looked at.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137b.3) <span class="fac">[137b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#137b3}

So, if I look at my sorrow in some sense, then I am not observing the impression that I receive.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137b.4) <span class="fac">[137b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp)</span> {#137b4}

But imagine, I stare at an object & ask myself “What kind of red am I seeing there?” I am not interested in the color of the object; rather, I am simply looking for a name for my current impression of it. Can I say: To think about an impression is not the same as ‘to observe it’?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-137b.5+138a.1) <span class="fac">[137b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/137b.webp),[138a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#137b5138a1}

01/02/1949

What does the person communicating to us when they say “Now I see it as …?” That is: what are the consequences of this communication, what kind of use can it have? It could have various consequences. For example, someone who sees the duck-rabbit as a duck will not be able to describe the expression of the rabbit's face. Spatial image in descriptive geometry. Whoever sees the cube schema _exactly_ will not be able to perform various drawing operations with it. [Not quite correct.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.2) <span class="fac">[138a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a2}

Related to the game “This could be …”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.3) <span class="fac">[138a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a3}

What do you communicate to me with the words …? What can I do with this utterance? What are its consequences?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.4) <span class="fac">[138a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a4}

Some drawings are always seen, flat as a figure in the plane, others sometimes, or always spatially.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.5) <span class="fac">[138a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a5}

One might now say: The visual impression of the spatially seen drawings is spatial; for the cube scheme, for example, it is a cube. (Because the description of the impression is the description of a cube.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.6) <span class="fac">[138a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a6}

“Now I always see it as ….” I previously saw this incorrectly in the picture as …; but not anymore. I now always see it as it is intended. – How does this manifest itself?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.7) <span class="fac">[138a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a7}

And it is then remarkable that our impression is something flat for some drawings, and something spatial for others. One wonders: “Where can this end?” [The image of a runner.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.8) <span class="fac">[138a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a8}

01/03/1949

“What does this color remind me of?” – Whoever looks at an object & asks himself this, – is he observing the facial **impression**? []

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.9) <span class="fac">[138a\[9\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp)</span> {#138a9}

What does someone communicate to me when they say “Now I see it as …”? What are the consequences of this communication? What can I do with it?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138a.10+138b.1) <span class="fac">[138a\[10\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138a.webp),[138b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138b.webp)</span> {#138a10138b1}

People often associate colors with vowels. It could be that for some, a vowel, when pronounced repeatedly, changes its color. The vowel _a_ would be ‘now blue – now red’. “Now I see it as …” might no longer mean anything more than “a is red” (combined with physiological observations, this change could also become important to us).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138b.2) <span class="fac">[138b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138b.webp)</span> {#138b2}

When I ask about the use, about the interest, of that communication, it occurs to me how often in aesthetic considerations it is said “You must see it _this_ way, that is how it is intended,” “If you see it _this_ way, you will see where the mistake lies,” “You must hear these measures as an introduction,” “You must listen for this key,” “You must phrase the theme _this_ way” (and this can refer to both listening and playing).

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-138b.3) <span class="fac">[138b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/138b.webp)</span> {#138b3}

The figure

![](../graphics/137,138b-1.svg)

is intended to represent a convex step, and is used to demonstrate certain spatial processes. I draw the line _a_ through the midpoints

![](../graphics/137,138b-2.svg)

of the two surfaces. – If someone only sees the figure spatially for a moment, and perhaps even then sometimes as a concave step, then it might be difficult for them to follow the demonstration. (As it is for someone in descriptive geometry who is unable to see the projections spatially.) (The role of perception in mathematics.) And if the flat aspect alternates with a spatial one for them, then it is no different than if I were showing them alternately something flat and one of two models during the demonstration.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.1) <span class="fac">[139a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a1}

But the uses in aesthetics and in descriptive geometry are fundamentally different. Is it not essential in aesthetics that the picture, the piece of music, etc., can change its aspect for me? – because for that depiction of spatial processes, it is of course not.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.2) <span class="fac">[139a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a2}

“If I see it _this_ way, it fits, but if I see it _this_ way, then it doesn’t.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.3) <span class="fac">[139a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a3}

Game: “It could also be …”.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.4) <span class="fac">[139a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a4}

“That is not _seeing_!” – “That is _seeing_!” – Both must be justified conceptually.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.5) <span class="fac">[139a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a5}

The question is: _In what way_ is it seeing?

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.6) <span class="fac">[139a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a6}

“Do you always _see_ this leaf as _green_, as long as you perceive it spatially, & when asked what color it is, would you answer “green” without lying?” Does this question have a clear sense? One possible answer might be: “Well, when I look at the leaf, I don’t keep saying to myself ‘Oh, how _green!_’”.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.7) <span class="fac">[139a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp)</span> {#139a7}

04.01.1949

What is the expression of the fact that I see this picture as a picture of bare trees? That I don’t just _know_ that it depicts such, that I don’t _read_ it like a blueprint? – I treat it differently. (Child & doll)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139a.8+139b.1) <span class="fac">[139a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139a.webp),[139b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139a8139b1}

If I see an animal in a picture pierced by an arrow, do I only _know_ that the arrowhead is connected to the feathers, or do I _see_ it? – I _behave_ towards these parts as I would towards an arrow, i.e., I don’t just say, as if it were a drawing of a machine that I am deciphering, “These two parts belong together; a rod goes through here”; but if asked, “What did you see in the picture?” I immediately answer: “An animal pierced by an arrow.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.2) <span class="fac">[139b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b2}

“The phenomenon initially amazes one, but a physiological explanation for it will certainly be found.” – Our problem is not causal but conceptual.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.3) <span class="fac">[139b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b3}

The question is: _In what way_ is it seeing?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.4) <span class="fac">[139b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b4}

I often see a contour continue when it is interrupted in the drawing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.5) <span class="fac">[139b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b5}

I see that in the picture, the arrow penetrates the animal. It has hit it in the neck and still protrudes at the nape of the neck. Imagine the picture as a silhouette. – Do you _see_ the arrow, or do you only _know_ that these two parts depict (are supposed to depict) a part of an arrow?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.6) <span class="fac">[139b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b6}

Compare Köhler’s figure of the mutually penetrating hexagons.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.7) <span class="fac">[139b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b7}

That is seeing! _In what way_ is it seeing?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-139b.8) <span class="fac">[139b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/139b.webp)</span> {#139b8}

The description I would give of the figure if I approached it naively is undoubtedly _that_. If the picture were shown to me for a short time & I were to describe it, _that_ would be the description; if I were to draw it afterward, I would certainly draw two identical, mutually intersecting hexagons, & in _this_ respect I would not err in the copy, even if otherwise much of it might be incorrect.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.1) <span class="fac">[140a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a1}

Is it knowledge or seeing? – What if it were _merely_ knowledge? In what cases would I say that it is merely knowledge? When I read a blueprint, for example.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.2) <span class="fac">[140a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a2}

What does it mean when, looking at a drawing in descriptive geometry, I say: “I know that it continues here, but I can’t _see_ it”? Does it simply mean that I lack the familiarity of ‘knowing’? Well, this familiarity is certainly one of our criteria. The criterion is a certain _kind_ of knowing. (Certain gestures, for example, that indicate the spatial relationships. Fine nuances of behaviour.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.3) <span class="fac">[140a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a3}

You must think about the role that pictures (as opposed to technical drawings) play in our lives. And this role is by no means uniform.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.4) <span class="fac">[140a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a4}

I would expect something different from someone who sees the drawing as… than from someone who only knows what it is supposed to depict.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.5) <span class="fac">[140a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a5}

[Remark about the _third_ person.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.6) <span class="fac">[140a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a6}

One sometimes hangs sayings on the wall. But not theorems of geometry. Our relationship to these two.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140a.7) <span class="fac">[140a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp)</span> {#140a7}

“If I see it _this_ way, then it fits with the rest.” This is a very specific language-game with the expression “to see something this way.” And the criterion of ‘seeing it _this_ way’ is different here than in the case of descriptive geometry.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-140a.8+140b.1) <span class="fac">[140a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140a.webp),[140b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#140a8140b1}

“Le stile c'est l'homme” “Le stile c'est l'homme même.” The first expression has a cheap, epigrammatic brevity. The second, correct one, opens up a completely different perspective. It says that style is the _image_ of the person.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-140b.2) <span class="fac">[140b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#140b2}

There are remarks that sow, and remarks that reap.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140b.3) <span class="fac">[140b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#140b3}

What is the criterion for him seeing it _this_ way, if, for example, he says, “If I see it _this_ way, then it fits with _this_”? – That he can, for example, make or suggest certain changes to the picture, building, etc., that have a certain effect on the viewer.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140b.4) <span class="fac">[140b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#140b4}

05.01.1949

_---_

How, if one were to say: The recounting of a dream is an odd form of memory disturbance; it combines a lot of memories from the previous day, from earlier days, yes, even from childhood, & makes of them a memory of an event during the time of sleep. We all know of cases in which we mix the memories of several days into _one_.

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140b.5) <span class="fac">[140b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#140b5}

When would I call it merely _knowing_? – If one treated the picture as if it were a technical drawing. One would **read** out what it depicts from it. (Subtle nuances of behaviour.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-140b.6) <span class="fac">[140b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/140b.webp)</span> {#140b6}

06.01.1949

I immediately recognize the 6 corners as such. Now I look at them & ask myself: “Am I really seeing them _as_ hexagons?” – and that: the whole time, during which I see them? – And I would like to answer: I don't think I think of them as hexagons the whole time.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141a.1) <span class="fac">[141a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a1}

The first thing that strikes the eye in this picture is: they are hexagons.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141a.2) <span class="fac">[141a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a2}

Someone tells me: “I immediately saw it as two hexagons. Yes, that was _all_ that I saw in it.” But how do I understand that? I think he would have answered the question “What do you see?” immediately with “Two hexagons.” He would also not have treated this answer as one of many possible answers. It is in this respect the same as the answer “An animal” – if I had shown him the picture of such an animal; or “A face” – if I had shown him the figure

![](../graphics/137,141a.svg)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141a.3) <span class="fac">[141a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a3}

I _recognize_ it immediately as a face, I am ready to treat it as such.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-141a.4) <span class="fac">[141a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a4}

Putting together the landscape of these conceptual relationships from its individual pieces is _too difficult_ for me. I can only do it very imperfectly.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141a.5) <span class="fac">[141a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a5}

It could also have been that I saw the picture as something else first, & then said to myself “Ah, it’s 2 hexagons!” But this did not happen. So the aspect would have changed. And does this now prove that I actually saw it in a particular aspect? (Well, as you like!)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141a.6) <span class="fac">[141a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a6}

“Is it a _real_ experience of seeing?” The question is: In what respect is it one?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141a.7) <span class="fac">[141a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141a.webp)</span> {#141a7}

[It is difficult to see ‥‥․] [The eye “See how it looks!”]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.1) <span class="fac">[141b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b1}

“For me it is an animal, pierced by an arrow.” I treat it as that, this is my _attitude_ towards the figure. This is one of its meanings: to call it seeing.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.2) <span class="fac">[141b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b2}

But can I also say in the same sense: “These are two hexagons for me”? Not in the same sense, but in a similar one. –

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.3) <span class="fac">[141b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b3}

So I see it, in this sense, therefore only as long _as_ I have this _attitude_ towards it? One could say that.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.4) <span class="fac">[141b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b4}

“This feature of the picture struck me.”

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.5) <span class="fac">[141b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b5}

The best description I can give of what was shown to me in a fleeting moment is _this_: ‥‥‥ “The impression was that of a standing animal.” So, a quite specific description emerged. – Was that _seeing_, or was it a thought? How am I supposed to decide?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.6) <span class="fac">[141b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b6}

But do I only see the picture in this aspect as long as I have this attitude towards it? – One can say that.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.7) <span class="fac">[141b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp)</span> {#141b7}

But couldn't one also say: “I always see it as that, if I never see it as something else”?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-141b.8+142a.1) <span class="fac">[141b\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/141b.webp),[142a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#141b8142a1}

“He sees it spatially & is therefore so familiar with the drawing, as if he were operating in the spatial model.” But isn't his particular manipulation in the drawing precisely the criterion for the fact that he sees it spatially? (Because what else do I know about his impression?)

_---_

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.2) <span class="fac">[142a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#142a2}

I don't just see it as an animal while I say this. A body also does not have its weight only during the time it is being weighed. (Definition of concepts.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.3) <span class="fac">[142a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#142a3}

It is a lion for me. How long is it a lion for me?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.4) <span class="fac">[142a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#142a4}

But wait! Do I ever actually say of the ordinary picture (of a lion) that I see it as a lion? I have never heard that. [→ ]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.5) <span class="fac">[142a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#142a5}

And yet I have talked about such seeing here!

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.6) <span class="fac">[142a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#142a6}

07.01.1949

I could say of a picture by Picasso that I do _not_ see it as people. Or of some: I could not have seen it as what it represents for a long time, but I do now. This is similar to: I was not able to hear this as a unit for a long time, but now I hear it as such. Previously it seemed to me like a lot of short pieces that kept breaking off – now I hear it as an organism. (Bruckner.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.7) <span class="fac">[142a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp)</span> {#142a7}

Would you understand if I said, “We _regard_ the photograph, the picture on our wall, as the people & other things, as the things that are depicted on them”?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142a.8+142b.1) <span class="fac">[142a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142a.webp),[142b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#142a8142b1}

This doesn't have to be the case. We could easily imagine people who do not have this relationship to the pictures. (People, for example, who would be repelled by our photographs because a face without color is eerie & ugly.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142b.2) <span class="fac">[142b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#142b2}

We do not say of a conventional picture of a person, “I see that as a person.” “I see it as a…” goes together with… [→] (“Goes together” in the language-game.)

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142b.3) <span class="fac">[142b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#142b3}

If I now say “We regard a portrait as a human being,” – when & for how long do we do this? Always, whenever we see it at all (& not see it as something _other_)? I could affirm this, & in doing so, I would define the concept of regarding. – The question is whether a _different_ concept becomes important to me besides this one: a concept of seeing it in this way, which only takes place while I am occupied with the picture as ‥‥ _occupied_.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142b.4) <span class="fac">[142b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#142b4}

The concept of noticing. I can say that I sometimes notice the similarity of this picture with… and so on; but I do not say that I sometimes notice that this photograph is a face. I could say: A picture does not always _live_ for me while I see it.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142b.5) <span class="fac">[142b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#142b5}

But the question now is: Is this “living” a “seeing,” or: on what grounds could I call it a “seeing”? What does this concept have in common with a concept of seeing?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142b.6) <span class="fac">[142b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp)</span> {#142b6}

Now, we do not say that we ‘see’ the conventional picture of a lion, for example, _as_ a lion:

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-142b.7+143a.1+143a.2+143a.3) <span class="fac">[142b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/142b.webp),[143a\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp),[143a\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp),[143a\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span> {#142b7143a1143a2143a3}

“Your picture smiles at me from the wall.” It does not have to do this every time I see it. And this expression is, after all, a justification for the expression that I see it differently then.

I am not striving for any kind of completeness with all these examples. No inventory of all psychological concepts. I only want to enable my reader to help himself in conceptual uncertainties.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-143a.4) <span class="fac">[143a\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span> {#143a4}

If I prepare for a contingency, you can be fairly sure that it will not happen. Possibly.

### [VB](/w-vb/#ms-137-143a.5) <span class="fac">[143a\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span> {#143a5}

08.01.1949

It is _difficult_ to know something, & to act as if one knows it.

### <span class="fac">[143a\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span>

[Do not flaunt your wealth.]

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143a.7) <span class="fac">[143a\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp)</span> {#143a7}

The child says, “Now it is a house.” – this can also be said in the game in which the box is a house, in various ways & in various situations. Someone comes into the room while the game is in progress; they are told, “Now it is a house.” This does not mean: “_Now_ it has become a house for me,” not the lighting up of the aspect. For it to be so, the tone & situation must be of a particular kind, & it is again about subtle differences in behaviour.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143a.8+143b.1) <span class="fac">[143a\[8\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143a.webp),[143b\[1\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143a8143b1}

09.01.1949

‘Subtle nuances of behaviour’ – If my understanding of a subject is expressed in that I whistle it correctly, that is an example of these subtle nuances.

But if “Now it is a house” does not express the _lighting up_ of the aspect, can it report the stable aspect?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143b.2) <span class="fac">[143b\[2\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143b2}

“He completely forgets that it is a box; it is actually a house for him.” (There are certain signs of this.) If you were to say this about someone, would it also be correct to say that he _sees_ it as a house?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143b.3) <span class="fac">[143b\[3\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143b3}

And whoever could play like this & in a particular situation exclaims with special expression, “Now it is a house!,” would express the lighting up of the aspect.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143b.4) <span class="fac">[143b\[4\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143b4}

But the expression of the voice & gesture is the same as if the object had changed & had finally _become_ that & this.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143b.5) <span class="fac">[143b\[5\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143b5}

I would like to say that what lights up here only remains as long as a certain engagement with the object lasts. (“Look how it looks!”) (The noticing of the family resemblance of this face to an absent person.) – “I would like to say” – & _is_ it so? – Ask yourself: “How long do I notice something?” – How long is it _new_ to me?

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143b.6) <span class="fac">[143b\[6\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143b6}

Would it be correct to say that in the change of aspect of the rotating drum, the perception of the _constancy_ of the object is missing? Because one can really doubt here whether the mode of movement has not changed.

### [LW I](/w-lw-1/#ms-137-143b.7) <span class="fac">[143b\[7\]](https://cdn.wittgensteinnachlass.com/2000px/webp/Ms-137/143b.webp)</span> {#143b7}

You must bear in mind that the description of the changing aspects is in every case of a different kind.
